\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left|\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{\Gamma}\Delta\eta_{N}\Delta\psi\right|
by

\leq\mathbb{E}\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\|\eta_{N}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{2}(\Gamma))}\|\psi\|_{H^{2}(\Gamma)}\leq|t_{1}-t_{2}|\cdot\mathbb{E}\|\eta_{N}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{2}(\Gamma))}^{2}\leq C|t_{1}-t_{2}|.

Similarly, by the viscoelasticity estimate
v_{N}\in L^{2}(\Omega;L^{2}(0,T;H^{1}(\Gamma)))
, we obtain that

\mathbb{E}\left|\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{\Gamma}\nabla v_{N}\cdot\nabla\psi\right|\leq C|t_{1}-t_{2}|^{1/2}.

(3)

Finally, we use the BDG inequality and the fact that
\|\psi\|_{C(\Gamma)}\leq C\|\psi\|_{H^{2}(\Gamma)}\leq C\|\psi\|_{X^{st}_{n}}\leq C
for a constant
C
that depends only on
n
(and is independent of
N
) to estimate:

\mathbb{E}\left|\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{\Gamma}G_{n}(\eta_{N},v_{N})\psi dW^{2}\right|^{p}\leq C\mathbb{E}\left|\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\left(\int_{\Gamma}G_{n}(\eta_{N},v_{N})\right)^{2}\right|^{p/2}\leq C\mathbb{E}\left|\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{\Gamma}\Big(G_{n}(\eta_{N},v_{N})\Big)^{2}\right|^{p/2}.

We then use (
8
), (
26
), and the fact that
P_{n}^{st}
is a bounded operator from
L^{2}(\Gamma)
to itself, to estimate that this is:

\displaystyle\leq C\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{\Gamma}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}|g_{k}(\eta_{N},v_{N})|^{2}\right)^{p/2}\leq C\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{\Gamma}(1+|\eta_{N}|^{2}+|v_{N}|^{2})\right)^{p/2}

\displaystyle\leq C|t_{1}-t_{2}|^{p/2}\mathbb{E}\Big(1+\|\eta_{N}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{2}(\Gamma))}^{2}+\|v_{N}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\Gamma))}\Big)^{p/2}\leq C|t_{1}-t_{2}|^{p/2}.

Conclusion of equicontinuity estimate.
Therefore, by applying the Kolmogorov continuity criterion, we obtain the following equicontinuity estimate on the
C^{1/3}
-seminorm of
v_{N}
:

\mathbb{E}[v_{N}]_{C^{1/3}(0,T;(X_{n}^{st})^{*})}\leq C,\qquad\text{ independently of $N$}.

Furthermore, since
v_{N}
are uniformly bounded in
L^{2}(\Omega;L^{\infty}(0,T;X_{n}^{st}))
where
X_{n}^{st}
is the finite dimensional subspace of
L^{2}(\Gamma)
defined in (
23
), we can use the finite-dimensionality of
X_{n}^{st}
as follows. We can identify
(X_{n}^{st})^{*}
with
X_{n}^{st}
and since
X_{n}^{st}
is a finite-dimensional subspace of
L^{2}(\Gamma)
, we can conclude tightness of the laws
\mu_{v}
in
C(0,T;X_{n}^{st})
and hence
C(0,T;L^{2}(\Gamma))
, from the equicontinuity estimate using the Arzela-Ascoli compactness criterion.

•
Tightness for fluid densities/velocities.
The proof of tightness for the components involving the fluid density and fluid velocity closely follow the proofs given in Section 4.3.2 in
[
7
]
and Lemma 4.4 in
[
39
]
on stochastic compressible isentropic Navier-Stokes equations on a fixed domain, so we just outline the main ideas here, without providing explicit details.

The first difficulty is that we have a bound on the kinetic energy
\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}}\rho_{N}(t)|{\boldsymbol{u}}_{N}(t)|^{2}
but we want a bound on
\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}}|{\boldsymbol{u}}_{N}(t)|^{2}
, which requires an
L^{\infty}
estimate on the inverse of the density
\rho_{N}^{-1}
that is uniform in
N
. In particular, we want to establish the following uniform convergence estimate (in probability) for the fluid velocity:

(39)

\lim_{M\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\left(\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}|\boldsymbol{u}_{N}(t)|^{2}\geq M\right)=0,\qquad\text{ uniformly in $N$},

as this bound will be useful for establishing tightness of the fluid densities/velocities. To establish (
39
), it suffices to estimate the following probabilities (uniformly, independently of
N
):

(40)

\mathbb{P}\left(\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}}\rho_{N}(t)|{\boldsymbol{u}}_{N}(t)|^{2}\geq M\right),\qquad\mathbb{P}\left(\|\rho_{N}^{-1}\|_{L^{\infty}([0,T]\times\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})}\geq M\right).

We can estimate the first probability in (
40
) by using the uniform boundedness of the kinetic energy, and then to estimate the second probability, we recall that by construction, for fixed
\varepsilon
and
\delta
, we have that the initial data
\rho_{0,\varepsilon,\delta}
(which is the initial data for all
N
) satisfies

0<\varepsilon\leq\rho_{0,\delta,\varepsilon}\leq\delta^{-1/\beta},

see (
21
). By using the fluid dissipation estimate and Poincaré’s inequality,
\mathbb{E}\left(\|{\boldsymbol{u}}_{N}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;X_{n}^{f})}^{2}\right)<\infty
. So by equivalence of norms in
X_{n}^{f}
and the embedding
H^{l}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha})
for
l>5/2
into
W^{1,\infty}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha})
:

\mathbb{E}\left(\|\text{div}({\boldsymbol{u}}_{N})\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{\infty}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}))}^{2}\right)<\infty,\quad\text{uniformly in $N$.}

Hence, by the comparison principle (see e.g. Lemma 2.2 in
[
20
]
),

(41)

0<{\varepsilon}\exp\left(-\int_{0}^{t}\|\text{div}{\boldsymbol{u}}_{N}(s)\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})}ds\right)\leq\rho_{N}\leq{\delta^{-\frac{1}{\beta}}}\exp\left(\int_{0}^{t}\|\text{div}{\boldsymbol{u}}_{N}(s)\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})}ds\right).

we have a bound on the second probability in (
40
). Thus, we can conclude that the desired uniform convergence (
39
) holds as a result of the estimates of both probabilities in (
40
).
We can use this bound along with the following observations to show tightness of the laws of the fluid densities and the fluid velocities.

Tightness of fluid densities.
Using a regularity result (Theorem A.2.2 in
[
7
]
, Lemma B.7 in
[
39
]
) and performing several standard estimates as in Lemma 4.4 in
[
39
]
, we can obtain the following two estimates: For
\frac{\beta}{\beta+1}=\theta\left(1-\frac{2}{3}\right)+(1-\theta)
and for some
\beta<q<\infty
and
1\leq r<\beta
,

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}\rho_{N}\|

{}_{L^{\beta}((0,T)\times\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})}+\|\rho_{N}\|_{L^{\beta}(0,T;W^{2,\beta}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}))}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\|\rho_{0,\delta,\varepsilon}\|_{W^{2,q}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})}+\|{\boldsymbol{u}}_{N}\|^{2}_{C(0,T;X^{f}_{n})}\|\rho_{N}\|^{\theta}_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{\beta}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}))}\|\rho_{N}^{\beta/2}\|^{\frac{1-\theta}{2\beta}}_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{1}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}))}\right),

\|\partial_{t}\nabla\rho_{N}\|_{L^{r}((0,T)\times\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})}+\|\nabla\rho_{N}\|_{L^{r}(0,T;W^{2,r}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}))}\leq C\|{\boldsymbol{u}}_{N}\|_{C(0,T;X^{f}_{n})}\|\rho_{N}\|_{L^{\beta}(0,T;W^{2,\beta}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}))},

for a constant
C
that is independent of
\omega
,
N
, and the remaining approximation parameters. Hence, by using (
39
) and the bounds on
\rho_{N}
in the uniform energy estimate (
36
), this gives a uniform estimate on
\|\partial_{t}\rho_{N}\|_{L^{\beta}((0,T)\times{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha})}
,
\|\partial_{t}\nabla\rho_{N}\|_{L^{r}((0,T)\times{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha})}
, and
\|\rho_{N}\|_{L^{\beta}(0,T;W^{2,\beta}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))}
in probability independently of
N
, which allows us to deduce tightness in the parameter
N
. We obtain tightness of
\rho_{N}
in
L^{\beta}(0,T;W^{1,\beta}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}))
via Aubin-Lions, using bounds of
\rho_{N}
in
L^{\beta}(0,T;W^{2,\beta}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}))
and
\partial_{t}\rho_{N}
in
L^{\beta}((0,T)\times\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})
, and we obtain tightness of
\rho_{N}
in
C(0,T;L^{\beta}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}))
via Arzela-Ascoli compactness arguments and Sobolev embedding, using bounds of
\rho_{N}
and
\partial_{t}\rho_{N}
, both in
L^{r}(0,T;W^{1,r}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}))
where we use the regularity of the time derivative to get a bound on
\rho_{N}
in
C(0,T;W^{1,r}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}))
for an appropriately chosen
1\leq r<\beta
.

Tightness of fluid velocities.
We can use the weak formulation for the momentum equation and
L^{\infty}
estimates on
\rho_{N}^{-1}
uniformly in
N
, to get an estimate on the increments of the fluid velocity. Such an increment estimate would allow us to conclude that

\lim_{M\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\left([{\boldsymbol{u}}_{N}]_{C^{1/3}(0,T;X_{n}^{f})}\geq M\right)=0,\quad\text{ uniformly in $N$},

where
[\cdot]_{C^{1/3}(0,T;X_{n}^{f})}
denotes the
1/3
-Hölder seminorm for a function taking values in
X^{f}_{n}
. Combining this with the uniform bound (
39
) and the fact that
X^{f}_{n}
is finite-dimensional allows us to conclude the tightness of the laws of the approximate fluid velocities via Arzela-Ascoli.

∎

5.2.
Identification of the limit as
N\to\infty

Next, we use the Skorohod representation theorem in conjunction with the result of
[
25
]
, in order to obtain a limiting random variable as
N\to\infty
keeping all other approximation parameters fixed, on a new probability space
(\tilde{\Omega},\tilde{\mathcal{F}},\tilde{\mathbb{P}})
1
1

1

Note, here and later, that in the proof of this version of the Skorohod representation theorem
\tilde{\Omega}=[0,1)\times[0,1)
,
\tilde{\mathcal{F}}
is the Borel algebra on
\tilde{\Omega}
and
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
is the Lebesgue measure on
\tilde{\Omega}
and thus
(\tilde{\Omega},\tilde{\mathcal{F}},\tilde{\mathbb{P}})
is independent of all the approximate parameters.
.

Since we are only allowing the time discretization parameter
N
to vary while keeping all other parameters
n,\varepsilon,\delta
fixed, we will notate only the dependence of the approximate solutions on
N
, as
\mathcal{U}_{N}:=(\rho_{N},{\boldsymbol{u}}_{N},\eta_{N},\eta^{*}_{N},v_{N},W^{1},W^{2})
, which we consider as random variables taking values in
\mathcal{X}
defined by (
37
).

Theorem 5.1
.

There exists a probability space
(\tilde{\Omega},\tilde{\mathcal{F}},\tilde{\mathbb{P}})
and random variables

\tilde{\mathcal{U}}_{N}=(\tilde{\rho}_{N},\tilde{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{N},\tilde{\eta}_{N},\tilde{\eta}^{*}_{N},\tilde{v}_{N},\tilde{W}_{N}^{1},\tilde{W}_{N}^{2})\text{ for $N=1,2,...$},\quad{\mathcal{U}}=(\rho,{\boldsymbol{u}},{\boldsymbol{u}},\eta,\eta^{*},v,\tilde{W}^{1},\tilde{W}^{2}),

defined on this new probability space, such that

(1)

\tilde{\mathcal{U}}_{N}
has the same law in
\mathcal{X}
as
\mathcal{U}_{N}
,

(2)

\tilde{\mathcal{U}}_{N}\to\mathcal{U}\text{ in the topology of $\mathcal{X}$, $\tilde{\mathbb{P}}$-almost surely as $N\to\infty$},

(3)

\tilde{\eta}^{*}_{N}=\tilde{\eta}_{N}
for every
t<\tau^{\eta}_{N}
where, for the fixed
s\in(\frac{3}{2},2)
,

\displaystyle\tau^{\eta}_{N}

\displaystyle:=T\wedge\inf\left\{t>0:\inf_{\Gamma}(1+{\tilde{\eta}_{N}}(t))\leq\alpha\text{ or }\|\tilde{\eta}_{N}(t)\|_{H^{s}(\Gamma)}\geq\frac{1}{\alpha}\right\}.

(4)

\partial_{t}\eta=v
,
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost surely.

Proof.

The first two statements essentially follow from the Skorohod representation theorem. Let
\tilde{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{\prime}
be the
\sigma
-field generated by the random variables
{{\boldsymbol{u}}}(s),v(s),\eta(s),\tilde{W}^{1}(s),\tilde{W}^{2}(s)
, for all
s\leq t
. Then we define

(42)

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}

\displaystyle:=\{\mathcal{A}\in\tilde{\mathcal{F}}\ |\ \tilde{\mathbb{P}}(\mathcal{A})=0\}\qquad\tilde{\mathcal{F}}_{t}:=\bigcap_{s\geq t}\sigma(\tilde{\mathcal{F}}_{s}^{\prime}\cup\mathcal{N}).

This gives a complete, right-continuous filtration
(\tilde{\mathcal{F}}_{t})_{t\in[0,T]}
, dependent on
n,\varepsilon,\delta
, on the new probability space
(\tilde{\Omega},\tilde{\mathcal{F}},\tilde{\mathbb{P}})
, to which the limiting noise processes and solutions are adapted. The filtration
(\tilde{\mathcal{F}}^{N}_{t})_{t\in[0,T]}
is constructed similarly.
To prove that the new random variables
\tilde{\mathcal{U}}_{N}
satisfy the same weak formulation as the old random variables, we apply the same classical arguments as in
[
1
]
(see also Theorem 2.9.1 in
[
7
]
).

The third statement follows from Proposition
8.8
in Appendix A.
The fact that
\partial_{t}\eta=v
,
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost surely follows from the fact that
\partial_{t}\eta_{N}=v_{N}
,
\mathbb{P}
-almost surely by definition of the splitting scheme on the original probability space. So by equivalence of laws,
\partial_{t}\tilde{\eta}_{N}=\tilde{v}_{N}
,
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost surely, and then we obtain
\partial_{t}\eta=v
,
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost surely by passing to the limit as
N\to\infty
and using the convergences given by the Skorohod representation theorem.

We will now prove that
\tilde{\eta}^{*}_{N}
and
\mathcal{T}_{\Delta t}\tilde{\eta}^{*}_{N}
have the same limit
\eta^{*}
in
L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{2}(\Gamma))
where, we recall, that
\mathcal{T}_{\Delta t}\tilde{\eta}^{*}_{N}
denotes the time shift of
\tilde{\eta}^{*}_{N}
, defined by
\mathcal{T}_{\Delta t}\tilde{\eta}^{*}_{N}=\eta_{0}
on
[0,\Delta t]
and
\mathcal{T}_{\Delta t}\tilde{\eta}^{*}_{N}(t)=\tilde{\eta}^{*}_{N}(t-\Delta t)
for
t\in[\Delta t,T]
.
To see this, we compute for all
t\in[0,T]
:

\|\tilde{\eta}^{*}_{N}(t)-\mathcal{T}_{\Delta t}\tilde{\eta}^{*}_{N}(t)\|_{H^{2}(\Gamma)}\leq\int_{t-{\Delta t}}^{t}\|\partial_{t}\tilde{\eta}^{*}_{N}(t)\|_{H^{2}(\Gamma)}\leq C\int_{t-{\Delta t}}^{t}\|\tilde{v}_{N}(t)\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}\leq C(\Delta t)\|\tilde{v}_{N}\|_{C(0,T;L^{2}(\Gamma))},

where this calculation is justified by the definition of the stopped process
\eta^{*}_{N}
, the fact that
v_{N}=\partial_{t}\eta_{N}
, and equivalence of norms combined with
v_{N}\in C(0,T;X_{n}^{st})
. We note that
C
depends only on the Galerkin parameter
n
(which we emphasize is kept fixed in this limit passage
N\to\infty
) and hence is independent of the parameter
N
. So using Sobolev embedding and equivalence of laws, we can transfer this estimate to the new probability space:

\|\tilde{\eta}^{*}_{N}-\mathcal{T}_{\Delta t}\tilde{\eta}^{*}_{N}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{2}(\Gamma))}\leq C(\Delta t)\|\tilde{v}_{N}\|_{C(0,T;L^{2}(\Gamma))}.

We complete our proof by recalling that we have, on the new probability space, the convergence
\tilde{v}_{N}\to\tilde{v}

\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost surely in
C(0,T;L^{2}(\Gamma))
. ∎

Recall from the definition (
37
) of the phase space
\mathcal{X}
that
(\tilde{\rho}_{N},\tilde{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{N},\tilde{\eta}_{N},\tilde{\eta}^{*}_{N},\tilde{v}_{N},\tilde{W}_{N}^{1},\tilde{W}_{N}^{2})
transferred to the new probability space take values in

C(0,T;L^{\beta}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}))\times C(0,T;L^{2}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}))\times C(0,T;C(\Gamma))^{2}\times C(0,T;L^{2}(\Gamma))\times C(0,T;{\mathbb{R}})^{2}.

Moreover, we argue that

(43)

\displaystyle{\eta}^{*}(t)={\eta}(t)\quad\text{ for any }t<\tau^{\eta},\quad\tilde{\mathbb{P}}\text{-almost surely.}

where for a given
\alpha
,

\tau^{\eta}:=T\wedge\inf\left\{t>0:\inf_{\Gamma}(1+\eta(t))\leq\alpha\text{ or }\|{\eta}(t)\|_{H^{s}(\Gamma)}\geq\frac{1}{\alpha}\right\}.

Indeed, observe that for almost any
\omega\in\tilde{\Omega}
and
t<\tau^{\eta}
, and for any
\epsilon>0
, there exists an
N
such that

\displaystyle\|{\eta}(t)-{\eta}^{*}(t)\|_{H^{s}(\Gamma)}

\displaystyle<\|{\eta}(t)-\tilde{\eta}_{N}(t)\|_{H^{s}(\Gamma)}+\|{\eta}^{*}(t)-\tilde{\eta}^{*}_{N}(t)\|_{H^{s}(\Gamma)}+\|\tilde{\eta}^{*}_{N}(t)-\tilde{\eta}_{N}(t)\|_{H^{s}(\Gamma)}<\epsilon.

This is true because, the almost sure uniform convergence of the structure displacements implies that for any
\epsilon>0
there exists an
N_{1}\in\mathbb{N}
such that the first two terms on the right side of the above inequality
are each bounded by
\frac{\epsilon}{2}
for all
N\geq N_{1}
.
Moreover, due to the uniform convergence of the structure displacement for a fixed outcome,
t<\tau^{\eta}
implies that
t<\tau^{\eta}_{N}
for infinitely many
N
’s thence the third term is equal to 0. This concludes the proof of (
43
).

Finally, we show that the new limiting random variables satisfy the desired weak formulation by passing to the limit in the weak formulation of the continuity equation (
34
) and the semidiscrete weak formulation (
35
). We handle the convergence of each of the terms in the weak formulation (
35
) for each fixed deterministic test function
\boldsymbol{q}\in X_{n}^{f}
and
\psi\in X_{n}^{st}
by using the convergence results in Theorem
5.1
. Similar techniques work for passing to the limit in the weak formulation of the approximate continuity equation (
34
), so we focus only on the passage to the limit in the momentum weak formulation (
35
), and note that by the equivalence of laws given by Theorem
5.1
, the weak formulation also holds (with the new approximate random variables on the new probability space)
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost surely on the new probability space. We only comment on the most involved terms in this limit passage below:

•

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}\mu^{\tilde{\eta}^{*}_{N}}_{\delta}\nabla\tilde{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{N}:\nabla\boldsymbol{q}\to\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}\mu_{\delta}^{\eta^{*}}\nabla{\boldsymbol{u}}:\nabla\boldsymbol{q}
, where we use the
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost sure convergence of
\mu^{\tilde{\eta}^{*}_{N}}_{\delta}
to
\mu_{\delta}^{\eta^{*}}
in
C([0,T]\times{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha})
which follows from
\tilde{\eta}^{*}_{N}\to\eta^{*}
in
C(0,T;C(\Gamma))
and the properties of the map
\eta\to\mu_{\delta}^{\eta}
(see (
19
) and (
31
)), and the convergence of
\tilde{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{N}\to{\boldsymbol{u}}
in
C(0,T;X^{f}_{n})
. Similarly, we have
\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}\lambda^{\tilde{\eta}^{*}_{N}}_{\delta}\text{div}(\tilde{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{N})\text{div}(\boldsymbol{q})\to\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}\lambda^{\eta^{*}}_{\delta}\text{div}({\boldsymbol{u}})\text{div}(\boldsymbol{q})
.

•

Next, we have

\frac{1}{\delta}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{T^{\delta}_{\tilde{\eta}^{*}_{N}}}(\tilde{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{N}-\tilde{v}_{N}\boldsymbol{e}_{z})\cdot\boldsymbol{q}-\frac{1}{\delta}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{T^{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{\Delta t}\tilde{\eta}^{*}_{N}}}(\mathcal{T}_{\Delta t}\tilde{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{N}-\tilde{v}_{N}\boldsymbol{e}_{z})\cdot\psi\boldsymbol{e}_{z}\to\frac{1}{\delta}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{T^{\delta}_{\eta^{*}}}({\boldsymbol{u}}-v\boldsymbol{e}_{z})\cdot(\boldsymbol{q}-\psi\boldsymbol{e}_{z}).

This follows immediately from the fact that
\tilde{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{N}\to{\boldsymbol{u}}
and
\tilde{v}_{N}\boldsymbol{e}_{z}\to v\boldsymbol{e}_{z}
in
C(0,T;L^{2}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}))
,
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost surely, and that
\mathcal{T}_{\Delta t}\tilde{\eta}^{*}_{N}\to\eta^{*}
in
C(0,T;C(\Gamma))
(and similarly for
\tilde{\eta}^{*}_{N}
),
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost surely, which implies for the indicator functions

\mathbbm{1}_{T^{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{\Delta t}\tilde{\eta}^{*}_{N}}}\to\mathbbm{1}_{T^{\delta}_{\eta^{*}}},\quad\text{ in }C(0,T;L^{2}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})),\qquad\tilde{\mathbb{P}}\text{-almost surely.}

•

Next, we show convergence of the structure stochastic integral. Using classical results about convergence of stochastic integrals
[
1
]
(see also Lemma 2.1 in
[
15
]
and Lemma 2.6.6 in
[
7
]
), it suffices to show that

\int_{\Gamma}G(\tilde{\eta}_{N},\tilde{v}_{N})\psi\to\int_{\Gamma}G(\eta,v)\psi,\quad\text{ in probability in $L^{2}(0,T;L_{2}(\mathcal{U}_{0};{\mathbb{R}}))$}.

By the growth assumption (
8
) and Theorem
5.1
, we can verify this convergence, as we have:

\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{T}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}(g_{k}(\eta,v)-g_{k}(\tilde{\eta}_{N},\tilde{v}_{N}),\psi)^{2}\leq C\|\psi\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}(\Gamma)}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}|\eta-\tilde{\eta}_{N}|^{2}+|v-\tilde{v}_{N}|^{2}\to 0.

Finally, we show convergence of the stochastic integrals for the fluid equations, which is the most involved convergence. Before showing convergence of these stochastic integrals, we first show the following convergence result:

Lemma 5.1
.

For almost every
(\tilde{\omega},t)\in\tilde{\Omega}\times[0,T]
:

(44)

\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\|f_{n,k}(\rho,\rho{\boldsymbol{u}})-f_{n,k}(\tilde{\rho}_{N},\tilde{\rho}_{N}\tilde{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{N})\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})}^{2}\to 0,\quad\text{ as $N\to\infty$}.

Proof.

We start with the following observation that we will use throughout the proof. Since
\tilde{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{N}\to\tilde{{\boldsymbol{u}}}
in
C(0,T;X^{f}_{n})
,
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost surely,

(45)

\sup_{N}\|{\boldsymbol{u}}_{N}(\tilde{\omega})\|_{C(0,T;X^{f}_{n})}\leq C(\tilde{\omega})

for
C(\tilde{\omega})
depending on
\tilde{\omega}\in\tilde{\Omega}
. By equivalence of norms in
X^{f}_{n}
and the comparison principle (
41
),

(46)

0<c(\tilde{\omega})\leq\rho_{N}(\tilde{\omega})\leq C(\tilde{\omega}),\quad 0<c(\tilde{\omega})\leq\rho(\tilde{\omega})\leq C(\tilde{\omega})\quad\text{ for all $(t,x)\in[0,T]\times\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}$,}

for positive constants
c(\tilde{\omega})
and
C(\tilde{\omega})
(independent of
N
, where we recall that the Galerkin parameter
n
is fixed), which depend only on the outcome
\tilde{\omega}\in\tilde{\Omega}
.

We recall the definition of
\displaystyle f_{n,k}(\rho,\boldsymbol{q}):=\mathcal{M}^{1/2}[\rho]\left(P_{n}^{f}\left(\frac{f_{k}(\rho,\boldsymbol{q})}{\rho^{1/2}}\right)\right)
from (
33
) and show the desired convergence by handling each element of the nonlinearity in the definition of
f_{n,k}(\rho,\rho\boldsymbol{u})
one at a time. We sketch the proof below:

• By Lemma A.1 in
[
8
]
, for
\rho_{1},\rho_{2}\in L^{2}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})
with
\rho_{1},\rho_{2}\geq\kappa>0
and a constant depending only on the Galerkin parameter
n
and the positive lower bound on density
\kappa
:

(47)

\|\mathcal{M}^{1/2}[\rho_{1}]-\mathcal{M}^{1/2}[\rho_{2}]\|_{\mathcal{L}(X^{f}_{n},X^{f}_{n})}\leq C(n,\kappa)\|\rho_{1}-\rho_{2}\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})}.

We can apply this result due to (
46
) to get that
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost surely for all
t\in[0,T]
:

\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\left\|\Big(\mathcal{M}^{1/2}[\rho]-\mathcal{M}^{1/2}[\rho_{N}]\Big)\left(P_{n}^{f}\left(\frac{f_{k}(\rho,\boldsymbol{q})}{\rho^{1/2}}\right)\right)\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})}^{2}\leq\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}C(\tilde{\omega})\|\rho-\rho_{N}\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})}^{2}\left\|\frac{f_{k}(\rho,\boldsymbol{q})}{\rho^{1/2}}\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})}^{2},

for a random constant
C(\tilde{\omega})
. Since
\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\left\|\frac{f_{k}(\rho,\boldsymbol{q})}{\rho^{1/2}}\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})}^{2}
is bounded by energy estimates and the assumption (
7
) on the noise and
\tilde{\rho}_{N}\to\rho
in
C(0,T;L^{\beta}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}))
(where we recall that
\beta\geq 4
)
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost surely, we have that for almost every
(\tilde{\omega},t)\in\tilde{\Omega}\times[0,T]
:

(48)

\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\left\|\Big(\mathcal{M}^{1/2}[\rho]-\mathcal{M}^{1/2}[\rho_{N}]\Big)\left(P_{n}^{f}\left(\frac{f_{k}(\rho,\boldsymbol{q})}{\rho^{1/2}}\right)\right)\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})}^{2}\to 0.

• Since
\mathcal{M}[\rho]
for
\rho>0
is a positive definite, symmetric operator on the finite-dimensional space
X_{n}
with
\|\mathcal{M}[\rho]\|_{\mathcal{L}(X^{f}_{n},X^{f}_{n})}\leq\|\rho\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})}
, we have that
\mathcal{M}^{1/2}[\rho]:X^{f}_{n}\to X^{f}_{n}
is well-defined with the bound
\|\mathcal{M}^{1/2}[\rho]\|_{\mathcal{L}(X^{f}_{n},X^{f}_{n})}\leq\|\rho\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})}^{1/2}
. So by the estimate (
46
) which is independent of
N
, we have that
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost surely for all
t\in[0,T]
:

\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\left\|\mathcal{M}^{1/2}[\rho_{N}]\left(P_{n}^{f}\left(\frac{f_{k}(\rho,\rho{\boldsymbol{u}})}{\rho^{1/2}}\right)-P_{n}^{f}\left(\frac{f_{k}(\tilde{\rho}_{N},\tilde{\rho}_{N}\tilde{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{N})}{\tilde{\rho}_{N}^{1/2}}\right)\right)\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})}^{2}\\
\leq C(\tilde{\omega})\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\left\|\frac{f_{k}(\rho,\rho{\boldsymbol{u}})}{\rho^{1/2}}-\frac{f_{k}(\tilde{\rho}_{N},\tilde{\rho}_{N}\tilde{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{N})}{\tilde{\rho}_{N}^{1/2}}\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})}^{2}

for a random constant
C(\tilde{\omega})
. Then, by (
7
), (
45
), (
46
), and the
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost sure convergences of
\tilde{\rho}_{N}\to\rho
in
C(0,T;L^{\beta}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}))
and
\tilde{\boldsymbol{u}}_{N}\to\boldsymbol{u}
in
C(0,T;X^{f}_{n})
, we have that
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost surely, for all
t\in[0,T]
:

\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\left\|\frac{f_{k}(\rho,\rho{\boldsymbol{u}})-f_{k}(\tilde{\rho}_{N},\tilde{\rho}_{N}\tilde{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{N})}{\tilde{\rho}_{N}^{1/2}}\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})}^{2}\leq C(\tilde{\omega})\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\|f_{k}(\rho,\rho{\boldsymbol{u}})-f_{k}(\tilde{\rho}_{N},\tilde{\rho}_{N}\tilde{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{N})\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})}^{2}\to 0.

By using (
7
) (
45
), (
46
), and the mean value theorem to estimate
\rho^{-1/2}-\tilde{\rho}_{N}^{-1/2}
, one can verify that for all
t\in[0,T]
:

\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\left\|f_{k}(\rho,\rho{\boldsymbol{u}})\left(\frac{1}{\rho^{1/2}}-\frac{1}{\tilde{\rho}_{N}^{1/2}}\right)\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})}^{2}\to 0,\quad\tilde{\mathbb{P}}\text{-almost surely.}

So for almost every
(\tilde{\omega},t)\in\tilde{\Omega}\times[0,T]
,
\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\left\|\frac{f_{k}(\rho,\rho{\boldsymbol{u}})}{\rho^{1/2}}-\frac{f_{k}(\tilde{\rho}_{N},\tilde{\rho}_{N}\tilde{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{N})}{\rho_{N}^{1/2}}\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})}^{2}\to 0
and also:

(49)

\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\left\|\mathcal{M}^{1/2}[\rho_{N}]\left(P_{n}^{f}\left(\frac{f_{k}(\rho,\rho{\boldsymbol{u}})}{\rho^{1/2}}\right)-P_{n}^{f}\left(\frac{f_{k}(\tilde{\rho}_{N},\tilde{\rho}_{N}\tilde{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{N})}{\tilde{\rho}_{N}^{1/2}}\right)\right)\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})}\to 0.

Together, (
48
) and (
49
) prove the desired convergence (
44
), which completes the proof.
∎

Using the convergence stated in the immediately preceding lemma, we can thus verify the following convergence result for the stochastic integrals as
N\to\infty
.

Proposition 5.2
.

For a given deterministic pair
(\boldsymbol{q},\psi)\in X_{n}
for a fixed Galerkin parameter
n
,

\int_{0}^{T}\Big(\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\tilde{\eta}^{*}_{N}}}\boldsymbol{F}_{n}(\tilde{\rho}_{N},\tilde{\rho}_{N}\tilde{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{N})d\tilde{W}_{N}^{1},\boldsymbol{q}\Big)\to\int_{0}^{T}\Big(\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\eta^{*}}}\boldsymbol{F}_{n}(\rho,\rho{\boldsymbol{u}})d\tilde{W}^{1},\boldsymbol{q}\Big),\quad\text{ $\tilde{\mathbb{P}}$-almost surely,}

where
\boldsymbol{F}_{n}
is defined in (
33
).

Proof.

To do this, by classical methods of
[
1
]
(for proofs see Lemma 2.1 in
[
15
]
and Lemma 2.6.6 in
[
7
]
), it suffices to show that

(50)

(\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\tilde{\eta}^{*}_{N}}}\boldsymbol{F}_{n}(\tilde{\rho}_{N},\tilde{\rho}_{N}\tilde{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{N}),\boldsymbol{q})\to(\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\eta^{*}}}\boldsymbol{F}_{n}(\rho,\rho{\boldsymbol{u}}),\boldsymbol{q})\quad\text{ in probability in }L^{2}(0,T;L_{2}(\mathcal{U}_{0};\mathbb{R})),

where
L_{2}(X,Y)
is the Hilbert-Schmidt norm of operators mapping Hilbert space
X
to
Y
.
We do this by showing the following:

(51)

\displaystyle(\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\tilde{\eta}^{*}_{N}}}\boldsymbol{F}_{n}(\tilde{\rho}_{N},\tilde{\rho}_{N}\tilde{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{N}),\boldsymbol{q})\to(\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\eta^{*}}}\boldsymbol{F}_{n}(\rho,\rho{\boldsymbol{u}}),\boldsymbol{q})\quad\text{in $L_{2}(\mathcal{U}_{0};{\mathbb{R}})$, for a.e. }(\tilde{\omega},t)\in\tilde{\Omega}\times[0,T],

(52)

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{T}\|\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\tilde{\eta}^{*}_{N}}}\boldsymbol{F}_{n}(\tilde{\rho}_{N},\tilde{\rho}_{N}\tilde{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{N}),\boldsymbol{q})-(\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\eta^{*}}}\boldsymbol{F}_{n}(\rho,\rho{\boldsymbol{u}}),\boldsymbol{q})\|_{L_{2}(\mathcal{U}_{0};{\mathbb{R}})}^{p}\leq C_{p},\ \text{ for $p\geq 2$},

where
C_{p}
depends only on
p\geq 2
. These two facts imply by the Vitali convergence theorem that

\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{T}\|\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\tilde{\eta}^{*}_{N}}}\boldsymbol{F}_{n}(\tilde{\rho}_{N},\tilde{\rho}_{N}\tilde{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{N}),\boldsymbol{q})-(\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\eta^{*}}}\boldsymbol{F}_{n}(\rho,\rho{\boldsymbol{u}}),\boldsymbol{q})\|_{L_{2}(\mathcal{U}_{0};{\mathbb{R}})}^{2}\to 0\quad\text{ as }N\to\infty,

from which (
50
) immediately follows.

Proof of (
51
).

Recalling the definition of
\displaystyle f_{n,k}(\rho,\boldsymbol{q}):=\mathcal{M}^{1/2}[\rho]\left(P_{n}^{f}\left(\frac{f_{k}(\rho,\boldsymbol{q})}{\rho^{1/2}}\right)\right)
from (
33
) we prove the following convergences:

(53)

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\Big(\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\eta^{*}}}f_{n,k}(\rho,\rho{\boldsymbol{u}})-\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\tilde{\eta}_{N}^{*}}}f_{n,k}(\rho,\rho{\boldsymbol{u}}),\boldsymbol{q}\Big)^{2}\to 0,\quad\text{ for a.e. $(\tilde{\omega},t)\in\tilde{\Omega}\times[0,T]$}.

(54)

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\Big(\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{{\tilde{\eta}_{N}^{*}}}}(f_{n,k}(\rho,\rho\boldsymbol{u})-f_{n,k}(\tilde{\rho}_{N},\tilde{\rho}_{N}\tilde{\boldsymbol{u}}_{N})),\boldsymbol{q}\Big)^{2}\to 0,\quad\text{ for a.e. $(\tilde{\omega},t)\in\tilde{\Omega}\times[0,T]$},

The result (
53
) follows from the fact that
\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\tilde{\eta}_{N}^{*}}}\to\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\eta^{*}}}
in
L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{p}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))
,
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost surely for any
1\leq p<\infty
whereas
(
54
) follows from (
44
).

Proof of (
52
).
Recalling the definition in (
33
), we estimate that for
p\geq 2
:

\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{T}\|(\boldsymbol{F}_{n}(\tilde{\rho}_{N},\tilde{\rho}_{N}\tilde{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{N}),\boldsymbol{q}))-(\boldsymbol{F}_{n}(\rho,\rho{\boldsymbol{u}}),\boldsymbol{q})\|_{L_{2}(\mathcal{U}_{0};{\mathbb{R}})}^{p}\\
\leq C\|\boldsymbol{q}\|^{p}_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})}\left[\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{T}\left(\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\|f_{n,k}(\tilde{\rho}_{N},\tilde{\rho}_{N}\tilde{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{N})\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})}^{2}\right)^{p/2}+\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{T}\left(\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\|f_{n,k}(\rho,\rho{\boldsymbol{u}})\|_{L_{2}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})}^{2}\right)^{p/2}\right].

This is uniformly bounded independently of
N
as a result of the definition of
f_{n,k}(\rho,q)
in (
33
), the estimate
\|\mathcal{M}^{1/2}[\rho]\|_{\mathcal{L}(X^{f}_{n},X^{f}_{n})}\leq C_{n}\|\rho\|_{L^{1}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})}^{1/2}
, the assumption (
7
) on the noise, and the uniform moment estimates on the energy (which are independent of
N
) in (
36
).

∎

6.
The Galerkin approximation: Passing to the limit
n\to\infty

In this section, we will emphasize the dependence of the martingale solution constructed on the probability space
(\tilde{\Omega},\tilde{\mathcal{F}},\tilde{\mathbb{P}})
in the previous section on the parameter
n
and we hence denote it by
(\rho_{n},{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n},\eta_{n},\eta^{*}_{n},v_{n},W_{n}^{1},W_{n}^{2})
. Notice that for that simplicity of notation, we temporarily suppress the dependence of this solution on the other parameters
\varepsilon,\delta
.
The aim of this section is to obtain bounds, uniformly in
n
, for these approximate solutions with the intent of passing
n
to
\infty
.
Recall that the random variables constructed in the previous section satisfy the following equation

(55)

\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}\rho_{n}(t){\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}(t)\cdot\boldsymbol{q}+\int_{\Gamma}v_{n}(t)\psi=\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}\boldsymbol{p}_{0,\delta,\varepsilon}\cdot\boldsymbol{q}+\int_{\Gamma}v_{0}\psi\\
+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}(\rho_{n}{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}\otimes{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}):\nabla\boldsymbol{q}+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}\Big(a\rho_{n}^{\gamma}+\delta\rho_{n}^{\beta}\Big)(\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{q})-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}{\mu^{\eta^{*}_{n}}_{\delta}}\nabla{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}:\nabla\boldsymbol{q}\\
{+\varepsilon\int_{0}^{t}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}}\ {\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}\rho_{n}\cdot\Delta{\boldsymbol{q}}}-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}{\lambda^{\eta^{*}_{n}}_{\delta}}\text{div}({\boldsymbol{u}}_{n})\text{div}(\boldsymbol{q})-\frac{1}{\delta}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{{T^{\delta}_{\eta^{*}_{n}}}}({\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}-v_{n}\boldsymbol{e}_{z})\cdot(\boldsymbol{q}-\psi\boldsymbol{e}_{z})\\
-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Gamma}\nabla v_{n}\cdot\nabla\psi-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Gamma}\nabla\eta_{n}\cdot\nabla\psi-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Gamma}\Delta\eta_{n}\Delta\psi\\
+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\eta_{n}^{*}}}\boldsymbol{F}_{n}(\rho_{n},\rho_{n}{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n})\cdot\boldsymbol{q}dW_{n}^{1}+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Gamma}G_{n}(\eta_{n},v_{n})\psi dW_{n}^{2},

\tilde{{\mathbb{P}}}
-almost surely for any
t\in[0,T]
and for any test function
\boldsymbol{q}\in X_{n}^{f}
and
\psi\in X^{st}_{n}
. Here,

\partial_{t}\eta_{n}=v_{n}.

Moreover, we have that the continuity equation is satisfied in a distributional sense,
\tilde{{\mathbb{P}}}
-almost surely as follows:

(56)

\partial_{t}\rho_{n}+\text{div}(\rho_{n}{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n})=\varepsilon\Delta\rho_{n},\,\,\text{ in }\mathcal{O}_{\alpha},\qquad\nabla\rho_{n}\cdot\boldsymbol{n}|_{\partial\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}=0,\qquad{{\rho_{n}(0)=\rho_{0,\delta,\varepsilon}}},

where
\boldsymbol{n}
is the unit normal to the boundary of the fixed maximal domain
{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}
.
Thanks to weak lower semicontinuity of the norm, the energy estimates found in Section
5
hold true for the Galerkin approximations which gives us the following result.

Lemma 6.1
.

The sequence of solutions
(\rho_{n},{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n},\eta_{n},\eta^{*}_{n},v_{n})
to (
55
) satisfies the following bounds: For any
p\geq 1
, there exists a constant
C>0
, independent of
n
, such that

(1)

\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\|{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}\|^{p}_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{1}_{0}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))}\leq C(\delta)
,

(2)

\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\|\sqrt{\rho_{n}}{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}\|^{p}_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))}\leq C
,

(3)

\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\|\delta^{\frac{1}{\beta}}\rho_{n}\|^{p}_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{\beta}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))}\leq C
,

(4)

\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\|\sqrt{\varepsilon\delta}\rho_{n}^{\frac{\beta}{2}}\|^{p}_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{1}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))}\leq C,\quad\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\|\sqrt{\varepsilon}\rho_{n}^{\frac{\gamma}{2}}\|^{p}_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{1}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))}\leq C
,

(5)

\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\|\sqrt{\varepsilon}\rho_{n}\|^{p}_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{1}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))}\leq C
,

(6)

\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\|v_{n}\|^{p}_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{1}(\Gamma))}\leq C
, where
\partial_{t}\eta_{n}=v_{n}
,

(7)

\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\|\eta_{n}\|^{p}_{{W}^{1,\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\Gamma))\cap L^{2}(0,T;H^{2}(\Gamma))}\leq C
,
\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\|\eta^{*}_{n}\|^{p}_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{2}(\Gamma))}\leq C
.

(8)

\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\|{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}-v_{n}{\bf e}_{z}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(T^{\delta}_{\eta^{*}_{n}}))}^{p}\leq C\delta^{\frac{p}{2}}
.

Proof.

Statement (5) is the only one that requires further explanation as the remaining statements are a direct consequence of the energy estimate (
36
). For that purpose, we consider the continuity equation (
56
) and test it with
\rho_{n}
to obtain that

(57)

\displaystyle{{2}}\varepsilon\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}}|\nabla\rho_{n}|^{2}

\displaystyle+\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}}|\rho_{n}|^{2}=\|\rho_{0,\delta,\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{L^{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha})}-\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}}\nabla\cdot{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}|\rho_{n}|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq{{\|\rho_{0,\delta,\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})}}}+\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\|{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{1}_{0}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))}+\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}}|\rho_{n}|^{4}\leq C,

where we recall in the final inequality that the artificial pressure exponent satisfies
\beta\geq 4
, and where we use the uniform bound in Lemma
6.1
(3).
∎

We quickly notice the following implications of the energy estimates Lemma
6.1
.
First, for any
p>1
there exists a constant
C_{p}>0
independent of
n
:

\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\|\rho_{n}{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}\|^{p}_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{\frac{2\beta}{\beta+1}}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))}\leq C_{p}.

Indeed, we apply the Hölder inequality to
\rho_{n}{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}
with
\beta+1
and
\frac{\beta+1}{\beta}
to obtain

(58)

\begin{split}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}}(\sqrt{\rho_{n}}|\sqrt{\rho_{n}}{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}|)^{\frac{2\beta}{\beta+1}}&\leq\left(\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}}|\rho_{n}|^{\beta}\right)^{\frac{1}{\beta+1}}\left(\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}}|\sqrt{\rho_{n}}{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}|^{2}\right)^{\frac{\beta}{\beta+1}}\\
&\leq C.\end{split}

Consequently, we see that div
(\rho_{n}{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n})
is bounded in
L^{p}(\tilde{\Omega};L^{\infty}(0,T;W^{-1,\frac{2\beta}{\beta+1}}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha})))
for all
p\in[1,\infty)
. We also know that
\varepsilon\Delta\rho_{n}
is bounded independently of
n
in
L^{{p}}(\tilde{\Omega};L^{\infty}(0,T;W^{-2,2}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha})))
.
Hence we conclude by using the continuity equation that for any
p\geq 1
, there exists
C>0
independent of
n
for which

(59)

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\|\rho_{n}\|^{p}_{W^{1,\infty}(0,T;W^{-2,\frac{2\beta}{\beta+1}}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))}\leq C.

Now we will derive uniform estimates for the fractional time derivative of order
<\frac{1}{2}
for the structural velocity which will enable us to obtain tightness of its laws in
L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x}
. While the ideas used in the previous section are still applicable, we will derive estimates that are independent not only of
n
but also of
\varepsilon
and
\delta
so that these estimates can be used in the subsequent sections as well.

Lemma 6.2
.

For some
0<\bar{\kappa}<\frac{1}{2}
and
C>0
independent of
n,\varepsilon
and
\delta
we have that

(60)

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\left[\sup_{0<h<T}\frac{1}{h^{\bar{\kappa}}}\|\mathcal{T}_{h}v_{n}-v_{n}\|_{L^{2}(h,T;L^{2}(\Gamma))}\right]\leq C,

where
\mathcal{T}_{h}f(t)=f(t-h)
.

Proof.

To obtain the left-hand side term in (
60
) we will test the weak formulation (
55
) with the time integral from
t-h
to
t
of a modification of the solutions
{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}
and
v_{n}
. This modification is necessary since
{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}
and
v_{n}
do not possess the required spatial regularity of a test function. Hence, this modification will be obtained by regularizing the approximate fluid and structure velocities.

To that end, we consider the following
H^{1}
-extension of the fluid velocity to
\Gamma\times(0,\infty)
constructed by interpolating between the fluid velocity
{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}
and the structure velocity
v_{n}
in a transition region of width
\lambda_{1}\delta^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{\beta}}
near the top boundary of the tubular region
T^{\delta}_{\eta^{*}_{n}}
for some
0<\lambda_{1}<1
:

{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{n,\text{ext}}(t)={{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{n}(t)+\left[\min\left(\frac{z-(1+{\eta}^{*}_{n}(t)+\delta^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{\beta}})}{\lambda_{1}\delta^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{\beta}}}+1,1\right)\right]^{+}\Big({v}_{n}(t)\boldsymbol{e}_{z}-{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{n}(t)\Big).

Since
{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n,ext}={\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}
in
{\mathcal{O}}_{\eta^{*}_{n}+(1-\lambda_{1})\delta^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{\beta}}}
(see Definition
1
) for any
q<6
and
p\geq 1
, we observe that

(61)

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\|{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n,ext}-{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}\|^{p}_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{q}({\mathcal{O}}_{\eta^{*}_{n}}\cup T^{\delta}_{\eta^{*}_{n}}))}\leq\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\|{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}-v_{n}\boldsymbol{e}_{z}\|^{p}_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{6}(T^{\delta}_{\eta^{*}_{n}}))}|\lambda_{1}\delta^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{\beta}}|^{\frac{6-q}{6q}}\leq C|\lambda_{1}|^{\frac{6-q}{6q}}.

Now for some
1<\sigma<2
we will squeeze this extended function vertically as follows,

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n,\sigma}(t,x,y,z)={\boldsymbol{u}}_{n,ext}(t,x,y,\sigma z).

Note that for some
C>0
independent of
n,\varepsilon
and
\delta
(and
\sigma,\lambda_{1}
) we have any
q\in(1,\infty)
that

(62)

\displaystyle\|{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n,\sigma}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{q}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))}\leq\frac{1}{\sigma}\|{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n,ext}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{q}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))}\leq(\|{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{q}({\mathcal{O}}_{\eta^{*}_{n}}\cup T^{\delta}_{\eta^{*}_{n}}))}+\|v_{n}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{q}(\Gamma))}),

and for any
r<2
and
p\geq 1
that,

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\|{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n,\sigma}\|^{p}_{L^{2}(0,T;W^{1,r}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))}

\displaystyle\leq C\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\|{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n,ext}\|^{p}_{L^{2}(0,T;W^{1,r}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))}

(63)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{\lambda_{1}^{p}}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}(\|{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{1}({\mathcal{O}}_{\eta^{*}_{n}}\cup T_{\eta^{*}_{n}}^{\delta}))}+\|v_{n}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{1}(\Gamma))})^{p}\leq\frac{C}{\lambda_{1}^{p}},

and for some
C
depending only on
0<m<1,l>1
and
\alpha
(see (47) in
[
42
]
) we have

(64)

\displaystyle\|{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n,\sigma}-{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n,ext}\|^{p}_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{l}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))}\leq C(1-\sigma)^{(\frac{3}{l}+m)p}\|{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n,ext}\|^{p}_{L^{2}(0,T;W^{m,l}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))}.

Note also that,

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n,\sigma}(x,y,z)-v_{n}(x,y)\boldsymbol{e}_{z}

\displaystyle={\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}(x,y,\sigma z)-v_{n}(x,y)\boldsymbol{e}_{z}\quad\text{if}\quad z\leq\frac{1+\eta^{*}_{n}+(1-\lambda_{1})\delta^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{\beta}}}{\sigma}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{(1+{\eta}^{*}_{n}+\delta^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{\beta}})-z}{\lambda_{1}\delta^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{\beta}}}\right)({\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}(x,y,\sigma z)-v_{n}(x,y)\boldsymbol{e}_{z})

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt\text{if}\quad\frac{1+\eta^{*}_{n}+(1-\lambda_{1})\delta^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{\beta}}}{\sigma}\leq z\leq\frac{1+\eta^{*}_{n}+\delta^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{\beta}}}{\sigma}

\displaystyle=0\qquad\text{otherwise}.

Hence,

(65)

\displaystyle\int_{\Gamma}\int_{\frac{1+\eta^{*}_{n}}{\sigma}}^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}|{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n,\sigma}-v_{n}\boldsymbol{e}_{z}|^{2}\leq\int_{\Gamma}\int_{1+\eta^{*}_{n}}^{1+\eta^{*}_{n}+\delta^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{\beta}}}|{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}-v_{n}\boldsymbol{e}_{z}|^{2}\leq\|{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}-v_{n}\boldsymbol{e}_{z}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(T^{\delta}_{\eta^{*}_{n}})}.

Now we will choose
\lambda\sim\sigma-1
such that

(66)

\displaystyle\text{dist}(\Gamma_{1+\eta^{*}_{n}},\Gamma_{\frac{1+\eta^{*}_{n}}{\sigma}})\geq\lambda.

Next, we let

\mathcal{S}^{\lambda,\delta}_{\eta}=\{(x,y,z):0<z<1+\eta+\delta^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{\beta}}+\lambda\},

and define

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}^{\lambda}=\left(\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{S}^{\lambda,\delta}_{\eta^{*}_{n}}}\right)^{\lambda}({\boldsymbol{u}}_{n,\sigma})^{\lambda},\qquad v_{n}^{\lambda}=(v_{n})^{\lambda},

where for any
f\in L^{p}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha})
,
p\geq 1
we denote its space regularization, using the standard 3D mollifiers, by
(f)^{\lambda}\in C^{\infty}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha})
.
Finally, we will take

(67)

{\boldsymbol{q}}_{n}=\int_{t-h}^{t}{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}^{\lambda},\qquad\psi_{n}=\int_{t-h}^{t}v_{n}^{\lambda},

We fix an
h>0
and let

\lambda\sim\sigma-1\sim h^{\theta}
for some appropriately chosen
\theta<\frac{1}{5}
, and we pick
\lambda_{1}\sim\lambda^{\frac{1}{4}(1-\frac{3}{2\gamma})}
; the reasons behind these choices will be apparent later in our calculations.

We test the coupled momentum equation (
55
) with
({\boldsymbol{q}}_{n},\psi_{n})
by applying the variant of the Ito formula given in Lemma 5.1 of
[
12
]
.
This yields,

\displaystyle-\int_{h}^{T}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}}\rho_{n}{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}\cdot\partial_{t}{\boldsymbol{q}}_{n}-\int_{h}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}v_{n}\partial_{t}\psi_{n}=\int_{h}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}(\rho_{n}{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}\otimes{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}):\nabla\boldsymbol{q}_{n}+\int_{h}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}\Big(a\rho_{n}^{\gamma}+\delta\rho_{n}^{\beta}\Big)(\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{q}_{n})

\displaystyle-\int_{{h}}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}\mu^{\eta^{*}_{n}}_{\delta}\nabla{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}:\nabla\boldsymbol{q}_{n}-\int_{h}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}\lambda^{\eta^{*}_{n}}_{\delta}\text{div}({\boldsymbol{u}}_{n})\text{div}(\boldsymbol{q}_{n})+\varepsilon\int_{h}^{T}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}}{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}\rho_{n}\cdot\Delta{\boldsymbol{q}}_{n}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{\delta}\int_{h}^{T}\int_{{T^{\delta}_{\eta^{*}_{n}}}}({\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}-v_{n}\boldsymbol{e}_{z})\cdot(\boldsymbol{q}_{n}-\psi_{n}\boldsymbol{e}_{z})-\int_{h}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\nabla v_{n}\cdot\nabla\psi_{n}-\int_{h}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\nabla\eta_{n}\cdot\nabla\psi_{n}-\int_{h}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\Delta\eta_{n}\Delta\psi_{n}

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}G_{n}(\eta_{n},v_{n})\psi_{n}dW_{n}^{2}(t)+\int_{h}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\eta_{n}^{*}}}\boldsymbol{F}_{n}(\rho_{n},\rho_{n}{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n})\cdot\boldsymbol{q}_{n}dW_{n}^{1}(t)

(68)

\displaystyle=I_{1}+...+I_{10}.

We will repeatedly use the following properties of mollification: For any
s,m\in\mathbb{R}
and
p>1
,

(69)

\begin{split}\|f-(f)^{\lambda}\|_{W^{s,p}}&\leq C\lambda^{m-s}\|f\|_{W^{m,p}},\\
\|(f)^{\lambda}\|_{W^{m+k,p}}&\leq C\lambda^{-k}\|f\|_{W^{m,p}},\quad\forall k\geq 0,\end{split}

where
(f)^{\lambda}
denotes the space regularization of
f\in L^{p}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha})
.
We will first analyze the two terms on the LHS. We begin with the more critical term:

\displaystyle\int_{h}^{T}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}}

\displaystyle\rho_{n}{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}\cdot\partial_{t}{\boldsymbol{q}}_{n}=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}}\rho_{n}{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}\cdot\left({\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}^{\lambda}(t)-{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}^{\lambda}(t-h)\right)

\displaystyle=\int_{h}^{T}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}}\rho_{n}^{\lambda}{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}^{\lambda}\cdot\left({\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}^{\lambda}(t)-{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}^{\lambda}(t-h)\right)+\rho_{n}({\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}-{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}^{\lambda})\left({\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}^{\lambda}(t)-{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}^{\lambda}(t-h)\right)

\displaystyle+\int_{h}^{T}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}}(\rho_{n}-\rho_{n}^{\lambda}){\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}^{\lambda}\left({\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}^{\lambda}(t)-{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}^{\lambda}(t-h)\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\int_{h}^{T}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}}\rho_{n}^{\lambda}(t)|{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}^{\lambda}(t)-{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}^{\lambda}(t-h)|^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\int_{h}^{T}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}}\rho_{n}^{\lambda}(t)\Big(|{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}^{\lambda}(t)|^{2}-|{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}^{\lambda}(t-h)|^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle+\int_{h}^{T}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}}\rho_{n}({\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}-{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}^{\lambda})\left({\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}^{\lambda}(t)-{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}^{\lambda}(t-h)\right)+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}}(\rho_{n}-\rho_{n}^{\lambda}){\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}^{\lambda}\left({\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}^{\lambda}(t)-{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}^{\lambda}(t-h)\right)

\displaystyle=I_{0}^{1}+...+I_{0}^{4}.

Observe that, since
\rho_{n}\geq 0
, the first term is nonnegative:

\displaystyle 0\leq I^{1}_{0}=\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}}\rho_{n}^{\lambda}(t)|{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}^{\lambda}(t)-{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}^{\lambda}(t-h)|^{2}.

Now, we will consider the second term
I_{0}^{2}
.

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{h}^{T}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}}\rho_{n}^{\lambda}(t)\left(|{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}^{\lambda}(t)|^{2}-|{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}^{\lambda}(t-h)|^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{h}^{T}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}}\rho_{n}^{\lambda}(t)|{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}^{\lambda}(t)|^{2}-\rho_{n}^{\lambda}(t-h)|{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}^{\lambda}(t-h)|^{2}-\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}}(\rho_{n}^{\lambda}(t)-\rho_{n}^{\lambda}(t-h))|{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}^{\lambda}(t-h)|^{2}.

Notice for the first term on the right hand side that

\displaystyle\left|\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{h}^{T}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}}\rho_{n}^{\lambda}(t)|{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}^{\lambda}(t)|^{2}-\rho_{n}^{\lambda}(t-h)|{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}^{\lambda}(t-h)|^{2}\right|

\displaystyle=\left|-\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{h}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}}\rho_{n}^{\lambda}|{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}^{\lambda}(t)|^{2}+\int_{T-h}^{T}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}}\rho_{n}^{\lambda}|{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}^{\lambda}(t)|^{2}\right|

\displaystyle\leq Ch\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\|\sqrt{\rho^{\lambda}_{n}}{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}^{\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))}^{2}\leq Ch.

We will next treat the second term on the right hand side. Recall the bounds (
59
) for
\partial_{t}\rho_{n}
. Note that
|\left(\mathbbm{1}_{T_{\eta^{*}_{n}+\lambda}}\right)^{\lambda}|\leq 1
. Moreover since
\beta>3
implies that
\frac{4\beta}{\beta-1}<6
and we have (
63
), we obtain,

\displaystyle|\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}}(\rho_{n}^{\lambda}(t)-\rho_{n}^{\lambda}(t-h))|{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}^{\lambda}(t-h)|^{2}|\leq Ch\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\left({\|\partial_{t}\rho_{n}^{\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{\frac{2\beta}{\beta+1}}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))}}\|({\boldsymbol{u}}_{n,\sigma})^{\lambda}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{\frac{4\beta}{\beta-1}}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))}\right)

\displaystyle\leq Ch\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\left({\lambda^{-2}\|\partial_{t}\rho_{n}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;W^{-2,{\frac{2\beta}{\beta+1}}}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))}}{{\|{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n,\sigma}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{1}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))}}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq Ch\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\left({\lambda^{-2}\|\partial_{t}\rho_{n}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;W^{-2,{\frac{2\beta}{\beta+1}}}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))}}\lambda_{1}^{-2}(\|{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{1}({\mathcal{O}}_{\eta^{*}_{n}}\cup T_{\eta^{*}_{n}}^{\delta}))}+\|v_{n}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{1}(\Gamma))})\right)

\displaystyle\leq Ch^{\kappa_{1}}.

We note here that, in the subsequent sections (e.g. Section
8
), when we do not have the
\beta
-integrability of the pressure, this estimate for
I_{0}^{2}
will change slightly as we will find bounds in terms of expectation of the
W^{1,\infty}(0,T;W^{-1,{\frac{2\gamma}{\gamma+1}}}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))
norm of the density. Thus the final estimate will possibly contain a smaller power of
\lambda
, depending on
\gamma
, arising from the inverse estimate for the fluid velocity.

Noting that
{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}^{\lambda}
has support in
\mathcal{S}_{\eta^{*}_{n}}^{2\lambda,\delta}
, we write the third term
I^{3}_{0}
as,

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\left|\int_{h}^{T}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}}\rho_{n}({\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}-{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}^{\lambda})\left({\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}^{\lambda}(t)-{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}^{\lambda}(t-h)\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{h}^{T}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}}\rho_{n}\left(|{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n,\sigma}-({\boldsymbol{u}}_{n,\sigma})^{\lambda}|+|{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n,ext}-{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n,\sigma}|\right)\left(\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{S}^{\lambda,\delta}_{\eta^{*}_{n}}}\right)^{\lambda}\left|\left({\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}^{\lambda}(t)-{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}^{\lambda}(t-h)\right)\right|

\displaystyle\qquad+\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{h}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{S}_{\eta^{*}_{n}}^{2\lambda,\delta}}\rho_{n}|{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n,ext}|\left|\left(\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{S}^{\lambda,\delta}_{\eta^{*}_{n}}}\right)^{\lambda}-\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\eta^{*}_{n}}\cup T^{\delta}_{\eta^{*}_{n}}}\right|\left|\left({\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}^{\lambda}(t)-{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}^{\lambda}(t-h)\right)\right|

\displaystyle\qquad+\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{h}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\eta^{*}_{n}}\cup T^{\delta}_{\eta^{*}_{n}}}\rho_{n}|{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n,ext}-{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}|\left|\left({\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}^{\lambda}(t)-{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}^{\lambda}(t-h)\right)\right|.

Now we choose
q<6
and
t>0
such that
1=\frac{1}{q}+\frac{1}{\gamma}+\frac{1}{t}+\frac{1}{6}
which is possible since, by assumption
\gamma>\frac{3}{2}
. Using (
61
), (
64
), and (
69
) we can then bound the right hand side as follows:

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\|\rho_{n}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{\gamma}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))}\left(\|({\boldsymbol{u}}_{n,\sigma})^{\lambda}-{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n,\sigma}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{\frac{6\gamma}{5\gamma-6}}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))}+\|{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n,ext}-{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n,\sigma}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{\frac{6\gamma}{5\gamma-6}}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\|({\boldsymbol{u}}_{n,\sigma})^{\lambda}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{6}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))}\Big]

\displaystyle\qquad+\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\left(\|\rho_{n}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{\gamma}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))}\|{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n,ext}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{q}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))}|\mathcal{S}_{\eta^{*}_{n}}^{2\lambda,\delta}\setminus T^{\delta}_{\eta^{*}_{n}}|^{\frac{1}{t}}\|({\boldsymbol{u}}_{n,\sigma})^{\lambda}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{6}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\left(\|\rho_{n}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{\gamma}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))}\|{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n,ext}-{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{\frac{6\gamma}{5\gamma-6}}(\mathcal{O}_{\eta^{*}_{n}}\cup T^{\delta}_{\eta^{*}_{n}}))}\|({\boldsymbol{u}}_{n,\sigma})^{\lambda}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{6}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\|\rho_{n}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{\gamma}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))}\left(\lambda^{1-\frac{3}{2\gamma}}\|{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n,\sigma}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;W^{1,r_{1}}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))}+(1-\sigma)^{\frac{7\gamma-9}{2\gamma}}\|{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n,ext}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;W^{1,r_{1}}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\|{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n,ext}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{6}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))}\Big]

\displaystyle\qquad+C\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\left(\|\rho_{n}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{\gamma}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))}\|{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n,ext}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;W^{1,r_{2}}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))}|\lambda|^{\frac{1}{t}}\|{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n,\sigma}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{6}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+C\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\left(\|\rho_{n}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{\gamma}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))}|\lambda_{1}|^{\frac{2}{3}-\frac{1}{\gamma}}\|{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}-v_{n}\boldsymbol{e}_{z}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{1}(T^{\delta}_{\eta^{*}_{n}}))}\|{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n,\sigma}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{6}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))}\right),

where we used the Sobolev embedding
W^{1,r_{1}}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})\subset W^{1-\frac{3}{2\gamma},\,\frac{6\gamma}{5\gamma-6}}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})
where
r_{1}=\frac{6\gamma}{5\gamma-3}<2
and
W^{1,r_{2}}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha})\hookrightarrow L^{q}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha})
for
q<6
,
r_{2}<2
satisfying
q=\frac{3r_{2}}{3-r_{2}}
.
To solidify our estimates, we will pick
q=\frac{12\gamma}{4\gamma-3}
and
\frac{1}{t}=\frac{1}{2}(1-\frac{3}{2\gamma})
.
Hence using (
62
) and (
63
), we obtain for some
\kappa_{2}>0
that,

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbb{E}}|I^{3}_{0}|

\displaystyle=\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\left|\int_{h}^{T}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}}\rho_{n}({\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}-{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}^{\lambda})\left({\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}^{\lambda}(t)-{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}^{\lambda}(t-h)\right)\right|

(70)

\displaystyle\leq C(\lambda^{1-\frac{3}{2\gamma}}\lambda_{1}^{-1}+(1-\sigma)^{\frac{7\gamma-9}{2\gamma}}\lambda_{1}^{-1}+\lambda^{\frac{1}{t}}\lambda_{1}^{-1}+|\lambda_{1}|^{\frac{2}{3}-\frac{1}{\gamma}})\leq Ch^{\kappa_{2}}.

Next we will consider the term
I_{0}^{4}
. We recall (
69
), (
63
), and (
62
) and the Sobolev embedding
W^{1,r}\hookrightarrow H^{1-\epsilon_{1}}
for
\epsilon_{1}>0
and
r=\frac{6}{3+\epsilon_{1}}<2
. Then there exists
\kappa_{3}>0
for an appropriately small
0<\epsilon_{1}\ll\frac{1}{4}(2-\frac{3}{\gamma})

such that,

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbb{E}}|I_{0}^{4}|\leq\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{h}^{T}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}}(\rho_{n}-\rho_{n}^{\lambda})|({\boldsymbol{u}}_{n,\sigma})^{\lambda}|\left(({\boldsymbol{u}}_{n,\sigma})^{\lambda}(t)|+|({\boldsymbol{u}}_{n,\sigma})^{\lambda}(t-h)|\right)

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\|\rho_{n}-\rho_{n}^{\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;W^{-1,\gamma}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))}\Big(\||({\boldsymbol{u}}_{n,\sigma})^{\lambda}|^{2}\|_{L^{1}(0,T;W^{1,\frac{\gamma}{\gamma-1}}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))}+\|({\boldsymbol{u}}_{n,\sigma})^{\lambda}(t)({\boldsymbol{u}}_{n,\sigma})^{\lambda}(t-h)\|_{L^{1}(h,T;W^{1,\frac{\gamma}{\gamma-1}}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})}\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{\mathbb{E}}(\|\rho_{n}-\rho_{n}^{\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;W^{-1,\gamma}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))}\|({\boldsymbol{u}}_{n,\sigma})^{\lambda}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;W^{1,\frac{6\gamma}{5\gamma-6}}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))}\|({\boldsymbol{u}}_{n,\sigma})^{\lambda}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{6}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))})

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{\mathbb{E}}(\lambda\|\rho_{n}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{\gamma}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))}\|({\boldsymbol{u}}_{n,\sigma})^{\lambda}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{\frac{3}{\gamma}}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))}\|{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n,\sigma}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{6}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))})

\displaystyle\leq\lambda\tilde{\mathbb{E}}(\|\rho_{n}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{\gamma}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))}\lambda^{1-{\frac{3}{\gamma}}-\epsilon_{1}}\|{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n,\sigma}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{1-\epsilon_{1}}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))}\|{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n,\sigma}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{6}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))})

\displaystyle\leq\lambda\tilde{\mathbb{E}}(\|\rho_{n}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{\gamma}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))}\lambda^{1-{\frac{3}{\gamma}}-\epsilon_{1}}\|{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n,\sigma}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;W^{1,\frac{6}{3+\epsilon_{1}}}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))}\|{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n,\sigma}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{6}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))})

\displaystyle\leq C\lambda^{2-{\frac{3}{\gamma}}-\epsilon_{1}}\lambda_{1}^{-1}\leq Ch^{\kappa_{3}}.

Similarly, the second term on the left-hand side of (
68
) can be written as

\displaystyle\int_{h}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}v_{n}(t)\partial_{t}\psi_{n}=\int_{h}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}v_{n}(v_{n}^{\lambda}(t)-v_{n}^{\lambda}(t-h))

\displaystyle=\int_{h}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}v_{n}(t)(v_{n}(t)-v_{n}(t-h))+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}v_{n}(t)(v_{n}^{\lambda}(t)-v_{n}(t)-v_{n}^{\lambda}(t-h)+v_{n}(t-h))

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\int_{h}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}(|v_{n}(t)|^{2}-|v_{n}(t-h)|^{2})+\frac{1}{2}\int_{h}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}|v_{n}(t)-v_{n}(t-h)|^{2}

\displaystyle+\int_{h}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}v_{n}(t)(v_{n}^{\lambda}(t)-v_{n}(t)-v_{n}^{\lambda}(t-h)+v_{n}(t-h))

\displaystyle=:J^{0}_{1}+J^{0}_{2}+J^{0}_{3}.

The second term
J^{0}_{2}
is the term of our interest, i.e. the left-hand side of the desired inequality (
60
).
For the third term we immediately obtain,

(71)

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbb{E}}|J^{0}_{3}|\leq\tilde{\mathbb{E}}(\|v_{n}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Gamma))}\|v_{n}^{\lambda}-v_{n}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Gamma))})\leq C\lambda\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\|v_{n}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{1}(\Gamma))}\leq C\lambda.

Moreover, the first term can be treated as follows,

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbb{E}}|J_{1}^{0}|=\left|\frac{1}{2}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{h}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}|v_{n}(t)|^{2}-|v_{n}(t-h)|^{2}\right|

\displaystyle=\left|-\frac{1}{2}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{h}\|v_{n}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}+\frac{1}{2}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{T-h}^{T}\|v_{n}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}\right|

\displaystyle\leq Ch\|v_{n}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\Gamma))}^{2}.

Now we will consider the terms on the right-hand side of (
68
) starting with
I_{1}
: Since
\lambda=h^{\theta}
, for some
\theta<\frac{1}{5}
, for any
s<\frac{1}{2}
and
m<1
we apply Theorem
8.2
and choose an
0<\epsilon_{1}\ll\frac{s}{4}
which is appropriately small to guarantee the existence of
\kappa_{4}>0
such that

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\left|\int_{h}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}(\rho_{n}{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}\otimes{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}):\nabla\int_{t-h}^{t}{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}^{\lambda}\right|

\displaystyle=\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\left|\int_{h}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}(\rho_{n}{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}\otimes{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}):\int_{t-h}^{t}\left(\nabla\left(\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{S}^{\lambda,\delta}_{\eta^{*}_{n}}}\right)^{\lambda}({\boldsymbol{u}}_{n,\sigma})^{\lambda}+\left(\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{S}^{\lambda,\delta}_{\eta^{*}_{n}}}\right)^{\lambda}\nabla({\boldsymbol{u}}_{n,\sigma})^{\lambda}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq{h}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\left[\|\sqrt{\rho_{n}}{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))}^{2}\left(\|({\boldsymbol{u}}_{n,\sigma})^{\lambda}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{\frac{5}{2}+\epsilon_{1}}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))}\|(\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{S}^{\lambda,\delta}_{\eta^{*}_{n}}})^{\lambda}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{\frac{5}{2}+\epsilon_{1}}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq{h}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}(\|\sqrt{\rho_{n}}{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))}^{2}\lambda^{-\frac{5}{2}-\epsilon_{1}}\|{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n,\sigma}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))}^{2}\lambda^{s-\frac{5}{2}-\epsilon_{1}}\|\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{S}^{\lambda,\delta}_{\eta^{*}_{n}}}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{s}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))})

\displaystyle\leq Ch^{\kappa_{4}}.

The terms
I_{2,3,4,5}
are treated identically.
Next, recall that
\left(\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{S}^{\lambda,\delta}_{\eta^{*}_{n}}}\right)^{\lambda}=1
in
T^{\delta}_{\eta^{*}_{n}}
. Moreover, since we have enforced (
66
), the bounds (
65
) imply for the penalty term that

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbb{E}}|I_{6}|

\displaystyle=\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\left|\frac{1}{\delta}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{{T^{\delta}_{\eta^{*}_{n}}}}({\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}-v_{n}\boldsymbol{e}_{z})\cdot\int_{t-h}^{t}((\boldsymbol{u}_{n,\sigma})^{\lambda}-v^{\lambda}_{n}\boldsymbol{e}_{z})\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{h}{\delta}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}(\|{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}-v_{n}{\bf e}_{z}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(T^{\delta}_{\eta^{*}_{n}}))}\|({\boldsymbol{u}}_{n,\sigma})^{\lambda}-v_{n}^{\lambda}{\bf e}_{z}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(T^{\delta}_{\eta^{*}_{n}}))})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{h}{\delta}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\|{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}-v_{n}{\bf e}_{z}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(T^{\delta}_{\eta^{*}_{n}}))}^{2}\leq Ch,

where we emphasize that, thanks to Lemma
6.1
(8), the constant
C>0
is independent of
\delta
. This is the reason behind choosing a non-trivial fluid test function.

Next, we see for
I_{8}
that

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbb{E}}|I_{8}|=\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\left|\int_{h}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\Delta\eta_{n}\left(\int_{t-h}^{t}\Delta v^{\lambda}_{n}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq Ch\tilde{\mathbb{E}}(\|\eta_{n}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{2}(\Gamma))}\|v^{\lambda}_{n}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{2}(\Gamma))})

\displaystyle\leq Ch\lambda^{-2}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}(\|\eta_{n}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{2}(\Gamma))}\|v_{n}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\Gamma))})

\displaystyle\leq Ch^{\kappa_{5}}.

Finally, for the stochastic integrals we find that by (
8
),

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\left|\int_{h}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}G_{n}(\eta_{n},v_{n})\psi_{n}dW_{n}^{2}\right|\leq Ch\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\left|\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{T}\left(\int_{\Gamma}|P_{n}^{st}g_{k}(\eta_{n},v_{n})|^{2}\right)\left(\sup_{t-h<s<t}\int_{\Gamma}|v_{n}(s)|^{2}\right)dt\right|^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq Ch\left(1+\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\|v_{n}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\Gamma))}^{2}+\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\|\eta_{n}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\Gamma))}\right)

\displaystyle\leq Ch.

The other stochastic integral is treated identically. Thus combining all the estimates we obtain (
60
) for
\bar{\kappa}=\min_{i}\{\kappa_{i}\}
.
∎

These uniform bounds give us the existence of a subsequence of the Galerkin solutions that converges in weak/weak-* topologies of the corresponding energy spaces.
Our next goal is to prove that a subsequence of the solutions to the Galerkin approximations (
55
) is tight in an appropriate phase space which is a subspace of the energy space. This will aid us in establishing the necessary almost sure convergence of the approximate solutions in the phase space.

6.1.
Tightness of laws

We begin by defining the appropriate spaces:

\displaystyle\mathcal{X}_{\rho}=C_{w}(0,T;L^{\beta}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}))\cap L^{4}(0,T;L^{4}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}))\cap\left(L^{2}(0,T;H^{1}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}))\cap{{L^{\beta+1}((0,T)\times{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}),w}}\right).

\displaystyle\mathcal{X}_{{\boldsymbol{u}}}=(L^{2}(0,T;H_{0}^{1}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})),w),\qquad\mathcal{X}_{\rho{\boldsymbol{u}}}=C_{w}(0,T;L^{\frac{2\beta}{\beta+1}}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}))\cap C([0,T];H^{-l}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))\quad\text{for }l>\frac{5}{2},

\displaystyle\mathcal{X}_{\eta}=C_{w}(0,T;H^{2}(\Gamma))\cap C([0,T];H^{s}(\Gamma)),\text{ for any }s<2,

\displaystyle\mathcal{X}_{v}=L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Gamma))\cap(L^{2}(0,T;H^{1}(\Gamma)),w),\quad\mathcal{X}_{W}=C(0,T;\mathcal{U}_{0})^{2}.

where
w
denotes weak topology.
The aim of this section is to prove the following proposition.

Proposition 6.1
.

Define the family of random variables

\mathcal{U}_{n}:=(\rho_{n},{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n},P^{f}_{n}(\rho_{n}{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}),\eta_{n},\eta_{n}^{*},v_{n},W^{1}_{n},W^{2}_{n}).

Then the sequence of measures
\{\tilde{{\mathbb{P}}}\circ\mathcal{U}^{-1}_{n}\}_{n\geq 1}
is tight in the phase space

\mathcal{X}=\mathcal{X}_{\rho}\times\mathcal{X}_{{\boldsymbol{u}}}\times\mathcal{X}_{\rho{\boldsymbol{u}}}\times\mathcal{X}_{\eta}\times\mathcal{X}_{\eta}\times\mathcal{X}_{v}\times\mathcal{X}_{W}.

Proof.

We prove this proposition by proving tightness of the laws of
\rho_{n},P_{n}(\rho_{n}{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}),{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n},v_{n},\eta_{n},\eta^{*}_{n}
individually in the corresponding phase spaces. Tychonoff’s theorem will then imply tightness of the joint laws of these random variables.

•
Tightness of
{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}
.

We begin by defining the following set which is relatively compact in
\mathcal{X}_{\boldsymbol{u}}
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{M}:=

\displaystyle\{{\boldsymbol{u}}\in L^{2}(0,T;H_{0}^{1}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha})):\|{\boldsymbol{u}}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;H_{0}^{1}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))}\leq M\}.

Observe that the uniform bounds in Lemma
6.1
(1) and the Chebyshev inequality give us, for any
M>0
, that

\displaystyle\tilde{{\mathbb{P}}}[\mathcal{K}_{M}^{c}]\leq\frac{1}{M^{2}}\mathbb{E}[\|{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{1}_{0}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))}]\leq\frac{C}{M^{2}}.

This proves the desired result of tightness of the laws of the approximate fluid velocity
{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}
. The tightness of laws of
v_{n}
in
(L^{2}(0,T;H^{1}(\Gamma)),w)
follows identically.

•
Tightness of laws of
\eta_{n}
and
\eta_{n}^{*}
.
To prove this result we recall the uniform bounds for
\tilde{\eta}_{n}
and
\tilde{\eta}_{n}^{*}
derived in Lemma
6.1
(6,7). Now, the Aubin-Lions theorem states that the set

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}_{M}:=

\displaystyle\{\eta\in L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{2}(\Gamma))\cap W^{1,\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\Gamma)):\|\eta\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{2}(\Gamma))}+\|\eta\|^{2}_{W^{1,\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\Gamma))}\leq M\},

is relatively compact in
C([0,T];H^{s}(\Gamma))
, for any
0<s<2
. Using the Chebyshev inequality, we obtain for some
C>0
independent of
n
that the following holds for
\eta_{n}
(and similarly for
\eta^{*}_{n}
):

(72)

\displaystyle\tilde{{\mathbb{P}}}\left[\eta_{n}\not\in{\mathcal{B}}_{M}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{{\mathbb{P}}}\left[{\|\eta_{n}\|}^{2}_{L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{2}(\Gamma))}\geq\frac{M}{2}\right]+\tilde{{\mathbb{P}}}\left[{\|\eta_{n}\|}^{2}_{W^{1,\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\Gamma))}\geq\frac{M}{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{4}{M}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\left[{\|\eta_{n}\|}^{2}_{L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{2}(\Gamma))}+\|\eta_{n}\|^{2}_{W^{1,\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\Gamma))}\right]\leq\frac{C}{M}.

•
Tightness of laws of
\rho_{n}
.

Since, at this stage, we are working on the fixed maximal domain
{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}
, this part of the proof follows closely the strategy in Proposition 4.2 in
[
8
]
applied toward obtaining a similar tightness result and in
[
20
]
for establishing compactness in the deterministic setting. Hence, in this section, we will summarize the technique briefly.
Recall that
\rho_{n}
satisfies the bounds (
59
). Thus the compact embedding

L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{\beta}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}))\cap C^{0,1}(0,T;W^{-2,\frac{2\beta}{\beta+1}}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}))\subset\subset C_{w}(0,T;L^{\beta}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})),

immediately gives us that the measures
\tilde{{\mathbb{P}}}\circ\rho^{-1}_{n}
are tight in
C_{w}(0,T;L^{\beta}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))
.

Observe that, since
\beta>3
we have that
4<\frac{12\beta}{\beta+6}
, and thus that
W^{\frac{1}{2},\frac{4\beta}{\beta+2}}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha})
is compactly embedded in
L^{4}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha})
. Hence, the Aubin-Lions theorem gives us that,

\displaystyle L^{4}(0,T;W^{\frac{1}{2},\frac{4\beta}{\beta+2}}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))\cap W^{1,\infty}(0,T;W^{-2,\frac{2\beta}{\beta+1}}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))\subset\subset L^{4}((0,T)\times{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}).

Hence our next aim is to obtain uniform boundedness of
\rho_{n}
in
L^{p}(\Omega;L^{4}(0,T;W^{\frac{1}{2},\frac{4\beta}{\beta+2}}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))
.
First, observe that, due to the Brezis-Mironescu interpolation inequality, we have,

\displaystyle\|\rho_{n}\|^{2}_{W^{\frac{1}{2},\frac{4\beta}{\beta+2}}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha})}\leq\|\rho_{n}\|_{L^{\beta}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha})}\|\rho_{n}\|_{W^{1,2}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha})}.

This implies that,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\|\rho_{n}\|^{4}_{W^{\frac{1}{2},\frac{4\beta}{\beta+2}}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha})}\leq\int_{0}^{T}\|\rho_{n}\|^{2}_{L^{\beta}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha})}\|\rho_{n}\|^{2}_{W^{1,2}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha})}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\|\rho_{n}\|^{2}_{L^{\beta}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha})}\int_{0}^{T}\|\rho_{n}\|^{2}_{W^{1,2}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha})},

thus establishing, for some constant
C>0
, that

(73)

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\|\rho_{n}\|^{4}_{W^{\frac{1}{2},\frac{4\beta}{\beta+2}}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha})}\right)^{p}\leq\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\|\rho_{n}\|^{4p}_{L^{\beta}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha})}+\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\|\rho_{n}\|^{2}_{H^{1}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha})}\right)^{2p}\leq C(\varepsilon,\delta).

As earlier, an application of the Chebyshev inequality concludes the proof of tightness of the sequence
\{\tilde{{\mathbb{P}}}\circ\rho^{-1}_{n}\}
in
L^{4}((0,T)\times{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha})
.

Finally, since,
\beta\geq 3
, interpolation and Lemma
6.1
(3),(4) then give us,

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\|\rho_{n}\|_{L^{\beta+1}((0,T)\times{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha})}

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\|\rho^{\beta}_{n}\|_{L^{\frac{4}{3}}(0,T;L^{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))}\leq\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\left(\|\rho^{\beta}_{n}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{1}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))}^{\frac{1}{4}}\|\rho^{\beta}_{n}\|_{L^{1}(0,T;L^{3}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))}^{\frac{3}{4}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\left(\|\rho^{\beta}_{n}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{1}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))}^{\frac{1}{4}}\|\rho^{\frac{\beta}{2}}_{n}\|_{L^{1}(0,T;L^{6}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))}^{\frac{3}{4}}\right)\leq C,

concluding the tightness of the sequence
\{\tilde{{\mathbb{P}}}\circ\rho^{-1}_{n}\}
in
\mathcal{X}_{\rho}.

•
Tightness of laws of
P^{f}_{n}(\rho_{n}{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n})
.

For this part of the proof we will utilize the following compact embedding for any
r>0
and
l>\frac{5}{2}
:

(74)

\displaystyle L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{\frac{2\beta}{\beta+1}}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}))\cap C^{r}(0,T;H^{-l}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}))\subset\subset C_{w}(0,T;L^{\frac{2\beta}{\beta+1}}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})).

Recall that we already have, thanks to (
58
), that

\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\|P_{n}^{f}(\rho_{n}{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n})\|^{p}_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{\frac{2\beta}{\beta+1}}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}))}\leq C_{p},

for any
p\geq 1
.
Next we will show that for any
l>\frac{5}{2}
and any
0\leq r<\frac{1}{2}
, there exists some
C>0
independent of
n
, such that

(75)

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\|P_{n}^{f}(\rho_{n}{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n})\|_{C^{r}(0,T;H^{-l}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}))}\leq C.

To obtain the aforementioned uniform bounds we consider (
55
) with
\psi=0
and write:

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}P_{n}^{f}(\rho_{n}(t){\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}(t))\cdot\boldsymbol{q}=\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}P_{n}^{f}(\boldsymbol{p}_{0,\delta,\varepsilon})\cdot\boldsymbol{q}

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}P_{n}^{f}(\rho_{n}{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}\otimes{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}):\nabla\boldsymbol{q}+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}{P_{n}^{f}}\Big(a\rho_{n}^{\gamma}+\delta\rho_{n}^{\beta}\Big)(\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{q})-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}{P_{n}^{f}\left(\mu^{\eta^{*}_{n}}_{\delta}\nabla{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}\right)}:\nabla\boldsymbol{q}

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}{P_{n}^{f}\left(\lambda_{\delta}^{\eta^{*}_{n}}\text{div}({\boldsymbol{u}}_{n})\right)}\text{div}(\boldsymbol{q})+\varepsilon\int_{0}^{t}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}}P_{n}^{f}({\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}\rho_{n})\cdot\Delta{\boldsymbol{q}}-\frac{1}{\delta}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}}{P_{n}^{f}(\mathbbm{1}_{T^{\delta}_{\eta^{*}_{n}}}({\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}-v_{n}\boldsymbol{e}_{z}))}\cdot\boldsymbol{q}

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}{P_{n}^{f}(\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\eta_{n}^{*}}}\boldsymbol{F}_{n}(\rho_{n},\rho_{n}{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}))}\cdot\boldsymbol{q}dW_{n}^{1}(t)

\displaystyle:=I_{0}+...+I_{7},

for any
{\boldsymbol{q}}\in H^{l}_{0}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha})
.
We will take
\sup_{{\boldsymbol{q}}\in H^{l}_{0}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha})}
,
l>\frac{5}{2}
on both sides and analyze each term appearing in this weak form individually.

We start with the first term
\displaystyle I_{1}:=\int_{0}^{t}\sup_{{\boldsymbol{q}}\in H^{l}_{0}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha})}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}}P_{n}^{f}(\rho_{n}{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}\otimes{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}):\nabla{\boldsymbol{q}}
.
For any
p\geq 1
, we obtain

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\|\partial_{t}I_{1}\|^{p}_{L^{2}(0,T)}\leq C(T)\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\left(\|\rho_{n}{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{\frac{2\beta}{\beta+1}}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))}\|{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;H_{0}^{1}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))}\right)^{p}\leq C.

For the next term,
I_{2}=\int_{0}^{t}\sup_{{\boldsymbol{q}}\in H^{l}_{0}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha})}|\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}}P_{n}^{f}(a\rho^{\gamma}_{n}+\delta\rho^{\beta}_{n})\nabla\cdot{\boldsymbol{q}}|,

we choose
l>\frac{5}{2}
to obtain the following estimate

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\|\partial_{t}I_{2}\|_{L^{2}(0,T)}\leq\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\|\rho_{n}^{\beta}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{1}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))}<C.

Next, we have
I_{3}=\int_{0}^{t}\sup_{{\boldsymbol{q}}\in H^{l}_{0}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha})}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}}|P_{n}^{f}(\mu_{\delta}^{\eta^{*}_{n}}\nabla{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}):\nabla{\boldsymbol{q}}|
. Then Lemma
6.1

readily gives us that

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\|\partial_{t}I_{3}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(0,T)}\leq\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\|{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{1}_{0}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))}<C.

The term
I_{4}
is treated identically.
Now, for
I_{5}=\varepsilon\int_{0}^{t}\sup_{{\boldsymbol{q}}\in H^{l}_{0}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha})}|\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}}P_{n}^{f}({\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}\rho_{n})\cdot\Delta{\boldsymbol{q}}|
we write,

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}I_{5}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(0,T)}\leq\int_{0}^{T}\|{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}\rho_{n}\|^{2}_{L^{\frac{3}{2}}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha})}\leq\int_{0}^{T}\|{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}\|_{L^{6}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha})}^{2}\|\rho_{n}\|_{L^{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha})}.

Hence, we obtain,

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbb{E}}[\|\partial_{t}I_{5}\|_{L^{2}(0,T)}]\leq\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\|{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{1}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))}+\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\|\rho_{n}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))}\leq C.

Next, we find bounds for the term
I_{6}=\frac{1}{\delta}\int_{0}^{t}\sup_{{\boldsymbol{q}}\in H^{l}_{0}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha})}|\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}}P_{n}^{f}(\mathbbm{1}_{T^{\delta}_{\eta^{*}_{n}}}({\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}-v_{n}\boldsymbol{e}_{z}))\cdot\boldsymbol{q}|
. Observe that, using the trace theorem we obtain

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbb{E}}[\|\partial_{t}I_{6}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(0,T)}]\leq\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{T}(\|{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}\|_{L^{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha})}+\|v_{n}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma)})^{2}\leq C(\delta).

We summarize our bounds so far as follows,

(76)

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\|I_{0}+...+I_{6}\|_{C^{\frac{1}{2}}([0,T];H^{-l}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))}\leq C\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\|I_{0}+...+I_{6}\|_{H^{1}(0,T;H^{-l}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))}\leq C.

Finally, we use the Burkholder-Davis-Gundy inequality to find for the stochastic term
I_{7}
the following bounds for any
t_{1}<t_{2}
,

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\left\|\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}P_{n}^{f}\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\eta_{n}^{*}}}\boldsymbol{F}_{n}(\rho_{n},\rho_{n}{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n})dW_{n}^{1}\right\|_{H^{-l}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha})}^{p}

\displaystyle\leq C\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\|f_{n,k}(\rho_{n},\rho_{n}{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n})\|^{2}_{H^{-1}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha})}\right)^{\frac{p}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\left(\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}}\rho_{n}+\rho_{n}|{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}|^{2}\right)^{\frac{p}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C|t_{2}-t_{1}|^{{\frac{p}{2}}}\left(\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\|\rho_{n}\|^{{\frac{p}{2}}}_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{\gamma}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))}+\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\|\sqrt{\rho_{n}}{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n}\|^{p}_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))}\right).

Then the Kolmogorov continuity theorem gives us that for any
r<\frac{1}{2}

(77)

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\left\|\int_{0}^{t}\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\eta_{n}^{*}}}\boldsymbol{F}_{n}(\rho_{n},\rho_{n}{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n})dW_{n}^{1}\right\|_{C^{r}([0,T];H^{-l}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))}<C.

Hence, combining (
76
) and (
77
) we obtain (
75
).
A final application of the embedding (
74
) and Chebyshev’s inequality then gives us that
\{\tilde{{\mathbb{P}}}\circ(\rho_{n}{\boldsymbol{u}}_{n})^{-1}\}
is tight in
C_{w}(0,T;L^{\frac{2\beta}{\beta+1}}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))
.

•
Tightness for
v_{n}
.

We will use the following variant of the Aubin-Lions theorem (see e.g.
[
38
]
,
[
43
]
) that states:
Assume that
\mathcal{Y}_{0}\subset\mathcal{Y}
are Banach spaces such that
\mathcal{Y}_{0}
and
\mathcal{Y}
are reflexive with compact embedding of
\mathcal{Y}_{0}
in
\mathcal{Y}
,
then for any
\sigma>0
, the embedding

(78)

\displaystyle\left\{v\in L^{2}(0,T;\mathcal{Y}_{0}):\sup_{0<h<T}\frac{1}{h^{\sigma}}\|T_{h}v-v\|_{L^{2}(h,T;\mathcal{Y})}<\infty\right\}\hookrightarrow_{c}L^{2}(0,T;\mathcal{Y}),

is compact. We take
\mathcal{Y}_{0}=H^{1}(\Gamma)
and
\mathcal{Y}=L^{2}(\Gamma)
, use the bounds obtained in Lemma
6.1
(6) and Lemma
6.2
, and with the aid of Chebyshev’s inequality, as earlier, we infer that the sequence of measures
\tilde{{\mathbb{P}}}\circ v_{n}^{-1}
is tight in
L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Gamma)).
This completes the proof of Proposition
6.1
.
∎

6.2.
Almost sure convergence and identification of the limit

Thanks to the tightness result obtained in Section
6.1
and Jakubowski’s version of the Skorohod representation theorem, we obtain the following convergence result.

Theorem 6.1
.

There exists a probability space
(\tilde{\Omega},\tilde{\mathcal{F}},\tilde{\mathbb{P}})
and random variables

\displaystyle\bar{\mathcal{U}}_{n}:=(\bar{\rho}_{n},\bar{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{n},\overline{(\rho{\boldsymbol{u}})}_{n},\bar{\eta}_{n},\bar{\eta}^{*}_{n},\bar{v}_{n},\bar{W}^{1}_{n},\bar{W}^{2}_{n})
for
n\in\mathbb{N}
and

{\mathcal{U}}:=(\rho,{{\boldsymbol{u}}},\overline{\rho{\boldsymbol{u}}},\eta,\eta^{*},v,\bar{W}^{1},\bar{W}^{2})

defined on this new probability space, such that

(1)

\bar{\mathcal{U}}_{n}
has the same law in
\mathcal{X}
as
\mathcal{U}_{n}
,

(2)

\bar{\mathcal{U}}_{n}\to\mathcal{U}_{n}
almost surely in
\mathcal{X}
.

(3)

\bar{\eta}^{*}_{n}=\bar{\eta}_{n}
for every
t<\tau^{\eta}_{n}
where, for the fixed
s\in(\frac{3}{2},2)
,

\displaystyle\tau^{\eta}_{n}
