\displaystyle\leq C\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\left(\|\mu^{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}_{\delta}\nabla\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{\delta}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))}\|\hat{\rho}_{\delta}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{\gamma}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))}^{{\Theta}}\right)\leq C.

The term
\int_{0}^{t}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}}\lambda^{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}_{\delta}\text{div}\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{\delta}(\chi_{\delta}\hat{\rho}^{\Theta}_{\delta}+\nabla\chi_{\delta}\cdot\nabla\Delta^{-1}\hat{\rho}_{\delta}^{\Theta})
is treated identically.

• Next, using (
119
), we have

\displaystyle\Bigg|\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}}(a\hat{\rho}_{\delta}^{\gamma}+\delta\hat{\rho}_{\delta}^{\beta})\nabla\chi_{\delta}\cdot\nabla\Delta^{-1}\hat{\rho}^{\Theta}_{\delta}\Bigg|

\displaystyle\leq C\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left(\|\hat{\rho}_{\delta}\|^{\gamma}_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{\gamma}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))}+\delta\|\hat{\rho}_{\delta}\|^{\beta}_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{\beta}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))}\right)\|\chi_{\delta}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;W^{1,\infty}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))}\|\nabla\Delta^{-1}\hat{\rho}_{\delta}^{\Theta}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{\infty}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))}\right]

\displaystyle\leq C\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left(\|\hat{\rho}_{\delta}\|^{\gamma}_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{\gamma}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))}+\delta\|\hat{\rho}_{\delta}\|^{\beta}_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{\beta}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))}\right)\|\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{2}(\Gamma))}\|\hat{\rho}_{\delta}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{\gamma}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))}^{{\Theta}}\right]\leq C(l,\alpha),

for any
\Theta<\frac{\gamma}{3}
, by the continuous embedding of
W^{1,\gamma/\Theta}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})\subset L^{\infty}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})
.

• The last term is treated using inequality (
120
) for any
\Theta<\frac{\gamma}{3}
, as follows:

\displaystyle\Bigg|\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}}{\partial_{t}\chi_{\delta}\hat{\rho}_{\delta}}\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{\delta}\cdot\nabla\Delta^{-1}\hat{\rho}^{\Theta}_{\delta}\Bigg|

\displaystyle\leq C\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\left({{\|\partial_{t}\chi_{\delta}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{\infty}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))}}}\|\hat{\rho}_{\delta}\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{\delta}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{\frac{2\gamma}{\gamma+1}}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))}\|\nabla\Delta^{-1}\hat{\rho}_{\delta}^{\Theta}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{\infty}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\left(\|\partial_{t}\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{1}(\Gamma))}\|\hat{\rho}_{\delta}\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{\delta}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{\frac{2\gamma}{\gamma+1}}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))}\|\hat{\rho}_{\delta}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{\gamma}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))}^{\Theta}\right)\leq C.

Finally, we note that, since the stochastic integral is an
(\hat{\mathcal{F}}^{\delta}_{t})_{t\in[0,T]}
-martingale, its expectation is zero. This concludes the proof of Lemma
8.3
.
∎

Next, we will need an estimate on the boundary, since we need to avoid the boundary for the interior estimates due to the badly behaved
1/\delta
penalty term and low spatial regularity of the structure displacements.

Proposition 8.4

(Boundary estimate for the pressure)
.

For any
\epsilon>0
, there exists an
l>0
and a
\delta_{0}
sufficiently small so that for all
\delta\in(0,\delta_{0}]
,

\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}\cap(A^{l}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}})^{c}}(a\hat{\rho}^{\gamma}_{\delta}+\delta\hat{\rho}^{\beta}_{\delta})\leq\epsilon.

Proof.

For any parameter
K>0
, we define:

\phi^{K}_{\delta}=-[\min\left(K(1+\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}(t,x,y)-z),1\right)]^{+}\boldsymbol{e}_{z},

and we will use
(\boldsymbol{q},\psi)=(\phi^{K}_{\delta},0)
as test functions in the weak formulation (
109
) which is justified thanks to Lemma 5.1 in
[
12
]
. Note that since

{\nabla\cdot\phi^{K}_{\delta}=K}\ \text{ on }\ B^{\frac{1}{K}}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}(\omega):=\{(x,y,z)\in\mathbb{R}^{3}:(x,y)\in\Gamma,\ 0<(1+\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}(t,x,y))-z<l\},

we have that

K\int_{0}^{T}\int_{B^{\frac{1}{K}}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}(\omega)}(a\hat{\rho}^{\gamma}_{\delta}+\delta\hat{\rho}^{\beta}_{\delta})=|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}}(a\hat{\rho}_{\delta}^{\gamma}+\delta\hat{\rho}^{\beta}_{\delta})(\nabla\cdot\phi^{K}_{\delta})|\\
=|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}\hat{\rho}_{\delta}\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{\delta}\partial_{t}\phi^{K}_{\delta}+\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}\hat{\rho}_{\delta}(t)\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{\delta}(t)\cdot\phi^{K}_{\delta}(t)-\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}\boldsymbol{p}_{0,\delta}\cdot\phi^{K}_{\delta}(0)\\
-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta}\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{\delta}\otimes\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{\delta}):\nabla\phi^{K}_{\delta}+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}\mu_{\delta}^{\hat{\eta}_{\delta}^{*}}\nabla\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{\delta}:\nabla\phi^{K}_{\delta}+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}\lambda^{\hat{\eta}_{\delta}^{*}}_{\delta}\text{div}(\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{\delta})\text{div}(\phi^{K}_{\delta})\\
+{\frac{1}{\delta}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{T^{\delta}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}}(\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{\delta}-\hat{v}_{\delta}\boldsymbol{e}_{z})\cdot(\phi_{\delta}^{K})}+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}\boldsymbol{F}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta},\hat{\rho}_{\delta}\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{\delta})\cdot\phi^{K}_{\delta}d\hat{W}^{1}_{\delta}(t)|.

Observe that by construction,
\phi^{K}_{\delta}|_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}\setminus{\mathcal{O}}_{{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}}=0
so the penalty term vanishes.
We estimate the terms on the right-hand side, based on the fact that the bounds in Lemma
8.1
hold for the new random variables
\hat{\mathcal{U}}_{\delta}
found in Theorem
8.1
as well, as follows:

• Outside of
B^{\frac{1}{K}}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}
, we have that
|\nabla\cdot\phi^{K}_{\delta}|\leq C
, and hence,
we obtain the estimate:

\left|\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}\cap A^{\frac{1}{K}}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}(\tilde{\omega})^{c}}(a\hat{\rho}^{\gamma}_{\delta}+\delta\hat{\rho}^{\beta}_{\delta})(\nabla\cdot\phi^{K}_{\delta})\right|\leq C.

• Note that
|\partial_{t}\phi^{K}_{\delta}|\leq K|\partial_{t}\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}|
. So since

\partial_{t}\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}\in L^{2}(\tilde{\Omega};L^{2}(0,T;H_{0}^{1}(\Gamma)))\subset L^{2}(\tilde{\Omega};L^{2}(0,T;L^{p}(\Gamma))),\quad\text{ for all $1<p<\infty$},

we have that
\partial_{t}\phi^{K}_{\delta}\in L^{2}(\tilde{\Omega};L^{2}(0,T;L^{p}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})))
, for all
1<p<\infty
, with

\|\partial_{t}\phi^{K}_{\delta}\|_{L^{2}(\tilde{\Omega};L^{2}(0,T;L^{p}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})))}\leq CK.

Since
\hat{\rho}_{\delta}\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{\delta}
is uniformly bounded in
L^{2}(\tilde{\Omega};L^{2}(0,T;L^{\frac{2\gamma}{\gamma+1}}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})))
and because
\partial_{t}\phi^{K}_{\delta}
is zero outside of
B^{\frac{1}{K}}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}
, we conclude that

\left|\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}\hat{\rho}_{\delta}\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{\delta}\partial_{t}\phi^{K}_{\delta}\right|=\left|\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{B^{\frac{1}{K}}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}(\omega)}\hat{\rho}_{\delta}\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{\delta}\partial_{t}\phi^{K}_{\delta}\right|\\
\leq\|\hat{\rho}_{\delta}\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{\delta}\|_{L^{2}(\tilde{\Omega};L^{2}(0,T;L^{\frac{2\gamma}{\gamma+1}}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})))}\|1_{B^{\frac{1}{K}}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}(\omega)}\|_{L^{\infty}(\tilde{\Omega};L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{\frac{4\gamma}{\gamma-1}}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})))}\|\partial_{t}\phi^{K}_{\delta}\|_{L^{2}(\tilde{\Omega};L^{2}(0,T;L^{\frac{4\gamma}{\gamma-1}}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})))}\\
\leq CK^{1-\frac{\gamma-1}{4\gamma}}.

• To estimate the terms
\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\left|\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}\hat{\rho}_{\delta}(t)\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{\delta}(t)\cdot\phi^{K}_{\delta}(t)\right|
and
\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\left|\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}\boldsymbol{p}_{0,\delta}\cdot\phi^{K}_{\delta}(0)\right|
, we use the fact that
\hat{\rho}_{\delta}\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{\delta}\in C_{w}(0,T;L^{\frac{2\gamma}{\gamma+1}}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}))

\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost surely so that
\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\|\hat{\rho}_{\delta}\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{\delta}\|^{p}_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{\frac{2\gamma}{2\gamma+1}}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))}\leq C
,
and the fact that
|\phi_{K}(t,\cdot)|\leq 1
for all
t\in[0,T]
. Hence, we have the immediate estimate that these terms are bounded by a uniform constant
C
.

• By definition we have

(121)

|\nabla\phi^{K}_{\delta}|\leq CK(1+|\nabla\hat{\eta}_{\delta}^{*}|),\qquad\text{ on $B^{\frac{1}{K}}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}$.}

and then we use the fact that
\nabla\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}\in L^{2}(\tilde{\Omega};L^{\infty}(0,T;H_{0}^{1}(\Gamma)))\subset L^{2}(\tilde{\Omega};L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{p}(\Gamma)))
for all
1\leq p<\infty
along with Lemma
8.1
(8) for any
q<6
to conclude that:

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\left|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{B^{\frac{1}{K}}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta}\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{\delta}\otimes\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{\delta})\cdot\nabla\phi^{K}_{\delta}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\|\hat{\rho}_{\delta}\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{\delta}\otimes\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{\delta}\|_{L^{2}(\tilde{\Omega};L^{1}(0,T;L^{\frac{q\gamma}{q+2\gamma}}(B^{\frac{1}{K}}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}))}

\displaystyle\cdot

\displaystyle\|1_{B^{\frac{1}{K}}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}\|_{L^{\infty}(\tilde{\Omega};L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{\frac{2q\gamma}{q(\gamma-1)-2\gamma}}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})))}\cdot\|\nabla\phi^{K}_{\delta}\|_{L^{2}(\tilde{\Omega};L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{\frac{2q\gamma}{q(\gamma-1)-2\gamma}}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})))}

(122)

\displaystyle\leq{{CK^{1-{\frac{q(\gamma-1)-2\gamma}{2q\gamma}}}}}.

• For the next term, we do a similar estimate using (
121
), noting that

\mu_{\delta}^{\hat{\eta}_{\delta}^{*}}\nabla\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{\delta}
is uniformly bounded in
L^{2}(\tilde{\Omega};L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})))
. Hence,

\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\left|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{B^{\frac{1}{K}}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}(\omega)}\mu_{\delta}^{\hat{\eta}_{\delta}^{*}}\nabla\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{\delta}:\nabla\phi^{K}_{\delta}\right|\leq C\|{\mu_{\delta}^{\hat{\eta}_{\delta}^{*}}}\nabla\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{\delta}\|_{L^{2}(\tilde{\Omega};L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})))}\\
\cdot\|1_{B^{\frac{1}{K}}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}\|_{L^{\infty}(\tilde{\Omega};L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{4}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})))}\cdot\|\nabla\phi^{K}_{\delta}\|_{L^{2}(\tilde{\Omega};L^{2}(0,T;L^{4}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})))}\leq{{CK^{3/4}}}.

We then have a similar estimate for the other viscosity term:

\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\left|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}\lambda^{\hat{\eta}_{\delta}^{*}}_{\delta}\text{div}(\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{\delta})\text{div}(\phi^{K}_{\delta})\right|\leq{{CK^{3/4}}}.

• Finally, the expectation of the stochastic integral term is zero.
Putting together all of these estimates, we have for any
q<6
, that

K\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{B^{\frac{1}{K}}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}(a\hat{\rho}_{\delta}^{\gamma}+\delta\hat{\rho}_{\delta}^{\beta})\leq C(K^{1-\frac{\gamma-1}{4\gamma}}+K^{1-{\frac{q(\gamma-1)-2\gamma}{2q\gamma}}}+K^{3/4}).

So given
\epsilon>0
, there exists
K
sufficiently large such that

(123)

\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{B^{\frac{1}{K}}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}(a\hat{\rho}^{\gamma}_{\delta}+\delta\hat{\rho}^{\beta}_{\delta})\leq\epsilon.

The same estimate near the bottom boundary is obtained using similar arguments on the set
\Gamma\times(0,l)
which, combined with (
123
), gives us the desired result.
∎

The two propositions above give us equi-integrability of the pressure in the moving domain.
Next we will bound the pressure in the small tubular neighborhood
T^{\delta}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}
of
\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}
.

Proposition 8.5
.

For some
\nu_{1}>0
there exists a constant
C
independent of
\delta
, such that

(124)

\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{T^{\delta}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}(a\hat{\rho}^{\gamma}_{\delta}+\delta\hat{\rho}^{\beta}_{\delta})\leq C\delta^{\nu_{1}}.

Thus,

(125)

\mathbbm{1}_{T^{\delta}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}(a\hat{\rho}^{\gamma}_{\delta}+\delta\hat{\rho}^{\beta}_{\delta})\to 0\qquad\text{ in }L^{1}(\tilde{\Omega}\times(0,T)\times{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha})).

Proof.

We define

\phi_{\delta}=\left[\min\left(\frac{1}{\delta}(z-(1+\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}(t,x,y))),1\right)\right]^{+}\boldsymbol{e}_{z}.

We will now use
(\boldsymbol{q},\psi)=(\phi_{\delta},0)
as test functions in the weak formulation (
109
) which is justified thanks to Lemma 5.1 in
[
12
]
. Note that
\nabla\cdot\phi_{\delta}=\frac{1}{\delta}
on the random set
S_{\delta}:=\{(x,y,z):0\leq z-(1+\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}(x,y))\leq\delta\}
(which contains
T^{\delta}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}
) and 0 otherwise, almost surely. We hence have that

\frac{1}{\delta}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{T^{\delta}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}(a\hat{\rho}^{\gamma}_{\delta}+\delta\hat{\rho}^{\beta}_{\delta})\leq\frac{1}{\delta}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{S_{\delta}}(a\hat{\rho}^{\gamma}_{\delta}+\delta\hat{\rho}^{\beta}_{\delta})=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}}(a\hat{\rho}_{\delta}^{\gamma}+\delta\hat{\rho}^{\beta}_{\delta})(\nabla\cdot\phi_{\delta})=-\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}\boldsymbol{p}_{0,\delta}\cdot\phi_{\delta}(0)\\
+\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}\hat{\rho}_{\delta}(t)\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{\delta}(t)\cdot\phi_{\delta}(t)+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}\hat{\rho}_{\delta}\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{\delta}\partial_{t}\phi_{\delta}-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta}\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{\delta}\otimes\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{\delta}):\nabla\phi_{\delta}+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}\hat{\mu}_{\delta}^{\hat{\eta}_{\delta}^{*}}\nabla\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{\delta}:\nabla\phi_{\delta}\\
+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}\hat{\lambda}^{\hat{\eta}_{\delta}^{*}}_{\delta}\text{div}(\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{\delta})\text{div}(\phi_{\delta})+{\frac{1}{\delta}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{T^{\delta}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}}(\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{\delta}-\hat{v}_{\delta}\boldsymbol{e}_{z})\cdot\phi_{\delta}}+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}\boldsymbol{F}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta},\hat{\rho}_{\delta}\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{\delta})\cdot\phi_{\delta}d\hat{W}^{1}_{\delta}(t).

All the terms here are treated as in Proposition
8.4
. The only difference is the appearance of the penalty term for which we observe that,

\frac{1}{\delta}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{T^{\delta}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}}(\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{\delta}-\hat{v}_{\delta}\boldsymbol{e}_{z})\cdot\phi_{\delta}\leq\frac{1}{\delta}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\|\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{\delta}-\hat{v}_{\delta}\boldsymbol{e}_{z}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(T^{\delta}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}))}\|\phi_{\delta}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}))}\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{\delta}}.

∎

Now, we can combine the results of Propositions
8.3
,
8.4
, and
8.5
to obtain the main result of this section.

Proposition 8.6
.

We conclude that for a limiting pressure function
\overline{p}
:

a\hat{\rho}^{\delta}_{\delta}+\delta\hat{\rho}^{\beta}_{\delta}\rightharpoonup\overline{p}\text{ weakly in }L^{1}(\tilde{\Omega};L^{1}(0,T;L^{1}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}))).

Proof.

Observe that Propositions
8.3
and
8.4
gives us the desired equi-integrability of
\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}(\hat{\rho}^{\gamma}_{\delta}+\delta\hat{\rho}_{\delta}^{\beta})
in
L^{1}(\tilde{\Omega}\times(0,T)\times{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha})
. Indeed, for any
\epsilon>0
there exists a
\Theta>0
and an
l>0
such that the pressure estimates, near the moving boundary, found in Proposition
8.4
hold. Then we take any measurable set
E\subset\tilde{\Omega}\times[0,T]\times{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}
such that
|E|<(\frac{1}{C_{l}}\epsilon)^{r_{\Theta}}
where
C_{l}
is the deterministic constant appearing in Proposition
8.3
and
1<r_{\Theta}<\infty
is an appropriately chosen power that appears in the following calculations due to the Hölder inequality. We then obtain,

\displaystyle\int_{E}\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}|a\hat{\rho}^{\gamma}_{\delta}+\delta\hat{\rho}^{\beta}_{\delta}|

\displaystyle=\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}(t)\cap(A^{l}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}(t))^{c}}\mathbbm{1}_{E}|a\hat{\rho}^{\gamma}_{\delta}+\delta\hat{\rho}^{\beta}_{\delta}|+\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{A^{l}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}(t)}\mathbbm{1}_{E}|a\hat{\rho}^{\gamma}_{\delta}+\delta\hat{\rho}^{\beta}_{\delta}|

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon+\left(\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{A^{l}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}(t)}\mathbbm{1}_{E}|a\hat{\rho}^{\gamma+\Theta}_{\delta}+\delta\hat{\rho}^{\beta+\Theta}_{\delta}|\right)^{1-\frac{1}{r_{\Theta}}}|E|^{\frac{1}{r_{\Theta}}}

(126)

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon+C_{l}|E|^{\frac{1}{r_{\Theta}}}\leq 2\epsilon.

By excluding the tubular neighborhood
T^{\delta}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}
(thus the penalty term) and using the same interior and exterior pressure arguments as in Propositions
8.3
and
8.4
we can extend this result to
{{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}\setminus({\mathcal{O}}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}\cup T^{\delta}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}})}
.
Moreover, due to Proposition
8.5
we have that
\mathbbm{1}_{T^{\delta}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}(a\rho_{\delta}^{\gamma}+\delta\rho_{\delta}^{\beta})\to 0
in
L^{1}(\tilde{\Omega}\times(0,T)\times{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha})
which implies equi-integrability of
\mathbbm{1}_{T^{\delta}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}(a\rho_{\delta}^{\gamma}+\delta\rho_{\delta}^{\beta})
.
Hence, we conclude that,

(127)

\displaystyle a\hat{\rho}^{\gamma}_{\delta}+\delta\hat{\rho}^{\beta}_{\delta}\rightharpoonup\bar{p}\text{ weakly in }L^{1}(\tilde{\Omega};L^{1}(0,T;L^{1}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))).

∎

8.5.
Passage to the limit as
\delta\to 0
in the weak formulation.

Our next aim is to pass
\delta\to 0
in (
91
). Before explicitly passing to the limit in the terms in the weak formulation (
91
), we first show that the kinematic coupling condition holds for the limiting fluid/structure functions
(\boldsymbol{u},v)
in the limit
\delta\to 0
. This is nontrivial, because the kinematic coupling condition does not necessarily hold for the approximate solutions
(\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{\delta},\hat{v}_{\delta})
due to the presence of the penalty term in the weak formulation. However, we will recover the kinematic coupling condition for the limiting functions as a consequence of the uniform bound given in (
95
), which controls the difference between
\hat{\boldsymbol{u}_{\delta}}
and
\hat{v}_{\delta}\boldsymbol{e}_{z}
in the exterior neighborhood
T^{\delta}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}
which formally “shrinks” to
\Gamma_{\eta^{*}}
in the limit as
\delta\to 0
. This is the content of the following proposition:

Proposition 8.7
.

The kinematic coupling condition
\boldsymbol{u}|_{\Gamma_{\eta^{*}}}=v\boldsymbol{e}_{z}
is satisfied
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost surely.

Proof.

First, we compute the difference between the fluid velocity and its trace along the moving interface over the exterior tubular neighborhood defined in (
27
):

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{T^{\delta}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}\left|\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{\delta}-\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{\delta}|_{\Gamma_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}\right|^{2}

\displaystyle=\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\int_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}^{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}+\delta^{\left(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{\beta}\right)}}\Big|\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{\delta}(t,x,y,z)-\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{\delta}(t,x,y,\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}(t,x,y))\Big|^{2}dzdydxdt

\displaystyle=\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\int_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}^{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}+\delta^{\left(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{\beta}\right)}}\left|\int_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}^{z}\partial_{z}\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{\delta}(t,x,y,w)dw\right|^{2}dzdydxdt

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\left(\delta^{2\left(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{\beta}\right)}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\int_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}^{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}+\delta^{\left(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{\beta}\right)}}\int_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}^{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}+\delta^{\left(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{\beta}\right)}}|\partial_{z}\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{\delta}(t,x,y,w)|^{2}dwdzdydxdt\right)

\displaystyle\leq\delta^{3\left(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{\beta}\right)}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\|\partial_{z}\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{\delta}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(T^{\delta}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}))}\leq C\delta^{3\left(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{\beta}\right)}.

We then combine this estimate with the uniform bound (
95
) on the difference between the fluid and structure velocity in the exterior tubular neighborhood to deduce:

\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\left|\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{\delta}|_{\Gamma_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}-\hat{v}_{\delta}\boldsymbol{e}_{z}\right|^{2}\leq\frac{C_{r}}{\delta^{\left(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{\beta}\right)}}\left(\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{T^{\delta}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}\left|\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{\delta}-\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{\delta}|_{\Gamma_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}\right|^{2}+\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{T^{\delta}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}|\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{\delta}-\hat{v}_{\delta}\boldsymbol{e}_{z}|^{2}\right)\\
\leq C\left[\delta^{2\left(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{\beta}\right)}+\delta^{-\left(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{\beta}\right)}\left(\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{T^{\delta}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}|\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{\delta}-\hat{v}_{\delta}\boldsymbol{e}_{z}|^{2}\right)\right]\leq C\left(\delta^{2\left(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{\beta}\right)}+\delta^{\left(\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{\beta}\right)}\right),

so we conclude that

(128)

\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\left|\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{\delta}|_{\Gamma_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}-\hat{v}_{\delta}\boldsymbol{e}_{z}\right|^{2}\to 0,\quad\text{ as }\delta\to 0.

The proof would be complete if we can show an appropriate convergence of the left-hand side of (
128
) to a quantity involving the limiting
\boldsymbol{u}|_{\Gamma_{\eta^{*}}}
and
v\boldsymbol{e}_{z}
as
\delta\to 0
. For the structure velocities, we recall that
\hat{v}_{\delta}\to\hat{v}
strongly
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost surely in
L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Gamma))
. It remains to consider the convergence of the traces of the fluid velocities.

We claim that we have a similar weak convergence of the traces along the moving interface
\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{\delta}|_{\Gamma_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}
to
\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}|_{\Gamma_{\eta}}
for the fluid velocities. To see this, we note that the following function
w
is well-defined for each
\delta
on
[0,T]\times\Gamma\times[0,\alpha]
, by the properties of the stopping of the process depending on the parameter
\alpha
:

\boldsymbol{w}_{\delta}(t,x,y,z)=\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{\delta}(t,x,y,1+\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}(t,x,y)-z).

Note that because
\nabla\boldsymbol{u}
is uniformly bounded in
L^{p}(\Omega;L^{2}([0,T]\times\mathcal{O}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}))
for any
1\leq p<\infty
, the traces
\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{\delta}|_{\Gamma_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}:=\boldsymbol{w}_{\delta}|_{\Gamma\times\{0\}}
are well-defined and, for any
q<2
,

(129)

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{w}_{\delta}(t)|_{z=0}\|_{W^{1-\frac{1}{q},q}(\Gamma)}\leq C_{tr}\|\boldsymbol{w}_{\delta}(t)\|_{W^{1,q}(\Gamma\times[0,\alpha])}\leq C(\alpha)\|\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{\delta}(t)\|_{H^{1}({\mathcal{O}}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}})},

where
C_{tr}
is the constant appearing in the usual trace theorem that depends only on
\alpha,\Gamma
.

Moreover, note, due to the strong uniform convergence of
\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}
and weak convergence of
\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{\delta}
in
L^{2}(0,T;L^{6}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}))
due to Theorem
8.1
, that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma\times[0,\alpha]}\boldsymbol{w}_{\delta}{\boldsymbol{q}}=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}}\mathbbm{1}_{S^{\alpha}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{\delta}{\boldsymbol{q}}\to\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}}\mathbbm{1}_{S^{\alpha}_{\eta^{*}}}{\boldsymbol{u}}{\boldsymbol{q}}=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma\times[0,\alpha]}\boldsymbol{w}{\boldsymbol{q}}.

where
S^{\alpha}_{\eta}:={\mathcal{O}}_{\eta}\setminus{\mathcal{O}}_{\eta-\alpha}
and
\boldsymbol{w}(t,x,y,z)={\boldsymbol{u}}(t,x,y,1+\eta^{*}(t,x,y)-z)
. Additionally, the
\delta
-independent almost sure bound (
129
), implies that

\boldsymbol{w}_{\delta}\rightharpoonup\boldsymbol{w}
weakly in
L^{2}(0,T;W^{1,q}(\Gamma\times[0,\alpha]))
almost surely. Hence
\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{\delta}|_{\Gamma_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}
converges weakly
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost surely in
L^{q}([0,T]\times\Gamma)
for
1\leq q<2
, to
\boldsymbol{w}|_{z=0}={\boldsymbol{u}}|_{\Gamma_{\eta^{*}}}
.

So combining this weak convergence and the
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost sure strong convergence of
\hat{v}_{\delta}\to v
in
L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Gamma))
with (
128
), we obtain using weak lower semicontinuity that for
1\leq q<2
:

\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\Big|{\boldsymbol{u}}|_{\Gamma_{\eta^{*}}}-v\boldsymbol{e}_{z}\Big|^{q}=0,

so that
{\boldsymbol{u}}|_{\Gamma_{\eta^{*}}}=v\boldsymbol{e}_{z}
,
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost surely.
∎

Finally, we comment on how to pass to the limit in the terms in the weak formulation (
109
) for the new random variables on the probability space. The only involved terms in the weak formulation (
109
) for the limit passage
\delta\to 0
are the advection term
\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta}\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{\delta}\otimes\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{\delta}):\nabla\boldsymbol{q}
and the stochastic integrals. We begin with showing convergence of the advection term. A primary difficulty that is involved in both convergences of the advection term and the stochastic integral is that we must estimate the contribution of
\hat{\rho}_{\delta}
outside of the approximate domain
\mathcal{O}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}
, since the spatial integrals are over the entire maximal domain
\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}
.

Limit passage for the advection term.
For the limit of the advection term, we will need to use the
(\hat{\mathcal{F}}_{t})_{t\geq 0}
-stopping time
\tau^{\eta}
and the
(\hat{\mathcal{F}}^{\delta}_{t})_{t\geq 0}
-stopping time
\tau^{\eta}_{\delta}
because the estimate Proposition
8.2
only holds up until the stopping time
\tau^{\eta}_{\delta}
defined in (
100
). We want to pass to the limit in the weak formulation almost surely, for almost every
t\in[0,\tau^{\eta}]
, where
\tau^{\eta}
is the stopping time corresponding to the
limiting structure displacement
. So consider any
t\in[0,\tau^{\eta}]
. By properties of stopping times, we have that

\tau^{\eta}\leq\liminf_{\delta\to 0}\tau^{\eta}_{\delta},\quad\tilde{{\mathbb{P}}}\text{-almost surely.}

So given any (deterministic) time
t\in[0,\tau^{\eta}(\tilde{\omega})]
and a specific outcome
\tilde{\omega}\in\tilde{\Omega}
, we can find a subsequence
\delta_{m}\to 0
(which depends on the outcome
\tilde{\omega}\in\tilde{\Omega}
) such that

(130)

t\leq\tau^{\eta}_{\delta_{m}}(\tilde{\omega}),\quad\text{ for all }m.

We then want to show that given a sequence of
(\hat{\mathcal{F}}^{\delta}_{t})_{t\in[0,T]}
-adapted, essentially bounded, smooth processes
\boldsymbol{q}_{\delta}
that converge
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost surely and strongly to
\boldsymbol{q}
in
C(0,T;C^{k}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}))
for any positive integer
k
, we have that:

(131)

\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta_{m}}\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{\delta_{m}}\otimes\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{\delta_{m}}):\nabla\boldsymbol{q}_{\delta_{m}}\to\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\eta}}\rho{\boldsymbol{u}}\otimes{\boldsymbol{u}}:\nabla\boldsymbol{q},

for the specific (deterministic) choice of
t\in[0,\tau^{\eta}(\tilde{\omega})]
, the specific outcome
\tilde{\omega}
, and the corresponding (random) subsequence
\delta_{m}\to 0
.

To do this, we separate the integral into two contributions, an integral over the physical domain with the exterior tubular neighborhood included and an integral over the remainder of the maximal domain:

(132)

\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta_{m}}\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{\delta_{m}}\otimes\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{\delta_{m}}):\nabla\boldsymbol{q}_{\delta_{m}}\\
=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\hat{\eta}_{\delta_{m}}^{*}}\cup T^{\delta_{m}}_{\hat{\eta}_{\delta_{m}}^{*}}}\hat{\rho}_{\delta_{m}}\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{\delta}\otimes\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{\delta_{m}}:\nabla\boldsymbol{q}_{\delta_{m}}+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{(\mathcal{O}_{\hat{\eta}_{\delta_{m}}^{*}}\cup T^{\delta_{m}}_{\hat{\eta}_{\delta_{m}}^{*}})^{c}}\hat{\rho}_{\delta_{m}}\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{\delta_{m}}\otimes\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{\delta_{m}}:\nabla\boldsymbol{q}_{\delta_{m}}.

The exterior integral.
We first consider the contribution on the exterior
(\mathcal{O}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}\cup T^{\delta}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}})^{c}
and omit the
m
index on the subsequence to simplify notation, where we emphasize that the (random) subsequence of
\delta_{m}
although not explicit in the notation, is still an important component of the proof due to (
130
). The goal is to show that for this fixed outcome
\tilde{\omega}\in\tilde{\Omega}
and fixed
t\in[0,\tau^{\eta}(\tilde{\omega})]
, we have that

(133)

\int_{0}^{t}\int_{(\mathcal{O}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}\cup T^{\delta}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}})^{c}}\hat{\rho}_{\delta}\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{\delta}\otimes\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{\delta}:\nabla\boldsymbol{q}_{\delta}\to 0.

Because the random test functions we construct (in the next Section
8.6
) will satisfy the convergence
\boldsymbol{q}_{\delta}\to\boldsymbol{q}
in
C(0,T;C^{k}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}))
for any positive integer
k
, strongly and
\tilde{{\mathbb{P}}}
-almost surely, we have that
\|\nabla\boldsymbol{q}_{\delta}(\tilde{\omega})\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})}
is uniformly bounded in
\delta
. For this fixed but arbitrary
\tilde{\omega}\in\tilde{\Omega}
and
t\in[0,\tau^{\eta}(\tilde{\omega})]
, we estimate:

\left|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{(\mathcal{O}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}\cup T^{\delta}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}})^{c}}\hat{\rho}_{\delta}\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{\delta}\otimes\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{\delta}:\nabla\boldsymbol{q}_{\delta}\right|\\
\leq C(\tilde{\omega})\|\sqrt{\hat{\rho}_{\delta}}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{r}((\mathcal{O}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}\cup T^{\delta}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}})^{c})}\|\sqrt{\hat{\rho}_{\delta}}\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{\delta}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}))}\|\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{\delta}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{q}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))},

for some
q<6
and
3<r<2\gamma
so that
\frac{1}{r}+\frac{1}{q}=\frac{1}{2}
.

Hence, (
133
) will follow once we make the following observations:

•

First,
\|\sqrt{\hat{\rho}_{\delta}}\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{\delta}(\tilde{\omega})\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}))}
is uniformly bounded in
\delta
.

•

We use the ideas employed in Proposition
8.2
and interpolation to conclude that for some
0<\theta<1
, depending only on
r
and
\gamma
, and any
t\in[0,\tau^{\eta}_{\delta}(\tilde{\omega})]
:

(134)

\begin{split}&\|\sqrt{\hat{\rho}}_{\delta}\|_{L^{r}((\mathcal{O}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}\cup T^{\delta}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}})^{c})}=\|\hat{\rho}_{\delta}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{\frac{r}{2}}((\mathcal{O}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}\cup T^{\delta}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}})^{c})}\\
&\leq\|\hat{\rho}_{\delta}\|^{\frac{\theta}{2}}_{L^{1}((\mathcal{O}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}\cup T^{\delta}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}})^{c}))}\|\hat{\rho}_{\delta}\|_{L^{\gamma}((\mathcal{O}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}\cup T^{\delta}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}})^{c})}^{\frac{1-\theta}{2}}\leq C(\tilde{\omega})\delta^{\frac{\theta\nu_{*}}{2}}=:C(\tilde{\omega})\delta^{\nu_{0}}.\end{split}

Here we define
\nu_{0}=\frac{\theta\nu_{*}}{2}
where
\nu_{*}
is any constant that satisfies the condition in Proposition
8.2
and
\theta
is determined by the interpolation inequality (
134
).
Here, because of the condition (
130
) on the subsequence
\delta_{m}
we have chosen, we can apply Proposition
8.2
, since this lemma only holds for times up to the stopping time
\tau^{\eta}_{\delta}
. Note that here, it is essential that we are applying the result about the
L^{1}
integrability of the pressure in the exterior domain in Proposition
8.2
pathwise, as in (
111
).

•

Thanks to (
94
) and Theorem
8.1
we have

(135)

\displaystyle\delta^{\nu_{0}/2}\|\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{\delta}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{q}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}))}

\displaystyle\leq\delta^{\nu_{0}/2}\|\nabla\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{\delta}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}))}\leq\|\sqrt{\mu^{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}_{\delta}}\nabla\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{\delta}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))}\leq C(\tilde{\omega}).

Then, we get the desired convergence (
133
).

The interior integral.
Next, we show that for the (random) subsequence
\delta_{m}
satisfying (
130
) for our fixed but arbitrary
t\in[0,\tau^{\eta}(\tilde{\omega})]
and
\tilde{\omega}\in\tilde{\Omega}
, and for a sequence of (random) test functions for which
\boldsymbol{q}_{\delta}(\tilde{\omega})\to\boldsymbol{q}(\tilde{\omega})
in
C(0,T;C^{k}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}))
for all nonnegative integers
k
, we have that:

(136)

\int_{0}^{t}\int_{{{\mathcal{O}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}\cup T^{\delta}_{\hat{\eta}_{\delta}^{*}}}}}\hat{\rho}_{\delta}\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{\delta}\otimes\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{\delta}:\nabla\boldsymbol{q}_{\delta}\to\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\eta}}\rho{\boldsymbol{u}}\otimes{\boldsymbol{u}}:\nabla\boldsymbol{q},

where we have omitted the explicit labeling of the (random) subsequence
\delta_{m}\to 0
satisfying (
130
) with the subscript
m
, for simplicity of notation. By the condition (
130
), we have that
\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau^{\eta}}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\eta}}\rho{\boldsymbol{u}}\otimes{\boldsymbol{u}}:\nabla\boldsymbol{q}=\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau^{\eta}}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\eta^{*}}}\rho{\boldsymbol{u}}\otimes{\boldsymbol{u}}:\nabla\boldsymbol{q}
, and hence, this convergence will follow from Lemma
8.3
(see below) and (
138
).

Conclusion of advection term limit.
Thus, combining both the exterior estimate (
133
) and the interior estimate (
136
) gives the desired convergence (
131
) for an arbitrary time
t\in[0,\tau^{\eta}(\tilde{\omega})]
and for an arbitrary outcome
\tilde{\omega}
in some measurable set of probability one in
\tilde{\Omega}
.

Convergence of the stochastic integrals.
Next we will show how to treat the stochastic integral. We first begin by showing two essential convergences that will appear several times in the proof of the convergence of the stochastic integral for the fluid, which involves terms of the type
\rho_{\delta}|\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{\delta}|^{2}
.

Lemma 8.3
.

For some
r>1
, we have the following
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost sure convergences:

\displaystyle\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}\hat{\rho}_{\delta}|\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{\delta}|^{2}

\displaystyle\rightharpoonup\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\eta^{*}}}\rho|{\boldsymbol{u}}|^{2},\quad\text{ weakly in }L^{r}((0,T)\times\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}),

\displaystyle\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}\hat{\rho}_{\delta}\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{\delta}\otimes\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{\delta}

\displaystyle\rightharpoonup\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\eta^{*}}}\rho{\boldsymbol{u}}\otimes{\boldsymbol{u}},\quad\text{ weakly in }L^{r}((0,T)\times\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}).

Proof.

We consider just the first convergence of
\hat{\rho}_{\delta}|\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{\delta}|^{2}
since the second convergence is analogous. Note that for some
q\in(3/2,\gamma)
, which is possible since
\gamma>3/2
, we have that:

\rho|{\boldsymbol{u}}|^{2}\leq|\rho|^{q}+|{\boldsymbol{u}}|^{\frac{2q}{q-1}},

where
\frac{2q}{q-1}<6
. So by considering the Carathéodory function
H(\rho,{\boldsymbol{u}})=\rho|{\boldsymbol{u}}|^{2}
, we obtain from (
101
) the following
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost sure convergence by considering the weak limit of
H(\hat{\rho}_{\delta},\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{\delta})
:

(137)

\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}\hat{\rho}_{\delta}|\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{\delta}|^{2}\rightharpoonup\overline{\rho|{\boldsymbol{u}}|^{2}},\text{ weakly in $L^{r}((0,T)\times\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})$ for some $r>1$.}

However, we have not yet identified this weak limit
\overline{\rho|{\boldsymbol{u}}|^{2}}
from (
101
) explicitly as
\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\eta^{*}}}\rho|{\boldsymbol{u}}|^{2}
, and if we are able to do this, the proof would be complete.

So we will now identify the weak limit
\overline{\rho|{\boldsymbol{u}}|^{2}}=\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\eta^{*}}}\rho|{\boldsymbol{u}}|^{2}
. By the application of the Skorohod representation theorem in Theorem
8.1
, we have the following
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost sure convergences:

\displaystyle\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}\rightharpoonup\eta^{*},\quad\text{ weakly-star in }L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{2}(\Gamma)),

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}\rightharpoonup\partial_{t}{\eta}^{*},\quad\text{ weakly-star in }L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\Gamma)).

In addition, we have from Theorem
8.1
and the strong convergence of

(138)

\displaystyle{{\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}\text{ and }}}\mathbbm{1}_{\left(\mathcal{O}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}\cup T^{\delta}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}\right)}\to\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\eta^{*}}},\quad\tilde{\mathbb{P}}\text{-almost surely in}\quad L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{q}(\Gamma))\quad\forall 1\leq q<\infty,

that

\displaystyle\mathbbm{1}_{\left(\mathcal{O}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}\cup T^{\delta}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}\right)}\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{\delta}\rightharpoonup\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\eta^{*}}}{\boldsymbol{u}},\quad\text{ weakly in }L^{2}(0,T;L^{r}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})),

\displaystyle\mathbbm{1}_{\left(\mathcal{O}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}\cup T^{\delta}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}\right)}\nabla\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{\delta}\rightharpoonup\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\eta^{*}}}\nabla{\boldsymbol{u}},\quad\text{ weakly in }L^{2}(0,T;L^{r}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})),

for any
1\leq r<2
. In addition,

\hat{\rho}_{\delta}\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{\delta}\rightharpoonup\rho{\boldsymbol{u}},\quad\text{ weakly-star in }L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{\frac{2\gamma}{\gamma+1}}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})),

where
\frac{2\gamma}{\gamma+1}>6/5
since
\gamma>3/2
. Applying a deterministic compactness result for establishing weak convergence of products of functions defined on moving domains, given by Lemma 2.8 in
[
11
]
, to the current situation outcome by outcome for each
\tilde{\omega}\in\tilde{\Omega}
, we obtain that:

(139)

\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta_{m}}}}\hat{\rho}_{\delta_{m}}|\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{\delta_{m}}|^{2}(\tilde{\omega})\rightharpoonup\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\eta^{*}}}\rho|{\boldsymbol{u}}|^{2}(\tilde{\omega}),\quad\text{ weakly in $L^{r}((0,T)\times\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})$},

for some
r>1
, along a subsequence
\delta_{m}\to 0
that
depends on the outcome
\tilde{\omega}\in\tilde{\Omega}
. Even though this weak convergence in (
139
) is along a subsequence that depends on the outcome
\tilde{\omega}\in\tilde{\Omega}
, we get the desired convergence as
\delta\to 0
(without considering a subsequence) of
\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}\hat{\rho}_{\delta}|\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{\delta}|^{2}\rightharpoonup\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\eta^{*}}}\rho|{\boldsymbol{u}}|^{2}
weakly
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost surely in
L^{r}((0,T)\times\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})
for some
r>1
, by using (
137
) and by using uniqueness of the weak limit for each
\tilde{\omega}\in\tilde{\Omega}
to identify
\overline{\rho|{\boldsymbol{u}}|^{2}}
as
\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\eta^{*}}}\rho|{\boldsymbol{u}}|^{2}
.
∎

Using Lemma
8.3
, we can establish the following convergence, which will be useful for passing to the limit in the stochastic integral as
\delta\to 0
.

Lemma 8.4
.

We have the following convergence
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost surely as
\delta\to 0
:

\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\hat{\eta}_{\delta}^{*}}}\hat{\rho}_{\delta}|\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{\delta}-{\boldsymbol{u}}|^{2}\to 0.

Proof.

We calculate that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}\hat{\rho}_{\delta}|\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{\delta}-\boldsymbol{u}|^{2}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}\Big(\hat{\rho}_{\delta}|\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{\delta}|^{2}-\rho|\boldsymbol{u}|^{2}\Big)+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\eta^{*}}}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta}-\rho)|\boldsymbol{u}|^{2}

(140)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}\Big(\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}-\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\eta^{*}}}\Big)(\hat{\rho}_{\delta}-\rho)|{\boldsymbol{u}}|^{2}+2\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}(\rho\boldsymbol{u}-\hat{\rho}_{\delta}\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{\delta})\cdot\boldsymbol{u},

and we show that each of the terms on the right-hand side of (
140
) converges to
0
,
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost surely.

Term 1.
We estimate the first term on the right-hand side of (
140
) by noting that

\displaystyle\left|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}}\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta}|\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{\delta}|^{2}-\rho|{\boldsymbol{u}}|^{2})\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\eta^{*}}}(\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}\hat{\rho}_{\delta}|\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{\delta}|^{2}-\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\eta^{*}}}\rho|{\boldsymbol{u}}|^{2})\right|

(141)

\displaystyle+\left|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}(\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\eta^{*}}}-\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}})\hat{\rho}_{\delta}|\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{\delta}|^{2}\right|+\left|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}(\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\eta^{*}}}-\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}})\rho|{\boldsymbol{u}}|^{2}\right|.

By Lemma
8.3
,
\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\hat{\eta}_{\delta}^{*}}}\hat{\rho}_{\delta}|\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{\delta}|^{2}\rightharpoonup\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\eta^{*}}}\rho|{\boldsymbol{u}}|^{2}
weakly in
L^{r}((0,T)\times\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})
for some
r>1
,
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost surely, so since
\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\eta^{*}}}\in L^{q}((0,T)\times\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})

\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost surely, for all
1\leq q<\infty
, we have that
\displaystyle\left|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\eta^{*}}}(\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}\hat{\rho}_{\delta}|\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{\delta}|^{2}-\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\eta^{*}}}\rho|{\boldsymbol{u}}|^{2})\right|\to 0
as
\delta\to 0
,
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost surely.

Combining (
138
) with the
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost sure boundedness of
\hat{\rho}_{\delta}|\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{\delta}|^{2}
and
\rho|{\boldsymbol{u}}|^{2}
in
L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{\frac{2\gamma}{\gamma+1}}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}))
independently of
\delta
(but potentially depending on the outcome
\tilde{\omega}\in\tilde{\Omega}
), we obtain that the remaining terms on the right-hand side of (
141
) converge to zero
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost surely as
\delta\to 0
. This shows that Term 1 on the right-hand side of (
140
) converges to
0
,
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost surely.

Term 2.
Since
\hat{\rho}_{\delta}\to\rho
weakly-star in
L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{\gamma}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}))
and
\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\eta^{*}}}|{\boldsymbol{u}}|^{2}\in L^{1}(0,T;L^{3}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}))
,
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost surely, we have that

\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\eta^{*}}}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta}-\rho)|{\boldsymbol{u}}|^{2}\to 0,\quad\tilde{\mathbb{P}}\text{-almost surely.}

Term 3.
Using the fact that
\hat{\rho}_{\delta}
is uniformly bounded in
L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{\gamma}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}))

\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost surely,
|{\boldsymbol{u}}|^{2}\in L^{1}(0,T;L^{3}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}))

\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost surely, and (
138
), we have that

\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}\Big(\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}-\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\eta^{*}}}\Big)(\hat{\rho}_{\delta}-\rho)|{\boldsymbol{u}}|^{2}\to 0,\quad\text{$\tilde{\mathbb{P}}$-almost surely.}

Term 4.
By using the
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost sure convergence
\hat{\rho}_{\delta}\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{\delta}\rightharpoonup\rho\boldsymbol{u}
weakly-star in
L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{\frac{2\gamma}{\gamma+1}}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}))
and the fact that
\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\eta^{*}}}\boldsymbol{u}\in L^{2}(0,T;L^{6}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}))

\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost surely, we have

\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\eta^{*}}}(\rho{\boldsymbol{u}}-\hat{\rho}_{\delta}\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{\delta})\cdot\boldsymbol{u}\to 0,\quad\text{$\tilde{\mathbb{P}}$-almost surely.}

Since uniform bounds combined with (
138
) imply that

\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}\Big(\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\eta^{*}}}-\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}\Big)(\rho{\boldsymbol{u}}-\hat{\rho}_{\delta}\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{\delta})\cdot{\boldsymbol{u}}\to 0,\quad\text{$\tilde{\mathbb{P}}$-almost surely},

we thus obtain the desired convergence that

\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}(\rho{\boldsymbol{u}}-\hat{\rho}_{\delta}\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{\delta})\cdot{\boldsymbol{u}}\to 0,\quad\text{$\tilde{\mathbb{P}}$-almost surely.}

Conclusion of the proof.
Since each of the four terms on the right-hand side of (
140
) converges to zero
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost surely, this completes the proof of the lemma.
∎

Before showing convergence of the stochastic integrals, we show two technical lemmas, regarding properties of compositions of the fluid densities with Lipschitz functions
b
.

Lemma 8.5
.

There exists
p_{0}>1
such that for every Lipschitz function
b:{\mathbb{R}}\to{\mathbb{R}}
with
b(0)=0
and
b^{\prime}(w)=0
for
|w|
sufficiently large and such that for all
1\leq p<p_{0}
:

\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}^{c}}b(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})\hat{u}_{\delta}\to 0,\qquad\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}^{c}}(b^{\prime}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})\hat{\rho}_{\delta}-b(\hat{\rho}_{\delta}))(\nabla\cdot\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{\delta})\to 0,

both strongly in
L^{p}([0,T]\times\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})
.

Proof.

Consider such a function
b
and some
1\leq p<p_{0}
where
p_{0}
will be appropriately chosen later in the proof. By the boundedness of
b
,
|b(z)|\leq Cz^{\frac{3(2-p)}{4p}}
and
|b^{\prime}(z)z|\leq Cz^{\frac{3(2-p)}{4p}}
for all
z\geq 0
. We estimate using (
134
):

\displaystyle\|\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}^{c}}b(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})\hat{u}_{\delta}

\displaystyle\|_{L^{p}([0,T]\times\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})}^{p}+\|\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}^{c}}(b^{\prime}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})\hat{\rho}_{\delta}-b(\hat{\rho}_{\delta}))(\nabla\cdot\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{\delta})\|_{L^{p}([0,T]\times\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})}^{p}

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{0}^{t}\int_{(\mathcal{O}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}\cup T^{\delta}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}})^{c}}\hat{\rho}_{\delta}^{\frac{3(2-p)}{4}}(|\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{\delta}|^{p}+|\nabla\cdot\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{\delta}|^{p})+C\int_{0}^{t}\int_{T^{\delta}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}\hat{\rho}_{\delta}^{\frac{3(2-p)}{4}}(|\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{\delta}|^{p}+|\nabla\cdot\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{\delta}|^{p})

\displaystyle\leq C\|\hat{\rho}_{\delta}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{3/2}(({\mathcal{O}}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}\cup T^{\delta}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}})^{c})}^{\frac{3(2-p)}{4}}\left(\|\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{\delta}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}))}^{p}+\|\nabla\cdot\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{\delta}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(({\mathcal{O}}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}\cup T^{\delta}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}})^{c}))}^{p}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+C|T^{\delta}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}|^{\frac{2\gamma-3(2-p)}{4\gamma}}\|\hat{\rho}_{\delta}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{\gamma}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}))}^{\frac{3(2-p)}{4}}\left(\|\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{\delta}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}))}^{p}+\|\nabla\cdot\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{\delta}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}\cup T^{\delta}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}))}^{p}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C_{\phi,k}(\tilde{\omega})\delta^{\frac{3(2-p)\nu_{0}}{2}}(1+\delta^{-\frac{\nu_{0}p}{2}})+C_{\phi,\kappa}(\tilde{\omega})\delta^{\frac{2\gamma-3(2-p)}{4\gamma}},

where in the last step, we use the fact from Theorem
8.1
that
\sqrt{\lambda^{{\eta}^{*}}_{\delta}}\nabla\cdot\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{\delta}
and
\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}\cup T^{\delta}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}
are both
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost surely bounded in
L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}))
and
\lambda^{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}_{\delta}\geq\delta^{\nu_{0}}
on
\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}
by (
94
). We can then choose
p_{0}>1
sufficiently small so that

\frac{3(2-p)\nu_{0}}{2}-\frac{\nu_{0}p}{2}>0\ \ \text{ and }\ \ \frac{2\gamma-3(2-p)}{4\gamma}>0

for all
1\leq p<p_{0}
, recalling that
\gamma>3/2
.
∎

We can use the previous technical lemma to prove the following weak continuity result on compositions of Lipschitz functions
b
with the fluid densities
\hat{\rho}_{\delta}
.

Lemma 8.6
.

Let
b:{\mathbb{R}}\to{\mathbb{R}}
be a
C^{1}
function such that
b(0)=0
and
b^{\prime}(w)=0
for
|w|
sufficiently large. Then, the following convergence holds
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
almost surely:

\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}b(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})\to\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\eta^{*}}}\overline{b(\rho)}\quad\text{ in }C_{w}(0,T;L^{\gamma}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})).

Proof.

We use the renormalized weak formulation, which holds
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost surely in the following distributional sense:

(142)

\partial_{t}b(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})+\text{div}(b(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{\delta})+(b^{\prime}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})\hat{\rho}_{\delta}-b(\hat{\rho}_{\delta}))\text{div}(\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{\delta})=0,

along with the weak limit properties of compositions with Carathéodory functions. Using the weak limit property stated in (
101
), we have the following
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost sure convergences:

(143)

\begin{split}b(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})\rightharpoonup\overline{b(\rho)}\text{ weakly in }L^{\gamma}([0,T]\times\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})&,\quad\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\eta_{\delta}^{*}}}b(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{\delta}\rightharpoonup\overline{b(\rho)\boldsymbol{u}}\text{ weakly in }L^{\min(\gamma,6)}([0,T]\times\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}),\\
\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\hat{\eta}_{\delta}^{*}}}b^{\prime}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})\hat{\rho}_{\delta}\text{div}(\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{\delta})&\rightharpoonup\overline{b^{\prime}(\rho)\rho\text{div}(\boldsymbol{u})}\text{ weakly in }L^{\min(\gamma,2)}([0,T]\times\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}),\\
\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}b(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})\text{div}(\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{\delta})&\rightharpoonup\overline{b(\rho)\boldsymbol{u}}\text{ weakly in }L^{\min(\gamma,2)}([0,T]\times\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}).\end{split}

Fix a choice of an arbitrary deterministic test function
\varphi\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})
, and fix an arbitrary outcome
\tilde{\omega}\in\tilde{\Omega}
such that the renormalized weak formulation and the weak convergences in (
143
) hold. The proof will be complete if we can show that

(144)

\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}b(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})\varphi\to\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\eta^{*}}}\overline{b(\rho)}\varphi\quad\text{ in }C(0,T;{\mathbb{R}}).

We once again emphasize that for the remainder of the argument, the outcome
\tilde{\omega}
is
fixed but arbitrary
in a measurable set of probability one.

Step 1.

Show that
b(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})\to\overline{b(\rho)}
in
C_{w}(0,T;L^{\gamma}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))
.
By the renormalized weak formulation, for any fixed deterministic test function
\varphi\in C_{c}^{\infty}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha})
:

(145)

\begin{split}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}b(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})(t)\varphi&=\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}b(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})(0)\varphi+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}b(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{\delta}\cdot\nabla\varphi+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}^{c}}b(\hat{\rho})\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{\delta}\cdot\nabla\varphi\\
&+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}(b(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})-b^{\prime}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})\hat{\rho}_{\delta})\text{div}(\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{\delta})\varphi+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}^{c}}(b(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})-b^{\prime}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})\hat{\rho}_{\delta})\text{div}(\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{\delta})\varphi,\end{split}

where we divide the integrals into the parts inside and outside
\mathcal{O}^{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}
. Note that by Lemma
8.5
, the two integrals in (
145
) involving
\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}^{c}}
converge to
0
,
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost surely as
\delta\to 0
. Combined with the weak convergences in (
143
), we conclude from (
145
) that
\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}b(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})(t)\varphi
converges as
\delta\to 0
to

\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}b(\rho_{0})\varphi+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}\overline{b(\rho)\boldsymbol{u}}\cdot\nabla\varphi+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}(\overline{b(\rho)\text{div}(\boldsymbol{u})}-\overline{b^{\prime}(\rho)\rho\text{div}(\boldsymbol{u})})\varphi

Since
\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}b(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{\delta}
and
\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}(b(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})-b^{\prime}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})\hat{\rho}_{\delta})\text{div}(\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{\delta})
are bounded in
L^{2}((0,T)\times\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})
independently of
\delta
by some constant
C(\tilde{\omega})
depending on
\tilde{\omega}\in\tilde{\Omega}
by (
101
) and since we have higher bounds for some
p>1
in Lemma
8.5
for the terms involving
\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}^{c}}
, we conclude from (
145
) that the functions
\displaystyle t\to\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}b(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})(t)\varphi

\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost surely are uniformly equicontinuous (with an equicontinuity parameter depending only on
\tilde{\omega}\in\tilde{\Omega}
) independently of
\delta
, for our fixed but arbitrary
\tilde{\omega}\in\tilde{\Omega}
. Hence, for our fixed
\tilde{\omega}
, the continuous functions
\displaystyle t\to\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}b(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})(t)\varphi
converge in
C(0,T;{\mathbb{R}})
as
\delta\to 0
for our fixed
\tilde{\omega}\in\tilde{\Omega}
. Since by (
143
),
b(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})
converges weakly to
\overline{b(\rho)}
in
L^{r}((0,T)\times\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})
for
1<r<\gamma
, we identify the
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost sure limit of
\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}b(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})\varphi
in
C(0,T;{\mathbb{R}})
as
\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}\overline{b(\rho)}\varphi
, which concludes Step 1.

Step 2.

Show that
\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}b(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})\varphi\to\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}\mathbbm{1}_{\eta^{*}}\overline{b(\rho)}\varphi
in
L^{\infty}(0,T;{\mathbb{R}})
for any deterministic
\varphi\in C_{c}^{\infty}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha})
.
From Step 1, we already have that
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost surely:

\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}}b(\hat{\rho})\varphi\to\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}}\overline{b(\rho)}\varphi\quad\text{ in }C(0,T;{\mathbb{R}}).

Thus, Step 2 is immediate once we observe that
\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}^{c}}b(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})\varphi\to 0
in
L^{\infty}(0,T;{\mathbb{R}})
,
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost surely, by an easier variant of the same argument in Lemma
8.5
, and once we observe that
\overline{b(\rho)}=\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\eta^{*}}}\overline{b(\rho)}
. At this stage, the only remaining item left to show is that this convergence is actually in
C(0,T;{\mathbb{R}})
rather than just
L^{\infty}(0,T;{\mathbb{R}})
. This will be accomplished in the final step, Step 3.

Step 3.

Show that
\displaystyle t\to\int_{{\mathcal{O}}}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}b(\hat{\rho}_{\delta}(t))\varphi
is continuous from
[0,T]\to{\mathbb{R}}
, for each deterministic
\varphi\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})
.
From Step 1, we know that
\displaystyle t\to\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}b(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})(t)\varphi
is a real-valued continuous function on
[0,T]
,
{\mathbb{P}}
-almost surely for any deterministic
\varphi\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})
. For any fixed open connected set
B\subset\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}
, we can show by approximating
\mathbbm{1}_{B}\varphi
in the
L^{\frac{\gamma}{\gamma-1}}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha})
norm by smooth compactly supported functions, that

(146)

t\to\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}}\mathbbm{1}_{B}b(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})\varphi\quad\text{ is continuous for any fixed, open and connected $B\subset{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}$.}

Since
\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}\in C(0,T;C(\Gamma))
,
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost surely, we can show the desired
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost sure continuity of
\displaystyle t\to\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}b(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})\varphi
by using the boundedness of
b
and writing for any fixed
t_{0}\in[0,T]
:

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}(t)}}b(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})(t)\varphi

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}(t_{0})}}b(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})(t_{0})\varphi

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}\Big(\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}(t)}}-\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}(t_{0})}}\Big)b(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})(t)+\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}(t_{0})}}\Big(b(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})(t)-b(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})(t_{0})\Big),

where we can use the continuity property for fixed sets in (
146
) to estimate the second quantity. This completes the proof of Lemma
8.6
.
∎

Now that we have the necessary convergence results from Lemma
8.4
and Lemma
8.6
, we can now establish the desired convergence of the stochastic integrals for the limit passage as
\delta\to 0
.

Lemma 8.7

(Convergence of stochastic integrals)
.

For any collection of
(\hat{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{\delta})_{t\geq 0}
-adapted, essentially bounded, smooth processes
\{\psi_{\delta}\}_{\delta\geq 0}\in C(0,T;C^{\infty}(\Gamma))
and
\{\boldsymbol{q}_{\delta}\}_{\delta\geq 0}\in C(0,T;C^{\infty}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))
such that
\boldsymbol{q}_{\delta}
converges almost surely to some
(\hat{\mathcal{F}}_{t})_{t\geq 0}
-adapted process
\boldsymbol{q}
in
C(0,T;H^{l}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}))
and
\psi_{\delta}
converges almost surely to some
(\hat{\mathcal{F}}_{t})_{t\geq 0}
-adapted process
\psi
in
C(0,T;H^{l}(\Gamma))

for
l>\frac{5}{2}
,
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-a.s. such that

(147)

\|\psi_{\delta}\|_{L^{\infty}(\tilde{\Omega}\times[0,T]\times\Gamma)}+\|\boldsymbol{q}_{\delta}\|_{L^{\infty}(\tilde{\Omega}\times[0,T];H^{l}_{0}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}))}\leq C,

for a constant
C
that is independent of
\delta
, we have the following
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost sure convergence:

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}\boldsymbol{F}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta},\hat{\rho}_{\delta}\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{\delta})\cdot\boldsymbol{q}_{\delta}d\hat{W}^{1}_{\delta}(t)\to\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\eta^{*}}}\overline{\boldsymbol{F}(\rho,\rho{\boldsymbol{u}})}\cdot\boldsymbol{q}d\hat{W}^{1}(t),

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}G(\hat{\eta}_{\delta},\hat{v}_{\delta})\cdot\psi_{\delta}d\hat{W}_{\delta}^{2}(t)\to\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}G(\eta,v)\cdot\psi d\hat{W}^{2}(t).

Here the limiting
\overline{\boldsymbol{F}(\rho,\rho{\boldsymbol{u}})}
is defined by its action on the orthonormal basis of
\mathcal{U}_{0}
, via
\overline{\boldsymbol{F}(\rho,\rho{\boldsymbol{u}})}\boldsymbol{e}_{k}=\overline{f_{k}(\rho,\rho{\boldsymbol{u}})}
where the functions
\overline{{f}_{k}(\rho,\rho{\boldsymbol{u}})}
are the weak limits of
f_{k}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta},\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{\delta},\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}\nabla\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{\delta})
in
L^{p}((0,T)\times\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})
for some
p>1
, given by the stochastic noise assumption (
7
) and the result on weak limits of Carathéodory functions composed with the approximate solutions, see (
101
).

Proof.

By classical methods in
[
1
]
(see Lemma 2.1 in
[
15
]
, Lemma 2.6.6. in
[
7
]
), it suffices to show:

\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\hat{\eta}_{\delta}^{*}}}\boldsymbol{F}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta},\hat{\rho}_{\delta}\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{\delta})\cdot\boldsymbol{q}_{\delta}\to\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\eta^{*}}}\overline{\boldsymbol{F}(\rho,\rho{\boldsymbol{u}})}\cdot\boldsymbol{q}\quad\text{ in $L^{2}(0,T;L_{2}(\mathcal{U}_{0};\mathbb{R}))$ in probability}.

We observe that this will be established if we show that
\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}{f}_{k}(\rho_{\delta},\rho_{\delta}{\boldsymbol{u}}_{\delta})\to\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\eta^{*}}}\overline{{f}_{k}(\rho,\rho{\boldsymbol{u}})}
almost surely in
L^{2}(0,T;H^{-l}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}))
for
l>5/2
, by the strong
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost sure convergence of
\boldsymbol{q}_{\delta}\to\boldsymbol{q}
in
C(0,T;C^{m}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}))
for all
m\in\mathbb{N}
. To prove this, it suffices to show the following two
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost sure strong convergences:

(148)

\displaystyle\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}f_{k}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta},\hat{\rho}_{\delta}\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{\delta})-{\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\hat{\eta}_{\delta}^{*}}}}f_{k}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta},\hat{\rho}_{\delta}{\boldsymbol{u}})\to 0\quad\text{ in }L^{2}(0,T;H^{-l}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})),

(149)

\displaystyle{\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\hat{\eta}_{\delta}^{*}}}}f_{k}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta},\hat{\rho}_{\delta}{\boldsymbol{u}})\to{\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\eta^{*}}}}\overline{f_{k}(\rho,\rho{\boldsymbol{u}})}\quad\text{ in }L^{2}(0,T;H^{-l}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})).

and the uniform estimate

(150)

\lim_{m\to\infty}\left(\sup_{\delta}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{T}\sum_{k=m}^{\infty}\left(\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}|f_{k}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta},\hat{\rho}_{\delta}\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{\delta})\cdot\boldsymbol{q}_{\delta}|+\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\eta^{*}}}|\overline{f_{k}(\rho,\rho\boldsymbol{u})}\cdot\boldsymbol{q}|\right)^{2}\right)=0.

To prove these statements, we use techniques from the proof of Proposition 4.4.12 in
[
7
]
for the analogous convergence of stochastic integrals in the context of stochastic Navier-Stokes equations on a fixed domain, where we must adapt many of the calculations to account for the fact that we only have uniform bounds of
\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{\delta}
on (moving) domains
\mathcal{O}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}
, rather than on a fixed domain.

Proof of (
150
).
Since
\frac{2\gamma}{\gamma+1}>\frac{6}{5}
, thanks to (
7
), we note that
f_{k}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta},\hat{\rho}_{\delta}\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{\delta})
is uniformly bounded in
L^{p}(\tilde{\Omega};L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{6/5}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})))
, and in fact it is also uniformly bounded in
L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{6/5}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}))

\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost surely, thanks to Theorem
8.1
. So along a subsequence depending on the outcome
\tilde{\omega}
,
f_{k}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta},\hat{\rho}_{\delta}\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{\delta})
converges weakly-star in
L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{6/5}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}))
to some limit, but this limit must
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost surely be
\overline{f_{k}(\rho,\rho{\boldsymbol{u}})}
, since this has already been identified via (
101
) as the
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost sure limit of
f_{k}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta},\hat{\rho}_{\delta}\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{\delta})
in
L^{r}((0,T)\times\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})
for some
r>1
. So by weak lower semi-continuity and the assumption on the noise (
7
), we conclude that for all
1\leq p<\infty
:

\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\left(\|f_{k}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta},\hat{\rho}_{\delta}{\boldsymbol{u}}_{\delta})\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{6/5}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}))}+\|\overline{f_{k}(\rho,{\boldsymbol{u}})}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{6/5}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})))}\right)^{p}\leq C_{p}c_{k}^{p},

for some constant
C_{p}
that is independent of
k
and
\delta
. Therefore, using the assumption on the test functions in (
147
):

\sup_{\delta}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{T}\sum_{k=m}^{\infty}\left(\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}|f_{k}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta},\hat{\rho}_{\delta}\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{\delta})\cdot\boldsymbol{q}_{\delta}|+\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\eta}}|\overline{f_{k}(\rho,\rho\boldsymbol{u})}\cdot\boldsymbol{q}|\right)^{2}\leq C_{p,T}\sum_{k=m}^{\infty}c_{k}^{2},

which establishes (
150
) since
\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}c_{k}^{2}<\infty
by the assumptions on the noise.

Proof of (
148
).
First, by using (
7
) we can estimate:

\int_{0}^{T}\|\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}\left(f_{k}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta},\hat{\rho}_{\delta}\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{\delta})-f_{k}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta},\hat{\rho}_{\delta}{\boldsymbol{u}})\right)\|_{H^{-l}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})}^{2}\leq C\int_{0}^{T}\left(\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}|f_{k}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta},\hat{\rho}_{\delta}\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{\delta})-f_{k}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta},\hat{\rho}_{\delta}{\boldsymbol{u}})|\right)^{2}\\
\leq c_{k}\int_{0}^{T}\left(\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}\hat{\rho}_{\delta}|\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{\delta}-{\boldsymbol{u}}|\right)^{2}\leq c_{k}{\|\hat{\rho}_{\delta}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{1}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}))}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}\hat{\rho}_{\delta}|\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{\delta}-{\boldsymbol{u}}|^{2}.

Since
\hat{\rho}_{\delta}\rightharpoonup\rho
weakly-star in
L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{\gamma}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}))

\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost surely, the norms
\|\hat{\rho}_{\delta}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{\gamma}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}))}
are bounded
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost surely (independently of
\delta
). Combining this with Lemma
8.4
establishes (
148
).

Proof of (
149
).
Using the renormalized continuity equation, we established the convergence of certain Carathéodory functions composed with
\hat{\rho}_{\delta}
in Lemma
8.6
, and we will combine the result of this lemma with an approximation and density argument to conclude (
149
). This is in the spirit of the proof of Proposition 4.4.12 in
[
7
]
, adapted in the current setting to handle difficulties arising from the moving domain. Recall from Lemma
8.6
that we have established convergence of Lipschitz functions of
\hat{\rho}_{\delta}
. Specifically, given an arbitrary
C^{1}
function
b:{\mathbb{R}}\to{\mathbb{R}}
with
b(0)=0
such that
b^{\prime}(w)=0
for
|w|
sufficiently large, we have that:

(151)

\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}b(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})\to\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\eta^{*}}}\overline{b(\rho)}\quad\text{ in }C_{w}(0,T;L^{\gamma}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})),\quad\text{$\tilde{\mathbb{P}}$-almost surely}.

From (
151
) and the compact embedding
C_{w}(0,T;L^{\gamma}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}))\subset\subset L^{2}(0,T;H^{-\sigma}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}))
for
\sigma>\frac{1}{6}
:

(152)

\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\hat{\eta}_{\delta}^{*}}}b(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})\to\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\eta^{*}}}\overline{b(\rho)},\quad\text{ $\tilde{\mathbb{P}}$-almost surely and strongly in $L^{2}(0,T;H^{-\sigma}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}))$ for $\sigma>\frac{1}{6}$.}

Note that, for any Lipschitz function
B:{\mathbb{R}}^{3}\to{\mathbb{R}}
,
B(\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\eta^{*}}}{\boldsymbol{u}})\in L^{2}(0,T;H^{\sigma}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}))

\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost surely for some
\sigma>\frac{1}{6}
sufficiently small by Theorem
8.2
, since
{\boldsymbol{u}}\in L^{2}(0,T;H^{1}(\mathcal{O}_{\eta^{*}}))
and
\eta^{*}\in C(0,T;H^{s}(\Gamma))
are bounded
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost surely, for
3/2<s<2
. Therefore, we have the following strong convergence for
l>\frac{5}{2}
,

(153)

\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}b(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})B(\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\eta^{*}}}{\boldsymbol{u}})=\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\hat{\eta}_{\delta}^{*}}}b(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})B({\boldsymbol{u}})\to\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\eta^{*}}}\overline{b(\rho)}B({\boldsymbol{u}}),\quad\tilde{\mathbb{P}}\text{-almost surely in $L^{2}(0,T;H^{-l}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}))$},

because, by the weak convergence result in Theorem
8.1
, we have
{\boldsymbol{u}}={\boldsymbol{u}}\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\eta^{*}}}
.

Finally, we claim that

(154)

\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}b(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})B(\boldsymbol{u})\to\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\eta^{*}}}\overline{b(\rho)B(\boldsymbol{u})},\quad\text{ $\tilde{\mathbb{P}}$-almost surely and strongly in $L^{2}(0,T;H^{-l}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}))$,}

where
\overline{b(\rho)B(\boldsymbol{u})}
is the weak limit of
b(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})B(\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{\delta})
in
L^{\min(\gamma,6)}([0,T]\times\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})
obtained from (
101
). This will follow from (
153
) once we show that
\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\eta^{*}}}\overline{b(\rho)}B({\boldsymbol{u}})
is the same as
\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\eta^{*}}}\overline{b(\rho)B({\boldsymbol{u}})}
, where we recall from (
101
) that the
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost sure weak convergences in (
143
) hold and furthermore,

b(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})B(\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{\delta})\rightharpoonup\overline{b(\rho)B({\boldsymbol{u}})},\quad\text{$\tilde{\mathbb{P}}$-almost surely, weakly in $L^{\min(\gamma,6)}((0,T)\times\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})$}.

By the boundedness of
B
and (
138
) we have for
1\leq r<\min(\gamma,6)
that:

(155)

\displaystyle\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\hat{\eta}_{\delta}^{*}}}b(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})B({\boldsymbol{u}})\rightharpoonup\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\eta^{*}}}\overline{b(\rho)}B({\boldsymbol{u}}),\quad\text{$\tilde{\mathbb{P}}$-almost surely, weakly in $L^{r}((0,T)\times\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})$},

(156)

\displaystyle\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\hat{\eta}_{\delta}^{*}}}b(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})B(\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{\delta})\rightharpoonup\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\eta^{*}}}\overline{b(\rho)B({\boldsymbol{u}})},\quad\text{$\tilde{\mathbb{P}}$-almost surely, weakly in $L^{r}((0,T)\times\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})$,}

where in the second convergence, we used that
\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}B(\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{\delta})=\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}B(\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{\delta})
since
B
is defined pointwise
B:{\mathbb{R}}^{3}\to{\mathbb{R}}
. For any test function
\varphi\in C_{c}^{\infty}([0,T]\times\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})
:

\displaystyle\left|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}b(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})\Big(B({\boldsymbol{u}})-B(\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{\delta})\Big)\varphi\right|\leq\text{Lip}(b)\cdot\text{Lip}(B)\cdot\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}([0,T]\times\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})}

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}\hat{\rho}_{\delta}|{\boldsymbol{u}}-\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{\delta}|

(157)

\displaystyle\leq\text{Lip}(b)\cdot\text{Lip}(B)\cdot\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}([0,T]\times\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})}\cdot\|\hat{\rho}_{\delta}\|^{1/2}_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{1}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}))}

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}\hat{\rho}_{\delta}|{\boldsymbol{u}}-\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{\delta}|^{2}\to 0

\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost surely, by the
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost sure boundedness of
\|\hat{\rho}_{\delta}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{1}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}))}\leq C(\tilde{\omega})
and Lemma
8.4
. Thus, we conclude from (
155
) and (
156
) that
\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\eta^{*}}}\overline{b(\rho)}B({\boldsymbol{u}})=\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\eta^{*}}}\overline{b(\rho)B({\boldsymbol{u}})}
, from which the desired convergence (
154
) follows.

Now that (
154
) is established, the desired
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost sure convergence in (
149
) follows by an approximation argument, by approximating
f_{k}(\rho,\rho\boldsymbol{u})
by finite sums of the form
\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{m}b_{m}(\rho)B_{m}(\boldsymbol{u})
almost surely in the
L^{2}(0,T;H^{-l}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}))
norm, where
b_{m}
and
B_{m}
are bounded Lipschitz functions with
b_{m}(0)=0
and
b_{m}^{\prime}(w)=0
for sufficiently large
|w|
.
∎

8.6.
Construction of test functions

The aim of this section is to construct smooth test functions for the weak formulation (
109
) in order to pass
\delta\to 0
. Recall that this formulation contains a term penalizing the boundary behavior of the fluid and structure velocities. Hence, we must construct test functions that satisfy the kinematic coupling condition so that this penalty term drops out. We must also ensure that these test functions are appropriately adapted so that the stochastic integrals appearing in the equation (
109
) are well-defined.

For that purpose, we begin by considering an
(\hat{\mathcal{F}}_{t})_{t\geq 0}-
adapted process
{\boldsymbol{q}}
taking
C^{1}
-paths in
C^{\infty}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha})
such that
{\boldsymbol{q}}|_{\Gamma_{\eta^{*}}}=\psi{\bf e}_{z}
for some
\psi\in C^{\infty}(\Gamma)
. Hence
({\boldsymbol{q}},\psi)
is an admissible test pair for the limiting equations. However,
(\boldsymbol{q},\psi)
might not be an admissible test function for the approximate equation due to the kinematic coupling condition being dependent on the structure displacement. Hence, we construct a sequence of
(\hat{\mathcal{F}}^{\delta}_{t})_{t\geq 0}-
adapted test functions that are admissible for the approximate problem with
\delta>0
, that converge to the target test function
(\boldsymbol{q},\psi)
for the limiting problem as
\delta\to 0
.

To do this, we first define test functions
(\boldsymbol{q}_{\lambda},\psi_{\lambda})
, that approximate
({\boldsymbol{q}},\psi)
as
\lambda\to 1
, such that
\boldsymbol{q}_{\lambda}
is smooth on the entire domain
{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}
and is equal to
\psi_{\lambda}\boldsymbol{e}_{z}
on
\Gamma_{\eta^{*}}
.
In particular, we define a random function
\tilde{\boldsymbol{q}}_{\lambda}
on
[0,T]\times\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}
by

\tilde{\boldsymbol{q}}_{\lambda}(x,y,z):=\boldsymbol{q}(x,y,\lambda z),\quad\text{ if }0\leq z\leq\lambda^{-1}(1+\eta^{*}(x,y)),

(158)

\tilde{\boldsymbol{q}}_{\lambda}(x,y,z):=\psi(x,y)\boldsymbol{e}_{z},\quad\text{ if }z\geq\lambda^{-1}(1+\eta^{*}(x,y)).

See Fig.
3
for a realization.
Now, thanks to the fact that

1+\eta^{*}\geq\alpha>0,
we can find a constant
\sigma_{\lambda}>0
, depending only on
\alpha,\lambda
, such that (see
[
42
]
)

(159)

\begin{split}&\sigma_{\lambda}\leq\text{distance between the two curves $1+\eta^{*}$ and $\lambda^{-1}(1+\eta^{*})$},\\
&\sigma_{\lambda}\to 0\text{ as the parameter }\lambda\to 1.\end{split}

Next we smooth out
\tilde{\boldsymbol{q}}_{\lambda}
by defining,

(160)

\boldsymbol{q}_{\lambda}:=\tilde{\boldsymbol{q}}_{\lambda}\ast\zeta_{\sigma_{\lambda}},

where
\zeta_{\sigma_{\lambda}}
is the standard rescaled three-dimensional convolution kernel supported in a ball of radius
\sigma_{\lambda}
. Then, we note that for all
\lambda\in(1,2)
,
\boldsymbol{q}_{\lambda}|_{\Gamma_{\eta^{*}}}=\psi_{\lambda}\boldsymbol{e}_{z}
, where

(161)

\psi_{\lambda}=\psi\ast\zeta_{\sigma_{\lambda}}.

Hence,
(\boldsymbol{q}_{\lambda},\psi_{\lambda})
is another admissible test function for the limiting weak formulation.

Next, we will approximate this
(\hat{\mathcal{F}}_{t})_{t\in[0,T]}
-adapted process by
(\hat{\mathcal{F}}^{\delta}_{t})_{t\in[0,T]}
-adapted processes. In fact, due to Proposition 3.3.2 in
[
13
]
, there exist
(\hat{\mathcal{F}}^{\delta}_{t})_{t\in[0,T]}
-adapted processes
{\boldsymbol{q}}_{\lambda,\delta}
in
C^{1}(0,T;H^{k}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))
and
\psi_{\delta,\lambda}
in
C^{1}(0,T;H^{k}(\Gamma))
, for any
k\geq 0
such that

(162)

\begin{split}&{\boldsymbol{q}}_{\lambda,\delta}\to{\boldsymbol{q}}_{\lambda}\quad\text{almost surely in }C^{1}(0,T;C^{k}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))\\
&\psi_{\lambda,\delta}\to\psi_{\lambda}\quad\text{almost surely in }C^{1}(0,T;C^{k}(\Gamma)).\end{split}

These processes may not satisfy the kinematic coupling condition.
To construct test functions for the approximate weak formulation (
109
) which approximate the limiting test function
(\boldsymbol{q}_{\lambda},\psi_{\lambda})
for
\lambda>1
, we introduce an arbitrary but fixed parameter
\kappa>0
. The corresponding fluid test function will be built so that it transitions smoothly, in a layer of width
\kappa
, from
{\boldsymbol{q}}_{\lambda,\delta}
in the interior of
{\mathcal{O}}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}
to
\psi_{\lambda,\delta}
on the fluid-structure interface
\Gamma_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}
which enforces the kinematic coupling condition along
\Gamma_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}
,
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost surely.

We begin the construction by recalling from Section
4.1
: since for some
s\in(3/2,2)
, we have
\|\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Gamma)}<\frac{1}{\alpha}
and thus
\|\eta^{*}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Gamma)}<\frac{1}{\alpha}
for almost any
\omega\in\tilde{\Omega}
and
t\in[0,T]
,
we can construct smooth bounding functions bounding functions
a^{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}_{\kappa}
and
b^{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}_{\kappa}
for each
\delta>0
. Furthermore, we recall that
a^{\eta^{*}}_{\kappa}
and
b^{\eta^{*}}_{\kappa}
for the limiting structure displacement
\eta^{*}
,
satisfy the properties (
15
), (
16
), (
17
) and Lemma
4.1
.

We further recall from Section
4.1
that
\phi_{0}:{\mathbb{R}}\to{\mathbb{R}}
is chosen to be a smooth function on
[0,\infty)
such that
\phi_{0}(w)=1
for
w\leq 1/4
,
\phi_{0}(w)=0
for
w\geq 3/4
, and
\phi_{0}
is decreasing on
[1/4,3/4]
. We then define a smooth function
\varphi_{\delta,\kappa}
for each
\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}
and each parameter
\kappa
by:

(163)

\displaystyle\varphi_{\delta,\kappa}(t,x,y,z)=\phi_{0}\left(\frac{z-b^{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}_{\kappa}(t,x,y)}{C_{\alpha}\kappa^{1/2}}\right),

where the constant
C_{\alpha}
