is as in (
13
).

Now, given the smooth test pair
(\boldsymbol{q}_{\lambda,\delta},\psi_{\lambda,\delta})
on the extended domain, approximating
(\boldsymbol{q}_{\lambda},\psi_{\lambda})
(and thus
(\boldsymbol{q},\psi)
) for the limiting weak formulation, we can construct a family of test functions
(\boldsymbol{q}_{\lambda,\delta,\kappa},\psi_{\lambda,\delta})
for
\lambda\in(1,2)
,
\delta>0
, and
\kappa>0
, which satisfy the kinematic coupling condition along the approximate moving boundary

\boldsymbol{q}_{\lambda,\delta,\kappa}|_{\Gamma_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}=\psi_{\lambda,\delta}\boldsymbol{e}_{z},

as follows, using the previously constructed function
\varphi_{\delta,\kappa}
from (
163
):

\boldsymbol{q}_{\lambda,\delta,\kappa}=\boldsymbol{q}_{\lambda,\delta}+(\psi_{\lambda,\delta}\boldsymbol{e}_{z}-\boldsymbol{q}_{\lambda,\delta})(1-\varphi_{\delta,\kappa}),\qquad\psi_{\lambda,\delta}=\psi_{\lambda}.

Note that this construction ensures that
(\boldsymbol{q}_{\lambda,\delta,\kappa},\psi_{\lambda,\delta})
is adapted to
(\hat{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{\delta})_{t\in[0,T]}
and satisfies the kinematic coupling condition so that the penalty in the weak formulation drops out for this pair of test functions.

Step 1:
\delta\to 0
.

We will fix
\kappa
and
\lambda
and handle the limit as
\delta\to 0
first, which requires defining the following limiting function
\varphi_{\kappa}
analogously to (
164
), corresponding to the limiting structure displacement
\eta^{*}
, where we recall that
b^{\eta^{*}}_{\kappa}
bounds from below the limiting structure displacement
\eta^{*}
:

(164)

\varphi_{\kappa}(t,x,y,z)=\phi_{0}\left(\frac{z-b^{\eta^{*}}_{\kappa}(t,x,y)}{C_{\alpha}\kappa^{1/2}}\right).

and we similarly define

(165)

\boldsymbol{q}_{\lambda,\kappa}=\boldsymbol{q}_{\lambda}+(\psi_{\lambda}\boldsymbol{e}_{z}-\boldsymbol{q}_{\lambda})(1-\varphi_{\kappa}),\qquad\psi_{\lambda,\kappa}=\psi_{\lambda}.

We have the following convergence result for the convergence of the approximate test functions
(\boldsymbol{q}_{\lambda,\delta,\kappa},\psi_{\lambda,\delta})
satisfying
\boldsymbol{q}_{\lambda,\delta,\kappa}|_{\Gamma_{\hat{\eta}_{\delta}^{*}}}=\psi_{\lambda,\delta}\boldsymbol{e}_{z}
almost surely, to a limiting test function
(\boldsymbol{q}_{\lambda,\kappa},\psi_{\lambda})
in the limit as
\delta\to 0
which satisfies
\boldsymbol{q}_{\lambda,\kappa}|_{\Gamma_{\eta^{*}}}=\psi_{\lambda}\boldsymbol{e}_{z}
almost surely.

Lemma 8.8
.

For all positive integers
k
and fixed parameters
\kappa>0
and
\lambda\in(1/2,1)
,

(\boldsymbol{q}_{\lambda,\delta,\kappa},\psi_{\lambda,\delta})\to(\boldsymbol{q}_{\lambda,\kappa},\psi_{\lambda})\quad\text{almost surely in }C([0,T];C^{k}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})\times C^{k}(\Gamma)),\quad\text{ as }\delta\to 0.

Proof.

Observe that thanks to Lemma
4.1
and the fact that by definition,
\phi_{0}:{\mathbb{R}}\to{\mathbb{R}}
is smooth, we have that
\varphi_{\kappa,\delta}\to\varphi_{\kappa}
almost surely in
C([0,T];C^{k}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}))
for any
k\geq 0
as
\delta\to 0
. This, combined with (
162
), immediately gives us the desired result.
∎

Observe that, for any
\delta,\kappa,\lambda>0
, the pair
({\boldsymbol{q}}_{\lambda,\delta,\kappa},\psi_{\lambda,\delta})
is an admissible test function for the coupled equation (
109
). We now apply a special version of the Itô formula, which can be proven by using a regularization argument as outlined in Lemma 5.1 in
[
12
]
, to obtain that:

(166)

\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}\hat{\rho}_{\delta}(t)\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{\delta}(t)\cdot\boldsymbol{q}_{\lambda,\delta,\kappa}(t)+\int_{\Gamma}\hat{v}_{\delta}(t)\psi_{\lambda,\delta}(t)=\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}\boldsymbol{p}_{0,\delta}\cdot\boldsymbol{q}_{\lambda,\delta,\kappa}(0)+\int_{\Gamma}v_{0}\psi_{\lambda,\delta}(0)\\
+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}\hat{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0,\delta}\cdot\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{q}_{\lambda,\delta,\kappa}+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Gamma}\hat{v}_{\delta}\partial_{t}\psi_{\lambda,\delta}+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta}\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{\delta}\otimes\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{\delta}):\nabla\boldsymbol{q}_{\lambda,\delta,\kappa}+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}\Big(a\hat{\rho}_{\delta}^{\gamma}+\delta\hat{\rho}_{\delta}^{\beta}\Big)(\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{q}_{\lambda,\delta,\kappa})\\
-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}{\mu^{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}_{\delta}}\nabla\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{\delta}:\nabla\boldsymbol{q}_{\lambda,\delta,\kappa}-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}{\lambda^{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}_{\delta}}\text{div}(\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{\delta})\text{div}(\boldsymbol{q}_{\lambda,\delta,\kappa})-\frac{1}{\delta}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Gamma}(\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{\delta}|_{\Gamma_{\hat{\eta}_{\delta}^{*}}}-\hat{v}_{\delta}\boldsymbol{e}_{z})\cdot(\boldsymbol{q}_{\lambda,\delta,\kappa}|_{\Gamma_{\hat{\eta}_{\delta}^{*}}}-\psi_{\lambda,\delta}\boldsymbol{e}_{z})\\
-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Gamma}\nabla\hat{v}_{\delta}\cdot\nabla\psi_{\lambda,\delta}-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Gamma}\nabla\hat{\eta}_{\delta}\cdot\nabla\psi_{\lambda,\delta}-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Gamma}\Delta\hat{\eta}_{\delta}\Delta\psi_{\lambda,\delta}\\
+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}\boldsymbol{F}_{\delta}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta},\hat{\rho}_{\delta}\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{\delta})\cdot\boldsymbol{q}_{\lambda,\delta,\kappa}d\hat{W}^{1}_{\delta}(t)+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Gamma}G(\hat{\eta}_{\delta},\hat{v}_{\delta})\psi_{\lambda,\delta}d\hat{W}^{2}_{\delta}(t),

holds
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost surely and for almost every
t\in[0,T]
.

We will first pass
\delta\to 0
using Theorem
8.1
, the convergence result for test functions in Lemma
8.8
, and convergence result for the stochastic integrals Lemma
8.7
. This yields:

(167)

\begin{split}&\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\eta^{*}}}\rho(t){{\boldsymbol{u}}}(t)\cdot\boldsymbol{q}_{\lambda,\kappa}(t)+\int_{\Gamma}v(t)\psi_{\lambda}(t)=\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\eta_{0}}}\boldsymbol{p}_{0}\cdot\boldsymbol{q}_{\lambda,\kappa}(0)+\int_{\Gamma}v_{0}\psi_{\lambda}(0)\\
&+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\eta^{*}}}\rho{{\boldsymbol{u}}}\cdot\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{q}_{\lambda,\kappa}+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\eta^{*}}}(\rho{{\boldsymbol{u}}}\otimes{{\boldsymbol{u}}}):\nabla\boldsymbol{q}_{\lambda,\kappa}+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{{{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}}}\bar{p}(\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{q}_{\lambda,\kappa})-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\eta^{*}}}\mu\nabla{{\boldsymbol{u}}}:\nabla\boldsymbol{q}_{\lambda,\kappa}\\
&-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\eta^{*}}}\lambda\text{div}({{\boldsymbol{u}}})\text{div}(\boldsymbol{q}_{\lambda,\kappa})+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Gamma}v\partial_{t}\psi_{\lambda}-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Gamma}\nabla v\cdot\nabla\psi_{\lambda}-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Gamma}\nabla\eta\cdot\nabla\psi_{\lambda}-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Gamma}\Delta\eta\Delta\psi_{\lambda}\\
&+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}\overline{\boldsymbol{F}(\rho,\rho{{\boldsymbol{u}}})}\cdot\boldsymbol{q}_{\lambda,\kappa}d\hat{W}^{1}+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Gamma}G(\eta,v)\psi_{\lambda}d\hat{W}^{2},\end{split}

holds
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost surely for almost every
t\in(0,\tau^{\eta})
and any specific choice of parameters
\lambda\in(1,2)
and
\kappa>0
.
To handle the convergence of the terms involving the viscosity coefficients
\mu^{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}_{\delta}
and
\lambda^{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}_{\delta}
, we recall that
\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}\nabla\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{\delta}
converges weakly to
\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\eta^{*}}}\nabla{\boldsymbol{u}}
in
L^{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha})
due to Theorem
8.1
, so since
\mu^{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}_{\delta}=\mu
on
\mathcal{O}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}
, we calculate

\mu\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\hat{\eta}_{\delta}^{*}}}\nabla\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{\delta}:\nabla\boldsymbol{q}_{\lambda,\delta,\kappa}\to\mu\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\eta^{*}}}\nabla{\boldsymbol{u}}:\nabla\boldsymbol{q}_{\lambda,\kappa},\qquad a.e.\,\,t\in[0,T].

The remaining part of the integral outside will vanish in the limit, since

\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}\setminus\mathcal{O}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}\mu^{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}_{\delta}\nabla\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{\delta}:\nabla\boldsymbol{q}_{\lambda,\delta,\kappa}=\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}(\sqrt{\mu^{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}_{\delta}}\nabla\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{\delta}):\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}\setminus\mathcal{O}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}(\sqrt{\mu^{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}_{\delta}}\nabla\boldsymbol{q}_{\lambda,\delta,\kappa})\to 0\quad\text{ almost surely},

by the weak convergence of
\sqrt{\mu_{\delta}^{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}\nabla\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{\delta}
in
L^{2}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})
and the fact that
\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}\setminus\mathcal{O}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}(\sqrt{\mu^{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}_{\delta}}\nabla\boldsymbol{q}_{\lambda,\delta,\kappa})\to 0
in
L^{q}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})
, for any
q\in(1,\infty)
by the properties of the extension of the viscosity coefficients (
31
) and the almost sure uniform pointwise convergence of
\nabla\boldsymbol{q}_{\lambda,\delta,\kappa}
in Lemma
8.8
.

Step 2: Choosing an appropriate
\kappa
.
Next, we will fix a specific and strategic choice of
\kappa
in (
167
) for the test functions
(\boldsymbol{q}_{\lambda,\kappa},\psi_{\lambda})
, which are admissible test functions for the limiting weak formulation satisfying
\boldsymbol{q}_{\lambda,\kappa}|_{\Gamma_{{\eta}^{*}}}=\psi_{\lambda}\boldsymbol{e}_{z}
.

Given
\lambda\in(1,2)
, we choose
\kappa
such that the curve
b^{\eta^{*}}_{\kappa}+\frac{1}{4}C_{\alpha}\kappa^{\frac{1}{2}}
is above the
set
\bigcup_{w\in\Gamma^{\lambda}_{\eta^{*}}}B(w,\sigma_{\lambda})
, where
B(w,\sigma_{\lambda})
denotes the 3D ball of radius
\sigma_{\lambda}
centered at
w
and
\Gamma^{\lambda}_{\eta^{*}}=\{(x,y,\frac{1}{\lambda}(1+\eta^{*})):(x,y)\in\Gamma\}
; see Fig.
3
.

Figure 3.
Choice of
\kappa

This will ensure that that for all
(x,y,z)\in\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}
, either
\varphi_{\kappa}=1
or
\boldsymbol{q}_{\lambda}=\psi_{\lambda}\boldsymbol{e}_{z}
. By the definition in (
165
), this means that for this specific choice of
\kappa
, we have that

\boldsymbol{q}_{\lambda,\kappa}=\boldsymbol{q}_{\lambda}

and hence the weak formulation (
167
) also holds, almost surely for almost every
t\in(0,\tau^{\eta})
, with
\boldsymbol{q}_{\lambda}
in place of
\boldsymbol{q}_{\lambda,\kappa}
:

(168)

\begin{split}&\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\eta^{*}}}\rho(t){{\boldsymbol{u}}}(t)\cdot\boldsymbol{q}_{\lambda}(t)+\int_{\Gamma}v(t)\psi_{\lambda}(t)=\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\eta_{0}}}{\boldsymbol{p}_{0}}\cdot\boldsymbol{q}_{\lambda}(0)+\int_{\Gamma}v_{0}\psi_{\lambda}(0)+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\eta^{*}}}\rho{{\boldsymbol{u}}}\cdot\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{q}_{\lambda}\\
&+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\eta^{*}}}(\rho{{\boldsymbol{u}}}\otimes{{\boldsymbol{u}}}):\nabla\boldsymbol{q}_{\lambda}+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}}\bar{p}(\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{q}_{\lambda})-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\eta^{*}}}\mu\nabla{{\boldsymbol{u}}}:\nabla\boldsymbol{q}_{\lambda}-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\eta^{*}}}\lambda\text{div}({{\boldsymbol{u}}})\text{div}(\boldsymbol{q}_{\lambda})\\
&+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Gamma}v\partial_{t}\psi_{\lambda}-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Gamma}\nabla v\cdot\nabla\psi_{\lambda}-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Gamma}\nabla\eta\cdot\nabla\psi_{\lambda}-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Gamma}\Delta\eta\Delta\psi_{\lambda}\\
&+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Gamma}G(\eta,v)\psi_{\lambda}d\hat{W}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}\overline{\boldsymbol{F}(\rho,\rho{{\boldsymbol{u}}})}\cdot\boldsymbol{q}_{\lambda}d\hat{W}^{1},\end{split}

Step 3:
\lambda\to 1
.
Finally, we pass to the limit as
\lambda\to 1
in the “squeezed” test functions
(\boldsymbol{q}_{\lambda},\psi_{\lambda})
, which we recall are defined by squeezing by a factor of
\lambda
and doing a constant extension by
\psi\boldsymbol{e}_{z}
to the maximal domain to get
\tilde{q}_{\lambda}
defined in (
158
), then using a spatial convolution to get a smooth function
\boldsymbol{q}_{\lambda}
as defined in (
160
). It is clear that for
\lambda\in(1,2)
,
\|\tilde{\boldsymbol{q}}_{\lambda}\|_{W^{1,\infty}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})}\leq\lambda\|\boldsymbol{q}\|_{W^{1,\infty}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})}
, so we have that
\|\boldsymbol{q}_{\lambda}\|_{W^{1,\infty}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})}\leq 2\|\boldsymbol{q}\|_{W^{1,\infty}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})}
after spatial convolution, for
\lambda\in(1,2)
. Furthermore, it is clear that
\boldsymbol{q}_{\lambda}
and
\nabla\boldsymbol{q}_{\lambda}
converge pointwise almost everywhere to
\tilde{\boldsymbol{q}}
and
\nabla\tilde{\boldsymbol{q}}
, where

\tilde{\boldsymbol{q}}|_{\mathcal{O}_{{\eta}^{*}}}=\boldsymbol{q},\qquad\tilde{\boldsymbol{q}}|_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}\setminus\mathcal{O}_{{\eta}^{*}}}=\psi\boldsymbol{e}_{z}.

Hence, we can use dominated convergence to pass to the limit as
\lambda\to 1
in (
168
) in order to obtain that the limiting weak formulation holds almost surely for
(\tilde{\boldsymbol{q}},\psi)
, which although is not smooth is piecewise smooth. Since
\tilde{\boldsymbol{q}}
agrees with
\boldsymbol{q}
on
\mathcal{O}_{{\eta}^{*}}
, the limiting weak formulation will hold almost surely with the originally given adapted test function
(\boldsymbol{q},\psi)
satisfying
\boldsymbol{q}|_{\Gamma_{{\eta}^{*}}}=\psi\boldsymbol{e}_{z}
, once we show that the pressure integral involving
\bar{p}
is the same as a pressure integral over
\mathcal{O}_{{\eta}^{*}}
, which will require us showing that the pressure vanishes outside
\mathcal{O}_{{\eta}^{*}}
. We will show this in Section
8.8
, by showing that in fact
\overline{p}=\rho^{\gamma}
and deriving the renormalized continuity equation (
10
). In addition, notice that some terms in the limiting weak formulation still contain
\eta^{*}
instead of
\eta
, however thanks to (
107
) we know that
\eta
and
\eta^{*}
agree at least until the almost surely positive stopping time
\tau^{\eta}
.

8.7.
Renormalized solutions

The aim of this section is to show that the limiting random variables
{\boldsymbol{u}},\rho
satisfy continuity equations in the renormalized sense, that is, the equation:

(169)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}}b(\rho)(\partial_{t}\phi+{\boldsymbol{u}}\cdot\nabla\phi)=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}}(b^{\prime}(\rho)\rho-b(\rho))(\nabla\cdot{\boldsymbol{u}})\phi,

holds
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost surely for almost every
t\in(0,T)
and for any
\phi\in C^{\infty}_{c}((0,T)\times{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha})
and
b\in C({\mathbb{R}})
with
b^{\prime}(z)=0
when
z\geq M_{b}
.
The idea is to choose an appropriate
b
in the approximate normalized continuity equation satisfied by the new random variables
\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{\delta},\hat{\rho}_{\delta}
which, at this stage, states that (
142
)
holds
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost surely for almost every
t\in(0,T)
and for any
\phi
in
C^{\infty}_{c}((0,T)\times{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha})
and
b\in C(\mathbb{R})
.
To that end, we define

(170)

\begin{split}T(z)=\begin{cases}\begin{array}[]{cc}z&z\in[0,1]\\
2&z\geq 3\\
\text{concave otherwise}.\end{array}\end{cases},\quad\text{and }\quad T_{k}(z)=kT(\frac{z}{k}).\end{split}

We will thus choose
b=T_{k}
in (
142
), pass
\delta\to 0
and then pass
k\to\infty
. To analyze these limits we will first study the relevant properties of
T_{k}
.

First, observe, due to Corollary 6.4 in
[
8
]
, which is a consequence of a fundamental theorem on Young measures, that,

(171)

\displaystyle T_{k}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})\to\overline{T_{k}(\rho)}\text{ in }C_{w}([0,T];L^{p}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha})),\quad\tilde{\mathbb{P}}\text{-almost surely,}\quad\forall p\in[1,\infty).

Lemma 8.9
.

We have the following convergence result for fixed
k
, as
\delta\to 0
:

(172)

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta}^{\gamma}+\delta\hat{\rho}_{\delta}^{\beta}-\lambda^{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}_{\delta}\nabla\cdot\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{\delta})T_{k}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})\,dxdt\to\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}}}(\bar{p}-\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\eta^{*}}}\lambda\nabla\cdot{\boldsymbol{u}})\overline{T_{k}(\rho)}\,dxdt.

Proof.

We consider a process
\varphi_{\delta}\in C^{\infty}_{c}({\mathcal{O}}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}})
such that
\varphi_{\delta}\to\varphi\in C^{\infty}_{c}({\mathcal{O}}_{\eta^{*}})
almost surely.
First, as earlier, we apply Ito’s formula with
{\Psi}_{k}(\rho,{\boldsymbol{u}})=\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}}{\boldsymbol{u}}\cdot\varphi\nabla\Delta^{-1}\varphi T_{k}(\rho)
to the approximate (
91
) and limiting momentum equation (
167
). Then using the convergence results obtained in Theorem
8.1
, (
171
) and (
127
)
we can see that,

(173)

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}}\varphi_{\delta}^{2}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta}^{\gamma}+\delta\hat{\rho}_{\delta}^{\beta}-\lambda^{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}_{\delta}\nabla\cdot\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{\delta})T_{k}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})\,\to\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}}\varphi^{2}(\bar{p}-\lambda\nabla\cdot{\boldsymbol{u}})\overline{T_{k}(\rho)}\,.

The proof of (
173
) is standard and so we skip it; see e.g.
[
8
,
10
]
. In (
173
), we then take
\varphi_{\delta}\in C^{\infty}_{c}({\mathcal{O}}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}})
such that
\varphi_{\delta}=1
on
A^{l}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}
, (see Definition (
113
)) for some
l>0
to be chosen later, that converges
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost surely to some
\varphi\in C^{\infty}_{c}({\mathcal{O}}_{\eta^{*}})
such that
\varphi=1
on
A^{l}_{\eta^{*}}
. Then we write,

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta}^{\gamma}+\delta\hat{\rho}_{\delta}^{\beta}-\lambda^{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}_{\delta}\nabla\cdot\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{\delta})T_{k}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})\,-\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}}(\bar{p}-\lambda\nabla\cdot{\boldsymbol{u}})\overline{T_{k}(\rho)}\,

\displaystyle=\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}\cap(A^{l}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}})^{c}}(1-\varphi_{\delta})(\hat{\rho}_{\delta}^{\gamma}+\delta\hat{\rho}_{\delta}^{\beta}-\lambda^{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}_{\delta}\nabla\cdot\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{\delta})T_{k}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})\,+\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}\varphi_{\delta}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta}^{\gamma}+\delta\hat{\rho}_{\delta}^{\beta}-\lambda^{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}_{\delta}\nabla\cdot\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{\delta})T_{k}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})\,

\displaystyle-\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\eta^{*}}\cap(A^{l}_{\eta^{*}})^{c}}(1-\varphi)(\bar{p}-\lambda\nabla\cdot{\boldsymbol{u}})\overline{T_{k}(\rho)}\,-\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\eta^{*}}}\varphi(\bar{p}-\lambda\nabla\cdot{\boldsymbol{u}})\overline{T_{k}(\rho)}.\,

Observe that for any
\epsilon>0
, Proposition
8.4
and an equivalent statement for
\bar{p}
, give the existence of some
l>0
for which,

\displaystyle\left|\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}\cap(A^{l}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}})^{c}}(1-\varphi_{\delta})(\hat{\rho}_{\delta}^{\gamma}+\delta\hat{\rho}_{\delta}^{\beta})T_{k}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})-\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\eta^{*}}\cap(A^{l}_{\eta^{*}})^{c}}(1-\varphi)\bar{p}\overline{T_{k}(\rho)}\right|<\epsilon.

This observation combined with (
173
) then gives us the desired result (
172
).
∎

Next, using the convergence result (
172
) we will next prove that the oscillation defect measure, first used in
[
20
]
, is bounded in expectation.

Lemma 8.10
.

For the oscillation defect measure we have,

osc_{\gamma+1}[\hat{\rho}_{\delta}\to\rho]({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}):=\limsup_{\delta\to 0}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}}|T_{k}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})-T_{k}(\rho)|^{\gamma+1}\leq C.

Proof.

Observe that,

\displaystyle\limsup_{\delta\to 0}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}}\left((\hat{\rho}_{\delta}^{\gamma}+\delta\hat{\rho}^{\beta}_{\delta})T_{k}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})-\bar{p}\overline{T_{k}(\rho)}\right)

\displaystyle=\limsup_{\delta\to 0}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}}\left((\hat{\rho}_{\delta}^{\gamma}+\delta\hat{\rho}^{\beta}_{\delta}-(\rho^{\gamma}+\delta\rho^{\beta}))(T_{k}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})-{T_{k}(\rho)})\right)

\displaystyle+\limsup_{\delta\to 0}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta}^{\gamma}+\delta\hat{\rho}^{\beta}_{\delta}-\bar{p})T_{k}(\rho)+(\rho^{\gamma}+\delta\rho^{\beta})(T_{k}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})-\overline{T_{k}(\rho)})

\displaystyle+\limsup_{\delta\to 0}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}}(\bar{p}-(\rho^{\gamma}+\delta\rho^{\beta}))(T_{k}(\rho)-\overline{T_{k}(\rho)})

thanks to (
127
), (
171
), and Theorem 11.27 in
[
19
]
, we obtain

\displaystyle\geq\limsup_{\delta\to 0}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}}\left((\hat{\rho}_{\delta}^{\gamma}+\delta\hat{\rho}^{\beta}_{\delta}-(\rho^{\gamma}+\delta\rho^{\beta}))(T_{k}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})-{T_{k}(\rho)})\right)

since
z\mapsto z^{\gamma}
is convex and
T_{k}
is concave,
(z^{\gamma}-y^{\gamma})(T_{k}(z)-T_{k}(y))\geq|T_{k}(z)-T_{k}(y)|^{\gamma+1}
for
z,y\geq 0
we have

\displaystyle\geq\limsup_{\delta\to 0}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\|T_{k}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})-T_{k}(\rho)\|^{\gamma+1}_{L^{\gamma+1}((0,T)\times{\mathcal{O}})}.

On the other hand, since
\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\eta^{*}}}\lambda(\nabla\cdot{\boldsymbol{u}})
is the weak limit of
\lambda^{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}_{\delta}(\nabla\cdot\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{\delta})
in
L^{2}(\tilde{\Omega},L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha})))
, we obtain

\displaystyle\limsup_{\delta\to 0}

\displaystyle\left|\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}}\lambda^{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}_{\delta}(\nabla\cdot\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{\delta})T_{k}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})-\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\eta^{*}}}\lambda(\nabla\cdot{\boldsymbol{u}})\overline{T_{k}(\rho)}\right|

\displaystyle=\limsup_{\delta\to 0}\left|\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}}\lambda^{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}_{\delta}(\nabla\cdot\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{\delta})T_{k}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})-\lambda^{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}_{\delta}(\nabla\cdot\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{\delta})\overline{T_{k}(\rho)}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\limsup_{\delta\to 0}(\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\|\lambda^{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}_{\delta}\nabla\cdot\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{\delta}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))})^{\frac{1}{2}}(\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\|T_{k}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})-\overline{T_{k}(\rho)}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))})^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\limsup_{\delta\to 0}C\|T_{k}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})-T_{k}(\rho)\|_{L^{2}(\tilde{\Omega};L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha})))}

\displaystyle\leq\limsup_{\delta\to 0}C\|T_{k}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})-T_{k}(\rho)\|_{L^{\gamma+1}(\tilde{\Omega}\times(0,T)\times{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha})}.

These bounds and an application of (
172
) thus give us the desired result as follows,

\displaystyle\limsup_{\delta\to 0}\|T_{k}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})-T_{k}(\rho)\|^{\gamma+1}_{L^{\gamma+1}(\tilde{\Omega}\times(0,T)\times{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha})}

\displaystyle\leq\limsup_{\delta\to 0}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}}\left((\hat{\rho}_{\delta}^{\gamma}+\delta\hat{\rho}^{\beta}_{\delta}-\lambda^{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}_{\delta}\nabla\cdot\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{\delta})T_{k}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})-(\bar{p}-\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\eta^{*}}}\lambda\nabla\cdot{\boldsymbol{u}})\overline{T_{k}(\rho)}\right)

\displaystyle+\limsup_{\delta\to 0}|\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}}\lambda^{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}_{\delta}(\nabla\cdot\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{\delta})T_{k}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})-\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\eta^{*}}}\lambda(\nabla\cdot{\boldsymbol{u}})\overline{T_{k}(\rho)}|

\displaystyle\leq C\limsup_{\delta\to 0}\|T_{k}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})-T_{k}(\rho)\|_{L^{\gamma+1}(\tilde{\Omega}\times(0,T)\times{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha})}.

∎

Lemma
8.10
is sufficient to show that the normalized continuity equation (
142
) holds true in the limit i.e. that (
169
) holds true.
As mentioned earlier, we will take
b=T_{k}
in (
142
) where
T_{k}
is defined in (
170
). This yields, for any
\phi\in C^{\infty}(0,T;C^{\infty}_{c}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))
,

(174)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}}T_{k}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})(\partial_{t}\phi+\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{\delta}\cdot\nabla\phi)=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}}(T_{k}^{\prime}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})\hat{\rho}_{\delta}-T_{k}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta}))(\nabla\cdot\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{\delta})\phi.

To pass
\delta\to 0
in the equation above, we rewrite it as,

(175)

\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}T_{k}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})(\partial_{t}\phi+\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{\delta}\cdot\nabla\phi)=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}(T_{k}^{\prime}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})\hat{\rho}_{\delta}-T_{k}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta}))\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}(\nabla\cdot\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{\delta})\phi+E_{\delta,\phi},

where the error term is:

E_{\delta,\phi}:=-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}\setminus\mathcal{O}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}T_{k}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{\delta}\cdot\nabla\phi+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}\setminus\mathcal{O}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}(T_{k}^{\prime}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})\hat{\rho}_{\delta}-T_{k}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta}))(\nabla\cdot\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{\delta})\phi.

Observe that, due to Corollary 6.4 in
[
8
]
, we have the following weak convergence

\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}(T_{k}^{\prime}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})\hat{\rho}_{\delta}-T_{k}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta}))(\nabla\cdot\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{\delta})\rightharpoonup\tilde{T}_{k}\quad\text{ in }L^{2}(\tilde{\Omega}\times(0,T)\times{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}).

We now pass to the limit in (
175
) as
\delta\to 0
.
Using the fact that
E_{\delta,\phi}\to 0
,
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost surely by Lemma
8.5
, and using the notation (
171
), we let
\delta\to 0
in (
175
) and obtain for any
\phi\in C^{\infty}(0,T;C_{c}^{\infty}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))
that

(176)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}}\overline{T_{k}(\rho)}(\partial_{t}\phi+{\boldsymbol{u}}\cdot\nabla\phi)=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}}\tilde{T}_{k}\phi,

holds
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost surely for any
t\in[0,T]
. Note that we used the fact that
\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}T_{k}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{\delta}
converges weakly to
\overline{T_{k}(\rho){\boldsymbol{u}}}

\tilde{{\mathbb{P}}}
-almost surely in
L^{\min(\gamma,2)}([0,T]\times\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})
by (
101
), and then we can show that
\overline{T_{k}(\rho){\boldsymbol{u}}}=\overline{T_{k}(\rho)}{\boldsymbol{u}}
by showing that
\displaystyle\left|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}T_{k}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})({\boldsymbol{u}}-\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{\delta})\varphi\right|\to 0

\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost surely for any test function
\varphi\in C_{c}^{\infty}([0,T]\times\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})
, using an argument similar to (
157
).

Now, by using a standard regularization method (see e.g.
[
20
]
) we write the renormalized equations for
\overline{T_{k}(\rho)}
satisfying (
176
):

(177)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}b(\overline{T_{k}(\rho)})+\text{div}(b(\overline{T_{k}(\rho)}){\boldsymbol{u}})+(b^{\prime}(\overline{T_{k}(\rho)})\overline{T_{k}(\rho)}-b(\overline{T_{k}(\rho)}))(\nabla\cdot{\boldsymbol{u}})+b^{\prime}(\overline{T_{k}(\rho)})\tilde{T}_{k}=0,

holds
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost surely in the sense of distributions on
[0,T]\times{{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}}
.

Our next aim is to pass
k\to\infty
in (
177
).
First, observe that for any
p<\gamma
, thanks to Lemma
8.10
, we have that

\displaystyle\|\overline{T_{k}(\rho)}-\rho\|^{p}_{L^{p}(\tilde{\Omega}\times(0,T)\times{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha})}

\displaystyle\leq\liminf_{\delta\to 0}\|{T_{k}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})}-\hat{\rho}_{\delta}\|^{p}_{L^{p}(\tilde{\Omega}\times(0,T)\times{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha})}\leq C\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\{|\hat{\rho}_{\delta}|\geq k\}}|\hat{\rho}_{\delta}|^{p}

\displaystyle\leq Ck^{{p-\gamma}}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}}|\hat{\rho}_{\delta}|^{\gamma}\to 0,\qquad\text{ as }k\to\infty.

That is, for any
p<\gamma
, we have proved that

(178)

\displaystyle\overline{T_{k}(\rho)}\to\rho\,\,\text{ in }\,\,L^{p}(\tilde{\Omega}\times(0,T)\times{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha})\,\,\text{ as }\,\,k\to\infty.

This convergence result allows us to pass
k\to\infty
in the first three terms on the left-hand side of (
177
).
To deal with the last term on the left-hand side of the equation (
177
), we will next show that

(179)

\displaystyle b^{\prime}(\overline{T_{k}(\rho)})\tilde{T}_{k}\to 0\quad\tilde{\mathbb{P}}\text{-almost surely }\quad\text{ as }k\to\infty

For that purpose, as proposed in
[
20
]
, we consider the set,

(180)

Q_{k,M}:=\{(\omega,t,x)\in\tilde{\Omega}\times(0,T)\times{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha};\,\,\overline{T_{k}(\rho)}\leq M_{b}\},

where
M_{b}
is such that

b^{\prime}(z)=0,\qquad\text{ for }z\geq M_{b}.

Then, we obtain that

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}}b^{\prime}(\overline{T_{k}(\rho)})\tilde{T}_{k}\leq\sup_{z\leq M_{b}}|b^{\prime}(z)|\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}}\mathbbm{1}_{Q_{k,M}}|\tilde{T}_{k}|

\displaystyle\leq\liminf_{\delta\to 0}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}}{\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}}\mathbbm{1}_{Q_{k,M}}|(T_{k}^{\prime}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})\hat{\rho}_{\delta}-T_{k}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta}))(\nabla\cdot\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{\delta})|

\displaystyle\leq\|\nabla\cdot\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{\delta}\|_{L^{2}(\tilde{\Omega};L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}})))}\liminf_{\delta\to 0}\|T_{k}^{\prime}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})\hat{\rho}_{\delta}-T_{k}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})\|^{\frac{\alpha_{0}}{2}}_{L^{1}(\tilde{\Omega}\times(0,T)\times{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha})}\|T_{k}^{\prime}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})\hat{\rho}_{\delta}-T_{k}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})\|^{\frac{(1-\alpha_{0})(1+\gamma)}{2}}_{L^{\gamma+1}(Q_{k,M})}

where
\alpha_{0}=\frac{\gamma-1}{\gamma}
.
Next, note that since we have

\sup_{\delta}\|T_{k}^{\prime}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})\hat{\rho}_{\delta}-T_{k}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})\|_{L^{1}(\tilde{\Omega}\times(0,T)\times{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha})}\leq k^{1-\gamma}\sup_{\delta}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}}\hat{\rho}_{\delta}^{\gamma}\to 0\quad\text{as }k\to\infty,

we will be done with the proof of (
179
) if we show that

(181)

\|T_{k}^{\prime}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})\hat{\rho}_{\delta}-T_{k}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})\|_{L^{\gamma+1}(Q_{k,M})}\leq C.

Indeed, observe that due to the fact that
T_{k}^{\prime}(z)z\leq T_{k}(z)
, we have

\displaystyle\|T_{k}^{\prime}

\displaystyle(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})\hat{\rho}_{\delta}-T_{k}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})\|_{L^{\gamma+1}(Q_{k,M})}\leq 2\|T_{k}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})\|_{L^{\gamma+1}(Q_{k,M})}

\displaystyle\leq 2\left(\|T_{k}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})-T_{k}(\rho)\|_{L^{\gamma+1}(\tilde{\Omega}\times(0,T)\times\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})}+\|T_{k}(\rho)\|_{L^{\gamma+1}(Q_{k,M})}\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2\left(\|T_{k}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})-T_{k}(\rho)\|_{L^{\gamma+1}(\tilde{\Omega}\times(0,T)\times\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})}+\|T_{k}(\rho)-\overline{T_{k}(\rho)}\|_{L^{\gamma+1}(\tilde{\Omega}\times(0,T)\times\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})}+\|\overline{T_{k}(\rho)}\|_{L^{\gamma+1}(Q_{k,M})}\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2\left(\|T_{k}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})-T_{k}(\rho)\|_{L^{\gamma+1}(\tilde{\Omega}\times(0,T)\times\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})}+\|T_{k}(\rho)-\overline{T_{k}(\rho)}\|_{L^{\gamma+1}(\tilde{\Omega}\times(0,T)\times\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})}+M_{b}\right),

where we used the definition of
Q_{k,M}
in (
180
) in the last inequality. In addition, by using Lemma
8.10
, we can immediately bound
\|T_{k}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})-T_{k}(\rho)\|_{L^{\gamma+1}(\tilde{\Omega}\times(0,T)\times\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})}\leq C
independently of
\delta
. To show a similar bound that
\|T_{k}(\rho)-\overline{T_{k}}(\rho)\|_{L^{\gamma+1}(\tilde{\Omega}\times(0,T)\times\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})}\leq C
which would establish the result (
181
), we observe that by the convergence of
T_{k}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})\to\overline{T_{k}(\rho)}
in
C_{w}(0,T;L^{p}(\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}))

\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost surely for
p\in[1,\infty)
, we have that
T_{k}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})-T_{k}(\rho)\rightharpoonup\overline{T_{k}(\rho)}-T_{k}(\rho)
weakly in
L^{\gamma+1}((0,T)\times\mathcal{O}_{\alpha})

\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-almost surely. So by weak lower semicontinuity of norms and Lemma
8.10
, we have
\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}}|\overline{T_{k}(\rho)}-T_{k}(\rho)|^{\gamma+1}dxdt\leq C
, which hence establishes (
181
). This proves (
179
) and thus ultimately proves (
169
).

8.8.
Strong convergence of density

The aim of this section is to prove that

(182)

\displaystyle\bar{p}=\rho^{\gamma}\quad\text{ a.e. on }\tilde{\Omega}\times(0,T)\times{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha},

by appealing to the monotonicity of the pressure. This procedure is standard and follows closely the steps introduced in
[
20
,
8
]
. For the sake of completion, we will briefly outline the steps involved in establishing (
182
) and, for details we will refer the reader to
[
20
]
wherever necessary. We begin by defining,

(183)

\displaystyle L_{k}(z)=\begin{cases}z\ln{z},\quad 0\leq z<k\\
z\ln{z}+z\int_{k}^{z}T_{k}(s)/s^{2}ds,\quad z\geq k,\end{cases}

where
T_{k}
is defined in (
170
).
Now, we choose
b=L_{k}
in (
142
) and (
169
), then we take the difference of the resulting equations and set
\phi\equiv 1
. This yields,

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}}(L_{k}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})-L_{k}(\rho))(t)\leq\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}}(L_{k}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})-L_{k}(\rho))(0)+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}}(T_{k}(\rho)\nabla\cdot{\boldsymbol{u}}-T_{k}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})\nabla\cdot\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{\delta}).

Hence, upon letting
k\to\infty
in the equation above and using Lemma
8.9
and monotonicity of pressure, we obtain for any
p<\gamma
that also uses vanishing outside for
\delta>0

\displaystyle{{\limsup_{\delta\to 0}}}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}}({L_{k}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})}-L_{k}(\rho))(t)\leq\limsup_{\delta\to 0}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}}(T_{k}(\rho)\nabla\cdot{\boldsymbol{u}}-T_{k}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})\nabla\cdot\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{\delta})

\displaystyle{{=\limsup_{\delta\to 0}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}}(T_{k}(\rho){{\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\eta^{*}}}}}\nabla\cdot{\boldsymbol{u}}-T_{k}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta})\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\hat{\eta}_{\delta}^{*}}}\nabla\cdot\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{\delta})}}

\displaystyle{{\leq\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}}(T_{k}(\rho)-\overline{T_{k}(\rho)}){{\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\eta^{*}}}}}\nabla\cdot{\boldsymbol{u}}}}

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{\mathbb{E}}(\|\nabla\cdot{\boldsymbol{u}}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{\eta^{*}}))}\|T_{k}(\rho)-\overline{T_{k}(\rho)}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))})

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{\mathbb{E}}(\|\nabla\cdot{\boldsymbol{u}}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{\eta^{*}}))}\|T_{k}(\rho)-\overline{T_{k}(\rho)}\|_{L^{p}((0,T)\times{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha})}^{\frac{\gamma-1}{\gamma+1-p}}\|T_{k}(\rho)-\overline{T_{k}(\rho)}\|_{L^{\gamma+1}((0,T)\times{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha})}^{(\frac{2-p}{\gamma+1-p})}).

Then the bounds derived in Lemma
8.10
and the convergence result (
178
) implies that,

(184)

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}\times(0,T)}\hat{\rho}_{\delta}\ln\hat{\rho}_{\delta}\to\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}\times(0,T)}\rho\ln\rho.

Using the convexity of
\rho\mapsto\rho\ln\rho
then gives us the desired strong convergence result for the density,

(185)

\displaystyle\hat{\rho}_{\delta}\to\rho,\quad\text{ in }\quad L^{1}(\tilde{\Omega};L^{1}(0,T;L^{1}({\mathcal{O}}_{\alpha}))).

This completes the proof of (
182
). This strong convergence (
185
) allows us to explicitly identify the term
\overline{\boldsymbol{F}(\rho,\rho\boldsymbol{u})}
in the limiting stochastic integral, see Lemma
8.7
, initially given as the weak limit via (
101
) of
\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\hat{\eta}^{*}_{\delta}}}\boldsymbol{F}(\hat{\rho}_{\delta},\hat{\rho}_{\delta}\hat{{\boldsymbol{u}}}_{\delta})
, as
\boldsymbol{F}(\rho,\rho\boldsymbol{u})
.

Using (
182
) in the limit of (
168
) we come to the conclusion that
for every
(\hat{\mathcal{F}}_{t})_{t\geq 0}-
adapted, essentially bounded smooth process
({\boldsymbol{q}},\psi)
such that

{\boldsymbol{q}}|_{\Gamma_{\eta^{*}}}=\psi\boldsymbol{e}_{z}
,
{\mathbb{P}}
-almost surely, the following equation holds for
{\mathbb{P}}
-almost surely, for almost every
t\in[0,\tau^{\eta}]
:

(186)

\begin{split}&{\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\eta^{*}}(t)}\rho(t){\boldsymbol{u}}(t){\boldsymbol{q}}(t)d{\bf x}+\int_{\Gamma}\partial_{t}\eta(t)\psi(t)dz}=\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\eta_{0}}}\boldsymbol{p}_{0}{\boldsymbol{q}}(0)d{\bf x}+\int_{\Gamma}v_{0}\psi(0)d\boldsymbol{z}\\
&+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\eta^{*}}(t)}\rho{\boldsymbol{u}}\cdot\partial_{t}{\boldsymbol{q}}d{\bf x}dt+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\eta^{*}}(t)}\rho{\boldsymbol{u}}\otimes{\boldsymbol{u}}:\nabla{\boldsymbol{q}}d{\bf x}dt-2\mu\int_{0}^{t}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\eta^{*}}(t)}\nabla{\boldsymbol{u}}\cdot\nabla{\boldsymbol{q}}d{\bf x}dt\\
&+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\eta^{*}}(t)}\rho^{\gamma}(\nabla\cdot{\boldsymbol{q}})d{\bf x}dt-\lambda\int_{0}^{t}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\eta^{*}}(t)}\text{div}({\boldsymbol{u}})\text{div}({\boldsymbol{q}})d{\bf x}dt\\
&+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Gamma}\partial_{t}\eta\partial_{t}\psi d\boldsymbol{z}dt-{\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Gamma}\nabla\partial_{t}\eta\cdot\nabla\psi d\boldsymbol{z}dt-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Gamma}(\nabla\eta\nabla\psi+\Delta\eta\Delta\psi)d\boldsymbol{z}dt}\\
&+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\eta^{*}}(t)}\boldsymbol{F}(\rho,\rho{\boldsymbol{u}})\cdot{\boldsymbol{q}}\,d\hat{W}^{1}(t)+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Gamma}G(\eta,\partial_{t}\eta)\psi d\hat{W}^{2}(t).\end{split}

Moreover,
{\boldsymbol{u}}|_{\Gamma_{\eta^{*}}}=v\boldsymbol{e}_{z}.

Observe that in this formulation some terms are defined on fluid domains corresponding to the artificial structure displacement
\eta^{*}
whereas the others are given in terms of
\eta
. To resolve this discrepancy (cf. (
11
)) we appeal to the fact that these two structural displacements are equal (see (
105
)) until the almost surely positive stopping time
\tau^{\eta}
(see (
107
)) defined in (
106
). This leads us to our final conclusion stated below.

Conclusion:
We conclude that the stochastic basis
(\tilde{\Omega},\tilde{\mathcal{F}},(\tilde{\mathcal{F}}_{t})_{t\geq 0},\tilde{\mathbb{P}},\hat{W}^{1},\hat{W}^{2})
and the random variables
({\boldsymbol{u}},\rho,\eta)
constructed in Theorem
8.1
and the stopping time
\tau^{\eta}
defined in (
106
) determine a martingale solution to the FSI problem in the sense of Definition
3.1
.

Appendix A: Equivalence of laws for stopped processes

Recall that in the proof of the main existence result, we constructed approximate solutions consisting of an approximate structure displacement
\eta_{N}
and a stopped structure displacement
\eta_{N}^{*}
, which is stopped at the first instance
\tau^{\eta}_{N}
, if it exists, at which
\eta_{N}
leaves desired bounds depending on
\alpha
:

\tau^{\eta}_{N}:=T\wedge\inf\left\{t>0:\inf_{\Gamma}(1+\eta_{N}(t))\leq\alpha\text{ or }\|\eta_{N}(t)\|_{H^{s}(\Gamma)}\geq\frac{1}{\alpha}\right\}.

We then used the Skorohod representation theorem to transfer these approximate solutions to a different probability space, but we want to justify that the new random variables
(\overline{\eta},\overline{\eta}^{*})
on the new probability space also have the property that they agree up until the time
\tau^{\eta}_{N}
at which
\tilde{\eta}
leaves the desired deterministic
\alpha
bounds:

\tau^{\eta}_{N}:=T\wedge\inf\left\{t>0:\inf_{\Gamma}(1+\overline{\eta}_{N}(t))\leq\alpha\text{ or }\|\overline{\eta}_{N}(t)\|_{H^{s}(\Gamma)}\leq\frac{1}{\alpha}\right\}.

The Skorohod representation theorem gives us that there is equivalence of laws, so to use equivalence of laws to establish this, we must show that the set of functions
(\eta_{1},\eta_{2})
, where
\eta_{2}
is equal to
\eta_{1}
stopped at the first time of leaving the desired
\alpha
bounds, is a measurable set of the phase space
C(0,T;H^{s}(\Gamma))
for a fixed
3/2<s<2
, after which we can use equivalence of laws to conclude.

To illustrate the main idea behind the measurability of this set of ordered pairs of continuous processes with their stopped processes, we consider an analogue of this situation in the simpler case of continuous real-valued functions in
C(0,T;{\mathbb{R}})
. Consider the phase space
C(0,T;{\mathbb{R}})\times C(0,T;{\mathbb{R}})
and for a given function
f
and a given positive number
R>0
, define

\tau_{R}=\inf\{t\in[0,T]:f(t)\geq R\},

and define the stopped process

f^{*}(t)=f(t\wedge\tau_{R}),\quad\text{ for }t\in[0,T],

where in using the star notation, we do not notate the explicit dependence on
R
, even though it is there implicitly. Define the set
B_{R}
to be the set

(187)

B_{R}:=\{(f,g)\in C(0,T;{\mathbb{R}})^{2}:g=f^{*}\},

or more informally, the set of all
(f,f^{*})
as
f
traverses through
C(0,T;{\mathbb{R}})
. We claim the following result about
B_{R}
:

Proposition 8.8
.

For each
R>0
,
B_{R}
is a measurable subset of
C(0,T;{\mathbb{R}})^{2}
.

Proof.

The proof will use a time discretization argument, where the main idea will be to associate to each continuous function in
C(0,T;{\mathbb{R}})
and each time discretization parameter a finite sequence of real numbers, where we can more easily impose the “stopped” process condition that
g=f^{*}
for
(f,g)\in B_{R}
for the finite sequences, and where we can impose this condition for infintely many (but countably many) time discretization parameters, to show that
B_{R}
is a measurable set.

Let
N
be the number of subintervals, let
\Delta t=T/N
, and for
n=0,1,...,N
, let
t_{n}=n\Delta t
. Consider the following continuous map
F_{N}:C(0,T;{\mathbb{R}})\times C(0,T;{\mathbb{R}})\to{\mathbb{R}}^{N}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{N}
:

(188)

F_{N}:(f,g)\to\\
\left(\max_{t\in[0,\Delta t]}f(t),\max_{t\in[\Delta t,2\Delta t]}f(t),...,\max_{t\in[(N-1)\Delta t,T]}f(t),\max_{t\in[0,\Delta t]}g(t),\max_{t\in[\Delta t,2\Delta t]}g(t),...,\max_{t\in[(N-1)\Delta t,T]}g(t)\right).

For each
N
, consider the following measurable subset
E_{N}
of
{\mathbb{R}}^{N}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{N}
, defined as the union of
E_{N,m}
for
m=1,2,...,N+1
, where

E_{N,N+1}=\{(a_{1},a_{2},...,a_{N},b_{1},b_{2},...,b_{N})\in{\mathbb{R}}^{N}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{N}:a_{i}=b_{i}\text{ and }b_{i}<R,\text{ for all }1\leq i\leq N\},

and for
1\leq m\leq N
,

E_{N,m}=\{(a_{1},a_{2},...,a_{N},b_{1},b_{2},...,b_{N})\in{\mathbb{R}}^{N}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{N}:a_{i}=b_{i}<R\text{ for }1\leq i<m,\ b_{i}=R\text{ for all }m\leq i\leq N\}.

Then, let
\displaystyle E_{N}=\bigcup_{m=1}^{N+1}E_{N,m}
. Note that for
B_{R}
defined in (
187
) and for the continuous map
F_{N}:C(0,T;{\mathbb{R}})\times C(0,T;{\mathbb{R}})\to{\mathbb{R}}^{N}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{N}
defined in (
188
), we have that

(189)

B_{R}=\bigcap_{N=1}^{\infty}F_{N}^{-1}(E_{N}),

where each
F_{N}^{-1}(E_{N})
is a measurable subset of
C(0,T;{\mathbb{R}})\times C(0,T;{\mathbb{R}})
since
E_{N}
is measurable in
{\mathbb{R}}^{N}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{N}
and by the continuity of
F_{N}
. This shows that
B_{R}
is measurable in
C(0,T;{\mathbb{R}})\times C(0,T;{\mathbb{R}})
.

It suffices to show that (
189
) holds. By the definition of
B_{R}
, it is easy to show immediately that
\displaystyle B_{R}\subset\bigcap_{N=1}^{\infty}F_{N}^{-1}(E_{N})
. To show the opposite inclusion, it suffices to show that if
(f,g)\notin B_{R}
, then
(f,g)\notin F_{N}^{-1}(E_{N})
for some
N
. If
(f,g)\notin B_{R}
, there are two possibilities:

•

g
cannot be written as a stopped process
g=h^{*}
for
h\in C(0,T;{\mathbb{R}})
. This can only happen if there exists
s,t\in[0,T]
with
s<t
such that
g(s)=R
and
g(t)\neq R
. Thus, taking
N
to be sufficiently large, we would see that
(f,g)\notin E_{N,m}
for any
m
.

•

g
can be written as
g=h^{*}
for some
h
, but if
t_{0}
is smallest time for which
g(t_{0})=R
(or
t_{0}=T
otherwise if such a time does not exist), there exists some
0\leq t<t_{0}
such that
f(t)\neq g(t)
, where
g(t)<R
. In this case, using continuity of
f
and
g
,
(f,g)\notin E_{N,m}
for any
m
for some
N
sufficiently large.

This verifies (
189
) and concludes the proof of the claim that
B_{R}
is measurable.

∎

Appendix B: Extension by 0

Theorem 8.2
.

Assume that
\eta:\Gamma:=\mathbb{T}^{2}\to\mathbb{R}
is an
\alpha
-Hölder continuous function such that
\sup_{\Gamma}|\eta|\leq L
. Let
{\mathcal{O}}_{\eta}
be the subgraph defined as
{\mathcal{O}}_{\eta}:=\{(x,y,z):0<z<\eta(x,y),(x,y)\in\Gamma\}
. Now, for any
{\boldsymbol{u}}\in H^{s}({\mathcal{O}}_{\eta})
define its extension by 0 as:

\displaystyle\tilde{\boldsymbol{u}}

\displaystyle={\boldsymbol{u}}\qquad\text{in }{\mathcal{O}}_{\eta},

\displaystyle\tilde{\boldsymbol{u}}

\displaystyle=0\qquad\text{in }\mathbb{R}^{3}\setminus{\mathcal{O}}_{\eta}.

Then

(190)

\displaystyle\|\tilde{\boldsymbol{u}}\|_{H^{s\alpha}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\leq C_{s,L,\alpha}\|{\boldsymbol{u}}\|_{H^{s}({\mathcal{O}}_{\eta})},\qquad\forall s<\frac{\alpha}{2}.

Proof.

For any open set
{\mathcal{O}}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{3}
we let
\mathcal{D}({\mathcal{O}})
be the set of all smooth functions with compact support in
{\mathcal{O}}
.
First we will prove that for
s<\frac{1}{2}
and any
{\boldsymbol{u}}\in\mathcal{D}({\mathcal{O}}_{\eta})
,

(191)

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\eta}}\text{dist}(x,\partial{\mathcal{O}}_{\eta})^{-2s\alpha}|{\boldsymbol{u}}(x)|^{2}\leq C_{s,L,\alpha}\|{\boldsymbol{u}}\|^{2}_{H^{s}({\mathcal{O}}_{\eta})}.

To prove this we consider any
y=(y^{\prime},y_{3})\in\partial{\mathcal{O}}_{\eta}
and use the triangle inequality to obtain for any
x=(x^{\prime},x_{3})\in{\mathcal{O}}_{\eta}
that

\displaystyle|\eta(x^{\prime})-x_{3}|

\displaystyle=|\eta(x^{\prime})-\eta(y^{\prime})+y_{3}-x_{3}|\leq C_{\alpha}|x^{\prime}-y^{\prime}|^{\alpha}+|x_{3}-y_{3}|

\displaystyle\leq(1+C_{\alpha})(|x-y|^{\alpha}+|x-y|).

This implies, for any
x=(x^{\prime},x_{3})\in{\mathcal{O}}_{\eta}
, that

(192)

\displaystyle\text{dist}(x,\partial{\mathcal{O}}_{\eta})^{\alpha}\geq\frac{|\eta(x^{\prime})-x_{3}|}{(1+C_{\alpha})(1+(\text{dist}(x,\partial{\mathcal{O}}_{\eta}))^{1-\alpha})}\geq\frac{1}{(1+L^{1-\alpha})(1+C_{\alpha})}|\eta(x^{\prime})-x_{3}|.

Applying Lemma 3.31 of
[
35
]
we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\eta}}\text{dist}(x,\partial{\mathcal{O}}_{\eta})^{-2s\alpha}|{\boldsymbol{u}}(x)|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{(1+L^{1-\alpha})(1+C_{\alpha})}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\eta}}|\eta(x^{\prime})-x_{3}|^{-2s}|{\boldsymbol{u}}(x)|^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{C}{(1+L^{1-\alpha})(1+C_{\alpha})}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{-2s}|{\boldsymbol{u}}(x^{\prime},\eta(x^{\prime})-t)|^{2}dtdx^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{s}}{(1+L^{1-\alpha})(1+C_{\alpha})}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\int_{y<\eta(x^{\prime})}\int_{z<\eta(x^{\prime})}\frac{|{\boldsymbol{u}}(x^{\prime},y)-{\boldsymbol{u}}(x^{\prime},z)|^{2}}{|x-y|^{1+2s}}dydzdx^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq C_{s,L,\alpha}\|{\boldsymbol{u}}\|_{H^{s}({\mathcal{O}}_{\eta})}

The second to last inequality above follows using identity (1.4.4.9) in
[
23
]
and the calculations that follow this equation on page 30 (see also Theorem 3.31 in
[
35
]
).
This proves (
191
).

Next, by elementary calculations we have (see e.g. Lemma 1.3.2.6 in
[
23
]
or Theorem 3.33 in
[
35
]
):

\displaystyle\|\tilde{\boldsymbol{u}}\|_{H^{s}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}=\left(\|{\boldsymbol{u}}\|^{2}_{H^{s}({\mathcal{O}}_{\eta})}+\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\eta}}|{\boldsymbol{u}}(x)|^{2}w_{s}(x)dx\right)^{\frac{1}{2}},

where the weight
\displaystyle w_{s}(x)=2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\setminus{\mathcal{O}}_{\eta}}\frac{1}{|x-y|^{3+2s}}dy
satisfies

\displaystyle{|w_{s}(x)|\leq C\text{dist}(x,\partial{\mathcal{O}}_{\eta})^{-2s}}.

Hence, thanks to (
191
), for any
{\boldsymbol{u}}\in\mathcal{D}({\mathcal{O}}_{\eta})
we have

\displaystyle\|\tilde{\boldsymbol{u}}\|_{H^{s\alpha}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\leq C_{s,L,\alpha}\|{\boldsymbol{u}}\|_{H^{s}({\mathcal{O}}_{\eta})}.

Hence, (
190
) will follow using the following two density results:

•

For any
{\mathcal{O}}
with
C^{0}
boundary, Theorem 1.4.2.2 in
[
23
]
, states that

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}({\mathcal{O}})\text{ is dense in }\tilde{H}^{s}({\mathcal{O}}):=\{{\boldsymbol{u}}\in H^{s}({\mathcal{O}}):\tilde{\boldsymbol{u}}\in H^{s}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\},\quad\forall s>0,

endowed with the norm
\|{\boldsymbol{u}}\|_{\tilde{H}^{s}({\mathcal{O}})}=\|\tilde{\boldsymbol{u}}\|_{H^{s}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}
.

•

Moreover, if
{\mathcal{O}}
is a
C^{0,\alpha}
domain, then thanks to Corollary 3.29 (viii) in
[
14
]
, we have that

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}({\mathcal{O}})\text{ is dense in }H^{s}({\mathcal{O}})\quad\forall 0<s<\frac{\alpha}{2}.

∎
